In the Satellite, flashing a **Mirror Force** (Super Rare) gets you stabbed. Flashing a **Marshmallon** (Rare) gets your bike stolen.

To survive the gangs and Sector Security, your deck needs to look like **absolute garbage**. It needs to look like a pile of mismatched, bent, water-damaged cards that you found in a gutter.

Here is your **"Trash-Tier" Bike Burn Deck**.
**Rarity:** 100% Common.
**Visuals:** No sleeves. Scratched edges. Coffee stains.
**Strategy:** Survive until the Bike kills them.

### The Deck List: "Scrap Heap Survival"
**Total Cards:** 40
**Monsters:** 4
**Spells:** 0 (Because Speed Spells are hard to find/expensive)
**Traps:** 36

#### Monsters (4) - *The "Meat Shields"*
These are Common cards found in Starter Decks. Nobody wants them.
*   **3x Speed Warrior:** (Common).
*   *Why:* It’s the "Rat" of 5D's. During the turn you summon it, its ATK doubles to 1800 (not that you care). Crucially, it’s a distraction.
*   **1x Zero Gardna:** (Common).
*   *Effect:* You can Tribute this card to make all Battle Damage 0 for the turn.
*   *Why:* It clears your own field so you can activate *Secret Barrel* / *Dimension Wall* without obstruction.

#### The Burn Engine (Common Traps)
*   **3x Just Desserts:** (Common - Starter Deck Yugi).
*   *Effect:* 500 dmg per opponent's monster.
*   *Context:* Security officers spam Goyo Guardian and Gate Blockers. This card hits for 1500+ easily.
*   **3x Secret Barrel:** (Common).
*   *Effect:* 200 dmg per card in their hand/field.
*   **3x Ojama Trio:** (Common).
*   *Effect:* Give them 3 useless Tokens.
*   *Why:* It clogs their zones so they can't Synchro Summon, AND it guarantees *Just Desserts* does 1500 dmg.

#### The Defense (Common Traps)
*   **3x Waboku:** (Common).
*   *Effect:* You take no battle damage, monsters aren't destroyed.
*   *Appearance:* This card has been printed a million times. You probably found one used as a bookmark in a trash can.
*   **3x Threatening Roar:** (Common).
*   *Effect:* Opponent cannot declare attacks.
*   **3x Dimension Wall:** (Common).
*   *Effect:* Instead of *Magic Cylinder* (which is Rare/Holo), you use this. You take no battle damage, and the opponent takes it instead.
*   *Why it's better:* It doesn't "Target" the monster, so it bypasses some protections.

#### The Draw Power (Common)
*   **3x Jar of Greed:** (Common).
*   *Effect:* Draw 1 card.
*   *Why:* It thins your deck. It’s chainable.
*   **3x Legacy of Yata-Garasu:** (Common).
*   *Effect:* Draw 1 card.
*   **3x Accumulated Fortune:** (Common).
*   *Effect:* If Chain Link is 4 or higher, Draw 2 cards.
*   *The Combo:* Opponent attacks -> Chain 1 *Roar* -> Chain 2 *Jar* -> Chain 3 *Secret Barrel* -> Chain 4 *Accumulated Fortune*.

---

### How this Deck Wins (The "Bike Burn" Loop)
You aren't winning with the cards. You are winning with the **Mechanic**.

**The Scene:** You are racing a Security Officer on your rusty, smoking bike.

1.  **Turn 1-3:** You do **NOTHING**. You just drive.
*   Every turn, you gain a **Speed Counter**.
*   You Set 3 Traps and pass.
2.  **The Attack:** The Officer attacks.
*   You flip *Waboku*. You take 0 damage. Your bike stabilizes.
3.  **Turn 4 (4 Speed Counters):**
*   You don't play a card.
*   You activate **Speed World 2** (The Field Mechanic).
*   **Effect:** Remove 4 Speed Counters. **Inflict 800 Damage.**
4.  **Repeat:**
*   You keep driving. You keep flipping *Jar of Greed* to find more *Wabokus*.
*   Every 4 turns, you shoot them for 800.
*   If they summon a lot of monsters, you flip *Just Desserts*.

### Why this is safe for you
*   **No Value:** If a gang jumps you and looks at your deck, they will see *Jar of Greed* and *Speed Warrior*. They will throw it back in your face. "This is trash! Where are the Tuners?"
*   **No Suspicion:** You don't look like a Pro. You look like a desperate survivor trying not to die.
*   **High Win Rate:** Despite being commons, **Chain Burn** destroys Synchro decks because Synchro decks rely on attacking. You don't let them attack.

You have the perfect **"Gray Man"** deck. You are invisible, worthless to robbers, and lethal in a race.
Now, start the engine. The pipeline opens in 3 days.What about my "Ground Burn" deck? (the one I use to fight back against gangs (or join them? should I join a gang?) or hustle someone and eventually ambush a cop?)This is the pivot point where you decide if you want to be a **Warlord** or a **Ghost**.

In the Satellite, your "Ground Burn" deck is your shiv. It’s ugly, it’s cheap, and it kills people before they realize they’re bleeding.

Here is the breakdown of your **Common-Only "Ground Burn"** deck and the strategic choice between Gang Life and the Solo Hustle.

### 1. The Deck: "Trash Chain Burn" (100% Common)
You cannot be caught with a Rare card. If a gang sees a *Marshmallon*, they will beat you until you drop it. Your deck must look like a pile of garbage that you scooped up from a puddle.

**The Philosophy:**
In *GX*, you stalled with *Gravity Bind* (Continuous).
In *5D's*, Synchro monsters like *Mist Wurm* or *Brionac* bounce/destroy cards easily.
**Chain Burn** uses "Chainable" Traps. If they try to destroy your card, you just activate it in response. It never dies without getting value.

**The List (40 Cards - All Common/Damaged):**

*   **Monsters (4):**
*   **3x Speed Warrior:** (The Satellite Mascot. It’s useless after Turn 1, but people ignore it. It’s your wall).
*   **1x Morphing Jar #2:** (If you can find one beaten up. Flips to shuffle monsters. Resets the field).

*   **Spells (0):**
*   Spells are for rich City kids. You don't have them.

*   **Traps (36) - The Killers:**
*   **3x Just Desserts:** (Deals 500 per monster. When they swarm 3 Synchro materials, this is 1500 damage).
*   **3x Secret Barrel:** (Deals 200 per card in hand/field. City kids love drawing cards. This punishes them).
*   **3x Ojama Trio:** (Clutters their field with 3 Tokens. They can't Synchro Summon. *Just Desserts* hits for 1500 minimum).
*   **3x Chain Strike:** (Common in some sets. Deals 400 x Chain Link Count. If you chain 4 cards, this does 1600-2000 damage).
*   **3x Accumulated Fortune:** (Draw 2 cards if Chain Link is 4+).

*   **The Defense:**
*   **3x Waboku:** (You take 0 damage. Your monsters survive).
*   **3x Threatening Roar:** (They skip Battle Phase).
*   **3x Dimension Wall:** (Better than Cylinder. You take 0, they take the damage. It doesn't target).

**How it plays:**
Opponent summons **Junk Warrior** (2300 ATK).
1.  **Attack Declaration.**
2.  **Chain 1:** *Threatening Roar* (Stop attack).
3.  **Chain 2:** *Just Desserts* (Burn).
4.  **Chain 3:** *Secret Barrel* (Burn).
5.  **Chain 4:** *Accumulated Fortune* (Draw 2).
**Result:** You took 0 damage, dealt ~2000 damage, and refilled your hand. You are invisible death.

---

### 2. The Choice: Gang vs. Solo

In Satellite, you have two career paths.

#### **Option A: Join a Duel Gang (The "Enforcers" Route)**
*   **Pros:**
*   **Protection:** Nobody jumps a member of the "Scrap Iron Gang."
*   **Resources:** Gangs hoard chips and cards. You might get access to a *Heavy Storm* or *Torrential Tribute*.
*   **The Bike:** Gangs often share parts. You might get your D-Wheel faster.
*   **Cons:**
*   **Turf Wars:** You are forced to duel rival gangs daily.
*   **The "Kalin" Problem:** Gang leaders (like Kalin Kessler) are psychotic. If you mess up, they might beat you up themselves.
*   **Sector Security Target:** The cops focus on gangs. You are 100% getting marked if you run with a crew.

#### **Option B: The Solo Hustler (The "Yusei" Route)**
*   **Pros:**
*   **The "Gray Man":** You are invisible. You look like a bum. Cops ignore bums.
*   **Freedom:** You keep what you kill. No kicking up a percentage to a boss.
*   **Cons:**
*   **Vulnerability:** If three guys corner you in an alley, you have to duel 1v3 or run.
*   **Slow Grind:** You have to find every screw and bolt yourself.

**My Advice:**
**Stay Solo, but be a Mercenary.**
Don't wear a gang jacket. Instead, become the guy gangs hire to take out a rival.
*   *Gang Leader:* "Hey, Trash Man. That guy in the North District keeps beating my men with a Synchro Deck. Take him out."
*   *You:* "I want a pristine CPU Chip."
*   *Result:* You burn the rival, take the chip, and disappear. You are a ghost.

---

### 3. The Hustle: How to Ambush a Cop
Eventually, you need the **CPU Chip**. You can't find this in the trash. It's only in **Sector Security Duel Runners**.
You have to hunt a predator.

**The Target:**
Officer Trudge (or a low-level patrolman).
*   **The Bait:** You stand in a restricted area (like an old subway entrance). You hold a "Rare" card (maybe a holographic wrapper that *looks* rare).
*   **The Hook:** He chases you. He corners you.
*   *Trudge:* "Got you, scum! Hand over the contraband!"
*   *You:* "I challenge you. If I win, you let me go." (You don't mention you're taking his chip).
*   *Trudge:* "Ha! A ground duel? Against my **Gate Blockers**? Fine. I'll crush you."

**The Duel:**
Trudge plays an "Anti-Meta" Police Deck (*Gate Blocker*, *Montage Dragon*, *Goyo Guardian*).
*   **His mistake:** He expects you to play Monsters.
*   **Your reality:** You Set 5 cards and pass.
*   **The Kill:** He summons *Goyo Guardian* (2800). He attacks.
*   You flip **Dimension Wall**.
*   He takes 2800 damage to his face.
*   You flip **Secret Barrel**. Game over.

**The Loot:**
He is unconscious (hologram impact is real in 5D's).
You pull out a screwdriver. You pop the panel on his D-Wheel.
You rip out the **T-66 Navigation Chip**.
You run before backup arrives.

**Status:**
*   **Deck:** Chain Burn (Commons).
*   **Inventory:** 1x Stolen Police CPU.
*   **Wanted Level:** 3 Stars.

You are one step closer to the pipeline. Keep your head down.Alright, so I have a "trash chain burn" and "bike burn" that are filled with common/uncommon physically torn/wet/abused cards that are taped together. Now I escaped to the underground warehouse district. Does my standard of living increases here? Also, at this point, am I more likely to get marked as a criminal?This is a massive shift in genre. You have moved from a **Post-Apocalyptic Survival** setting (Satellite) to a **Cyberpunk Crime Noir** setting (The Underground).

Here is your status report on life in the New Domino City Warehouse District.

### 1. Living Standards: From "Hell" to "Purgatory"
**Does it increase?** **Yes, significantly.**

*   **Shelter:** In Satellite, you slept in a pipe to avoid toxic rain. In the Underground, you rent a "Coffin Hotel" capsule or squat in a dry, abandoned warehouse. You have electricity. You have a roof that doesn't leak.
*   **Food:** In Satellite, you ate nutrient blocks. Here, you can buy **Cup Ramen** and **Canned Coffee** from vending machines using the DP you win. It tastes like heaven compared to garbage.
*   **Hygiene:** There are public bathhouses. You can finally wash the radioactive dust off your skin.

**The Cost:**
Everything costs money. In Satellite, you bartered scrap metal. Here, you need **Yen/DP**. If you don't win duels, you don't eat.

### 2. The Criminal Mark: "The Marker"
**Are you marked?**
Technically, **not yet.**
*   The **Marker** (that yellow tattoo on the face seen on Yusei) is applied **after you are arrested and processed** at the Facility.
*   Since you escaped the pipeline without being caught, your face is clean.

**However, you are at Extreme Risk:**
*   **The ID Signal:** Every legal D-Wheel broadcasts a signal. Yours is built from a stolen Police CPU. If you didn't hack it perfectly, it might ping a tower.
*   **The Cameras:** New Domino City is a surveillance state. There are cameras everywhere.
*   **The "Look":** You look like a Satellite refugee. Your clothes are ragged, you are too skinny, and you smell like ozone.
*   *City Police:* They profile people. If a cop sees you, they will ask for ID. You don't have one.
*   *The Result:* If they catch you, they **will** Mark you and send you to prison.

**Your Strategy:**
Keep your helmet on. **Always.** Even when you aren't riding. It hides your face and makes you look like a generic "Riding Duelist."

### 3. The Social Stigma: "Why are your cards wet?"
This is your biggest problem in the City.
In Satellite, everyone played with trash. In the City, duelists use **Mint Condition** cards in shiny sleeves.

**The Underground Duel:**
You enter a warehouse gambling match.
*   **Opponent:** A low-level gangster with a pristine "Blackwing" or "X-Saber" deck.
*   **You:** You shuffle your deck. It makes a crunchy sound because of the dried mud. Some cards are held together with scotch tape.
*   **Reaction:**
*   *Opponent:* "Gross. Don't touch my deck with those hands. Are those cards... moldy?"
*   *Spectators:* "Look at that trash. He's definitely a Satellite Rat."

**The Gameplay Advantage:**
They underestimate you.
*   They see a taped-up *Speed Warrior* and assume you are a beggar.
*   Then you chain 4 common Traps and burn them for 3000 damage.
*   **The Hustle:** You play into the "Trash" persona. "Oh, please sir, I just want enough money for soup." Then you rob them blind.

### 4. The New Objective: "Laundering"
You cannot walk into a Card Shop yet. You are too suspicious.
You need to **Launder your Deck**.

**The Priority Purchase:**
Your first paycheck shouldn't go to food. It should go to **Card Sleeves**.
*   **Why:** Opaque (black) sleeves hide the fact that the back of your cards are peeled and water-damaged.
*   **The Result:** Your deck looks uniform. You look like a professional, eccentric duelist rather than a homeless person.

### 5. Summary of Underground Life
*   **Job:** Illegal Riding Duels (Betting matches).
*   **Deck:** "Bike Burn" (Still trash commons, now in Black Sleeves).
*   **Status:** Fugitive (Unmarked).
*   **Living:** Sleeping in your D-Wheel in a garage. Eating Ramen.
*   **Threat Level:** High. If you beat the wrong Gangster, they won't pay you—they'll call the cops on the "Satellite Scum."

**Advice:**
Find **"The Bootlegger"** (a character archetype like Saiga/Blister). You need a fake ID signal for your bike, or Sector Security will track your stolen CPU within the week.Lets say I hustled my way through the card sleeve + clothes + shower so I look like just a normal duelist. Also I got my fake ID signal. Now what? do I upgrade my bike or my deck first?This is the critical decision that separates a **Survivor** from a **Professional**.

The answer is **Upgrade the Bike first.**
In the 5D's era, your D-Wheel is not just a vehicle; it is your **Life Points** and your **Win Condition**.

Here is the tactical breakdown of why you spend your first paycheck on an Engine, not a *Lava Golem*.

### 1. The "Speed World" Math (Why the Bike Matters)
You are playing a **"Bike Burn"** deck. Your main source of damage is removing Speed Counters to deal 800 damage.

*   **Trash Bike:**
*   **Acceleration:** Poor. You might struggle to gain a counter every turn if you take a corner too wide.
*   **Stability:** **Zero.** When you take damage in a Riding Duel, you lose Speed Counters. On a trash bike, a simple attack might knock you from 6 Counters down to 2.
*   **Result:** You effectively "jam your gun." You can't shoot the 800 damage because you can't hold the counters.

*   **Upgraded Bike:**
*   **Shielding/Frame:** Reduces the counters lost when you take damage.
*   **Acceleration:** Guarantees you gain a counter every Standby Phase.
*   **Result:** You reload faster. You fire more often. **The Bike IS the Deck.**

### 2. The Camouflage Factor
You have a Fake ID signal, but you are riding a pile of rusted scrap metal.
*   **The visual mismatch:** If a cop sees a "Legal" signal coming from a vehicle that looks like it fell out of a garbage truck, they will pull you over for a "Safety Inspection."
*   **The Goal:** You need your bike to look **Generic**. You want the "Honda Civic" of D-Wheels. Boring, functional, and blends into traffic.
*   *Purchase:* **Cowling/Body Kit.** Cover the rusty pipes with sleek plastic. Paint it a standard color (Dark Blue or Black).

### 3. The "Ground Deck" is already perfect
You might be tempted to buy *Marshmallon* or *Lava Golem*, but remember the Satellite lessons:
*   **Commons are Killers:** Your *Just Desserts* and *Chain Strike* kill Pro League players just as dead as they kill gang members.
*   **The "Sleeper" Effect:** In the Underground, if you play a shiny Super Rare, opponents will try to steal it or target you. If you beat them with 40 Common cards, they just think they got unlucky. **Keep the deck trash-tier rarity for now.**

---

### The Shopping List (The Mechanic's Garage)
Go to the black market mechanic (The "Bootlegger"). Here is your upgrade path:

**Priority 1: The "Stabilizer" (Frame)**
*   **Cost:** High.
*   **Effect:** Reduces shock from attacks.
*   **Gameplay:** Instead of losing 2 Speed Counters when hit, you only lose 1. This is critical for maintaining your ammo.

**Priority 2: The "Overclock" (CPU)**
*   **Cost:** Medium.
*   **Effect:** Faster calculation.
*   **Gameplay:** Allows you to activate complex Trap Chains without the bike's autopilot lagging or erroring out.

**Priority 3: Speed Spells (The Deck Upgrade)**
*   *Only after the bike is fixed.*
*   Start buying **Speed Spell - Count Up** (Gain 2 Counters) and **Speed Spell - Gap Storm** (Destroy Spells/Traps).
*   *Note:* You can't use these if your bike is too slow to cast them.

### Summary
**Fix the Gun (The Bike) before you buy expensive Bullets (The Cards).**
Once your D-Wheel is stable, fast, and looks normal, you can start hunting the big bounties in the Underground Circuit without fearing a breakdown or a police stop.Ok lets say I finally got my bike looking normal enough. Do I then get an apartment so I can live normally? or I'm still a fugitive and must fight? What do I do next here?You are at a crossroads. You have the camouflage (clothes, bike, signal), but you do not have the **Paperwork**.

To answer your question: **No, you cannot get a normal apartment.**
In New Domino City, a legal lease requires a Citizen ID scan, a background check, and a Sector Security clearance. If you try to rent a nice studio apartment in the downtown area, the landlord will scan your ID, realize it’s a fake, and call the cops.

You are still a fugitive. You are just a **well-dressed fugitive.**

Here is your survival guide for the **"Mid-Game"** of 5D's.

### 1. Housing: The "Daimon Area"
You need to live in the **Daimon Area** (the rough, rundown district of the City).
*   **The Landlords:** They don't ask for ID. They ask for cash (DP) upfront.
*   **The Room:** It’s basically a closet above a ramen shop or a garage. It smells like grease.
*   **The Benefit:** Your neighbors are ex-cons, failed pro duelists, and smugglers. Nobody calls the cops because everyone has secrets.
*   **Storage:** Most importantly, these places have **Garages** on the ground floor to hide your D-Wheel. You never leave your bike on the street.

### 2. The Career: "The Duel Chaser"
Now that your bike works and you have a base of operations, you need to make real money. You are done hustling drunks for pocket change.

You become a **Bounty Hunter (Duel Chaser)**.
*   **The Context:** Sector Security is busy. They often outsource the capture of low-level criminals or speeders to licensed Chasers.
*   **The Loophole:** Since you have a fake signal, you sign up under a pseudonym (e.g., "Ghost").
*   **The Job:**
*   Security posts a bounty: *"Stop the 'Blackbird' Gang member on Route 7."*
*   You chase them down.
*   You force a Riding Duel.
*   You use your **Bike Burn** to wreck them.
*   The specialized system on your D-Wheel shuts their engine down when their LP hits 0.
*   **The Payday:** You collect the bounty anonymously via terminal.

**Why this fits you:**
You are a "Burn" player. You don't need to steal their cards or play honorably. You just need to crash their bike by reducing their LP to zero as fast as possible. You are the ultimate interceptor.

### 3. Deck Upgrade: Finally Buying "Bullets"
Your bike is fixed. Now you stop using trash cards. You need to upgrade your deck from "Scrap Heap" to **"Engine of Destruction."**

You visit the **Black Market Card Shop** (again, in the Daimon Area).

**The Shopping List:**
1.  **Lava Golem (Ultra Rare):**
*   *Cost:* Expensive.
*   *Why:* In the City, everyone plays **Stardust Dragon**. *Stardust* can negate destruction effects.
*   *The Counter:* *Lava Golem* does not destroy. It Tributes. It removes *Stardust Dragon* without triggering its effect, then burns them.
2.  **Speed Spell - Speed Fusion (Common/Rare):**
*   *Why:* You don't fuse. But you can use it to bluff.
*   *Wait, actually:* If you run **"Full-Force Strike"**, you take 0 damage from battles.
3.  **Zoma the Spirit (Ultra/Super Rare):**
*   *Why:* It’s a Trap Monster (1800 ATK). When it dies by battle, it deals damage equal to the attacker's ATK.
*   *Scenario:* They attack with *Red Dragon Archfiend* (3000 ATK). You flip *Zoma*. Zoma dies. They take 3000 Damage. **You win.**

### 4. The Turning Point: The Invitation
After a few months of being the terrifying "Ghost" of the highway who burns criminals for bounties, you get noticed.

Not by the Police. **By the Director.**

**Rex Goodwin** (The Director of Sector Security) is monitoring the Duel Chasers. He sees a duelist with a:
1.  95% Win Rate.
2.  Unique "Burn" Strategy (rare in Riding Duels).
3.  Mysterious background.

**The Event:**
You are eating ramen in your crappy apartment.
A man in a suit (maybe Jaeger, the clown guy) slides an envelope under your door.

**The Letter:**
*"Invitation to the Fortune Cup Qualifiers."*
*   **The Prize:** Full Citizenship. A Clean Record. A luxury penthouse in the City.
*   **The Catch:** You have to duel the best riders in the world on live TV.
*   **The Risk:** If you go on TV, everyone from the Satellite will see you. The gangs you burned. The cops you robbed.

**Do you accept?**
*   **Yes:** You risk exposure, but you get a shot at the King (Jack Atlas).
*   **No:** You stay a rat in the Daimon Area forever.

(Spoiler: You have to accept. The plot demands it. And honestly? Your *Chain Burn* deck destroys Jack Atlas's *Red Dragon Archfiend* because Jack attacks blindly.)Okay wait, there's no way I have 95% winrate with a bike burn deck or a trash ground burn deck. I mean its a burn deck, I know people only have 4000 LP, but is my burn deck really that strong?You are right to be skeptical. A **95% win rate** with a pile of wet cardboard sounds like main character plot armor.

Let’s look at the **Real Math** of the 5D's Anime Format (4000 LP) vs. your specific deck.

The truth is: **You don't have a 95% win rate against *Duelists*. You have a 95% win rate against *Idiots*.**

Here is the realistic breakdown of why your trash deck is a "Pub Stomper" but hits a hard wall against competent players.

### 1. The "4000 LP" Glitch
The single biggest reason you are winning is the format.
In real life (TCG), players start with **8000 LP**. Burn cards are balanced around that number.
In the 5D's Anime, players start with **4000 LP**.

**The Math of a Kill:**
*   **Your Hand:** *Ojama Trio*, *Just Desserts*, *Secret Barrel*.
*   **The Setup:**
1.  Opponent summons 1 monster.
2.  You activate **Ojama Trio**. (Opponent now has 4 monsters).
3.  You activate **Just Desserts**.
*   **Damage:** 500 x 4 = **2000**.
*   **Impact:** You just took **50% of their Life Points** with **two cards**.
4.  You activate **Secret Barrel** (Opponent has 4 cards in hand + 4 on field = 8 cards).
*   **Damage:** 200 x 8 = **1600**.
5.  **Total Damage:** 3600.
6.  **Remaining LP:** 400. (A stiff breeze kills them now).

**Conclusion:** In the Underground/Gang setting, people play aggressive "Beatdown" decks. They don't run healing. They don't run negation. Against these people, you are mathematically effectively playing an **FTK (First Turn Kill)** deck.

### 2. The Hard Counter: Why you aren't King of Games
However, your win rate drops to **0%** the moment you face a "Pro" or someone with a brain.

Here is what stops your Trash Burn deck cold:

**A. Prime Material Dragon (The Hard Counter)**
*   **Effect:** *Any effect that would inflict damage to a player makes them gain that much LP instead.*
*   **Scenario:** You chain *Just Desserts* for 2000 damage.
*   **Result:** They **Heal** 2000. You now have to deal 6000 damage with a deck made of garbage. You lose instantly.

**B. Royal Decree / Jinzo**
*   **Effect:** Negate all Traps.
*   **Scenario:** Your deck is 36 Traps.
*   **Result:** You are a guy riding a motorcycle with no cards. You lose.

**C. Black-Winged Dragon (Crow's Ace)**
*   **Effect:** Whenever he would take effect damage, he places a counter on this dragon instead (taking 0). Then he removes the counters to shrink your monster and burn you back.
*   **Result:** You literally fuel his boss monster.

### 3. The "Bike" Factor (Riding Duel Physics)
In a Riding Duel, your win rate is artificially higher because of **Speed World 2**.

*   **The Gun:** You have a guaranteed 800 damage every 4 turns.
*   **The Wall:** You run 3x *Waboku* and 3x *Threatening Roar*.
*   **The Reality:** Most low-level duelists in 5D's rely on **Battle** to win. They summon *Junk Warrior* and punch.
*   **You:** You negate the Battle Phase.
*   If they can't attack, they can't lower your Speed Counters.
*   If they can't lower your Speed Counters, you shoot them again.

### 4. Realistic Win Rate Adjustment

*   **Vs. Gang Members / Thugs:** **90% Win Rate.**
*   They play unoptimized decks. They over-extend. They take 2000 damage to the face and panic.
*   **Vs. Sector Security (Grunts):** **80% Win Rate.**
*   They play "Gate Blockers" or "Goyos." They are annoying but predictable.
*   **Vs. Pro Duelists (Hunter Pace, Bolt Tanner):** **40% Win Rate.**
*   They have side decks. They have *Giant Trunade*. If they bounce your backrow, you die.
*   **Vs. Main Characters (Yusei, Jack, Crow):** **10% Win Rate.**
*   Jack attacks blindly, so you *might* cheese him once.
*   Yusei is an engineer; he will figure out your loop by Turn 3 and dismantle it with *Stardust Dragon*.
*   Crow hard counters you with *Black-Winged Dragon*.

### 5. Why the "Invitation" is a Trap
Rex Goodwin invited you to the **Fortune Cup** not because you are the best duelist in the world, but because you are a **Wildcard**.

*   He sees a guy with a Trash Deck beating Gangsters in 3 turns.
*   He thinks: *"This guy will make for good TV. Either he humiliates the Pros, or the Pros humiliate him. Either way, the crowd loves a freak show."*

**Your Reality Check:**
You are a "One-Trick Pony" (just like in GX).
Your trick is incredibly lethal in a 4000 LP format, but if you go to the Fortune Cup with a deck made of wet cardboard, **Akiza Izinski** (The Black Rose Witch) will literally manifest her psychic powers and put you in the hospital.

You need to spend that "Bounty Hunter" money on **Upgrades** before the tournament starts. You need **Lava Golem**. You need **Metaion the Timelord**. You need insurance.Lets talk about cost though, (since dp actually matters here) how much dp do I need here?
1. To survive (get sleeve, get clothes, rent an "apartment")
2. To upgrade (upgrade bike, get a decent card, maybe a bit quality of life upgrade)
3. To become a legitimate bounty hunter (get fake signal, upgrade my deck (and bike again))Based on the economy of the *World Championship* games (which simulate the anime world) and the lore of 5D's, here is your **Financial Roadmap to Legitimacy**.

In this world, **1 DP ≈ 10 to 20 Yen** (roughly 10-20 cents).
*   **A Win:** 500 - 1,000 DP.
*   **A Pack of Cards:** 150 DP.

### Phase 1: Survival (The "Civilian" Disguise)
**Goal:** Stop looking like a homeless person so Sector Security doesn't scan you.
**Target Budget:** **5,000 DP** (approx. 5-10 Duel Wins).

1.  **Card Sleeves (Black/Opaque):** **500 DP.**
*   *Critical:* Hides the water damage on your "trash" cards. If you play unsleeved cards in the City, you are immediately flagged as Satellite scum.
2.  **Clothes (The "Generic Duelist" Skin):** **1,500 DP.**
*   Buy a used leather jacket and jeans from a thrift store in Daimon. You need to cover your Satellite markings/tattoos.
3.  **Rent (The "Coffin"):** **3,000 DP / Month.**
*   A closet-sized room in the Daimon Area (slums of the City).
*   *Includes:* A lock on the door, electricity, and a shared garage for your bike.
*   *Doesn't Include:* Hot water (use public baths).

**Status:** You are now "The Ghost." You have a roof and a deck that looks legal.

---

### Phase 2: The Bike Upgrade (The "Runner")
**Goal:** Make your bike stable enough to hold Speed Counters so you can actually burn people.
**Target Budget:** **12,000 DP** (approx. 20-30 Duel Wins).

1.  **Engine (Rank II - "DWE-2000"):** **4,000 DP.**
*   Your scavenged forklift engine vibrates too much. This upgrade stops the bike from shaking your cards off the dashboard.
2.  **CPU (Rank II - "KPC-450"):** **5,000 DP.**
*   *Essential:* Reduces the lag between playing a Trap Card and the bike responding. Allows you to chain 3 cards without the system crashing.
3.  **Frame (Rank I - Shield):** **3,000 DP.**
*   Reduces the Speed Counters you lose when you take damage. Essential for a Burn player, because you *will* take hits.

**Status:** Your bike is no longer a death trap. It is a "Mid-Tier" street racer.

---

### Phase 3: The "Bounty Hunter" Conversion (The Pro)
**Goal:** Become a registered Hunter so you can make legal money.
**Target Budget:** **20,000+ DP** (Grinding Underground Tournaments).

1.  **The Fake ID Signal:** **10,000 DP.**
*   You pay a hacker (like Blister/Saiga) to register your bike's CPU as a "Legal" City D-Wheel.
*   *Result:* Police scanners will read you as "John Doe, Citizen" instead of "UNREGISTERED SATELLITE TRASH."
2.  **Deck Upgrade ("Engine of Destruction"):** **10,000 DP.**
*   You need to buy the **"Structure Deck: Marik"** or similar Burn staples from the black market.
*   **Lava Golem (Ultra Rare):** 3,000 DP each. (Get 2).
*   **Metaion, the Timelord:** 5,000 DP (The "I Win" button).

### The Daily Grind Schedule

*   **Morning:** Sleep (Underground duels happen at night).
*   **Afternoon:** Hunt for "Bounties" posted on the dark web.
*   *Target:* "The Slasher" (Uses a generic Warrior deck).
*   *Reward:* 2,000 DP.
*   *Your Strategy:* Chase him down on the highway. Burn him for 4000 damage. Collect the fee.
*   **Evening:** Maintenance. Spend 500 DP on "Ramen" and "Canned Coffee."
*   **Night:** Underground Duel Circuit.
*   Betting matches. You bet 1,000 DP to win 2,000 DP.
*   *Deck:* Use **Chain Burn**. Win in 3 turns. Leave before they realize you played zero monsters.

**Total Time to "Legitimacy":** About **3 Months** of grinding.
After 3 months, you will have the bike, the deck, and the ID to enter the **Fortune Cup** without getting arrested immediately.What if I go to prison though? you said theres an essential characters there that I have to meet?If you choose the **Prison Route**, you are effectively choosing to join the **Main Plot**.

In the "Solo Grind" route, you are just a wealthy fugitive.
In the "Prison" route, you get the **Mark** (the yellow criminal tattoo on your face) and the **Connections** to actually enter the Pro League.

Here is what happens if you let Sector Security catch you, and why it might be the smartest (and most painful) decision you make.

### 1. The Arrest: "Welcome to the Facility"
You are cornered. Maybe you ran out of gas, or maybe Officer Trudge brought backup.
*   **The Confiscation:** They take your D-Wheel. They take your Deck.
*   **The Branding:** You are strapped to a chair. A laser etches a yellow marker onto your cheek.
*   *Effect:* You are now permanently marked as "Trash." You can never get a legal job in the City again. You are banned from most shops.
*   **The Transfer:** You are thrown into a cell with two other inmates.

### 2. The Essential Characters (Cellmates)

You are dropped into the cell with **Yanagi Tenzen** (The Old Man) and **Bolt Tanner** (The Ex-Pro).

#### **A. Yanagi Tenzen (The Lore/Bank)**
*   **Who he is:** An eccentric old man who claims to have "Treasure Cards."
*   **Why you need him:**
1.  **Totem Pole (Card):** In the anime, he gives Yusei this card. It protects you from 3 attacks. It is *perfect* for your Burn strategy.
2.  **Money:** In the lore, Yanagi is actually wealthy/has artifacts. If you protect him from bullies, he becomes your patron later.

#### **B. Bolt Tanner (The Connection)**
*   **Who he is:** A former Pro Duelist who fell from grace. He is the "Boss" of the cell block.
*   **The Hazing:** He will challenge you. "You think you're tough, Satellite? Use Yanagi's trash deck and beat me."
*   **The Duel:**
*   **Tanner's Deck:** **"Ushi Oni" Beatdown**. He summons big monsters and hits hard.
*   **Your Strategy:** You use Yanagi’s "Crystal Skull" (Burn damage) and "Totem Pole" (Stall).
*   **Result:** You win. Tanner respects strength.
*   **The Reward (Crucial):** Tanner gives you the contact info for **Blister (Saiga)**.
*   *Who is Blister?* The best mechanic/forger in the City. He is the guy who can make your D-Wheel legal and get you the Fake ID signal for free (or cheap).

### 3. The Boss Fight: Warden Armstrong
The Warden (Takasu) runs an illegal underground dueling ring in the prison. He finds out you are a strong duelist (because you beat Tanner).
He offers you a deal: **"Beat me, and I'll pardon you and your friends."**

**The Matchup: Chain Burn vs. Iron Chain**
*   **Armstrong's Deck:** **"Iron Chain"**.
*   *Strategy:* Mill (sending cards from your deck to grave) and Burn.
*   *Ace:* **Iron Chain Dragon** (2500 ATK).
*   **The Handicap:** You don't have your D-Wheel. You are using your confiscated **Ground Burn Deck** (Security usually returns it for these "entertainment" matches).

**The Duel:**
*   **Armstrong:** "I activate **Poison Chain**! If I attack, you lose cards from your deck!"
*   **You:** "I don't care about my deck. I care about *Just Desserts*."
*   **The Interaction:**
*   Armstrong summons 3 monsters to swarm you.
*   **You:** "Thank you for the ammo."
*   **Chain 1:** *Just Desserts* (1500 Dmg).
*   **Chain 2:** *Secret Barrel* (1000 Dmg).
*   **Chain 3:** *Accumulated Fortune*.
*   **The Climax:** Armstrong tries to shock you with his electric collar (he cheats). You grit your teeth, flip **Dimension Wall**, and reflect his Iron Chain Dragon's attack right back at him.

### 4. The Aftermath: Freedom & The "Mark"
You win. Armstrong is humiliated. Rex Goodwin (The Director) watches the footage and orders your release because he wants to see you in the Fortune Cup.

**The Trade-off:**
*   **Bad News:** You have a tattoo on your face. Everyone knows you are a criminal.
*   **Good News:**
1.  **Blister (Saiga)** contacts you. He respects Tanner, so he helps you. He upgrades your D-Wheel **for free** and gives you a top-tier CPU.
2.  **Street Cred:** In the Underground, you are a legend. "The guy who beat the Warden."
3.  **Accommodation:** You can crash at Blister’s hideout in the Daimon Area. It’s safer than a solo apartment.

### Verdict: Should you go to Prison?
**YES.**
If you stay solo, you are just grinding for scraps.
If you go to Prison, you skip the "Grind Phase." You get the upgrades, the contacts, and the plot relevance immediately.

**Plus:** Beating a corrupt Warden with a pile of Common Trap cards is the ultimate "Burn Player" satisfaction. Just make sure you don't drop the soap—or your *Mirror Force*.ok but my deck still sucks though. Do I still go with the "Slightly not that trash ground burn" and the "Bike burn v2" with the time lords and the golem? or do I start slowly accumulating syncrho monsters since I have blister's hideout?This is the "Mid-Season Upgrade" moment.

You are living with Blister (Saiga), a master mechanic and former Pro. You have a roof, coffee, and internet access.
**Do not switch to Synchros.**

Here is the cold, hard truth about the 5D's economy and the "Synchro Trap":

1.  **The "Pay-to-Win" Gap:** To play a competent Synchro deck, you need **Tuners** (like *Plaguespreader Zombie* or *Gale the Whirlwind*) and **Bosses** (like *Stardust Dragon* or *Brionac*). These are the most expensive cards in the City. You would be trading your entire life savings for a mediocre Synchro deck that Jack Atlas would crush in two turns.
2.  **The "Identity" Advantage:** Everyone in the Fortune Cup plays Synchro. They all side-deck against *other* Synchro decks. If you show up with a deck that **punishes Special Summoning**, you have the element of surprise.

You shouldn't try to beat them at their own game. You should double down on being the **"Synchro Killer."**

Here is your Roadmap to upgrading from "Trash Burn" to **"Disaster Burn" (Pro Version)** using Blister's connections.

---

### 1. The Living Situation: "The Safehouse"
*   **Rent:** 0 DP (Blister lets you crash because Tanner vouched for you).
*   **Expenses:** You pay for parts and food.
*   **Status:** You are "safe" indoors, but the moment you step outside, that Mark on your face makes you a target. You are effectively under house arrest unless you are wearing a helmet/hoodie.

### 2. The Deck Upgrade: "Disaster Burn"
Your "Trash" deck was 36 Traps. It’s too brittle. If they have *Royal Decree*, you die.
You need **Monsters** that function as removal and defense.

**The Shopping List (Blister's Black Market Connection):**

*   **3x Lava Golem (Ultra Rare)**
*   *Cost:* Expensive (~9,000 DP).
*   *Why:* The ultimate Synchro Killer. Opponent summons *Stardust Dragon* and *Red Dragon Archfiend*? You Tribute them both. You remove their unbreakable board and give them a 3000 ATK cage that burns them.
*   **3x Battle Fader (Super Rare)**
*   *Cost:* Medium.
*   *Why:* In 5D's, One-Turn-Kills (OTK) are common.
*   *Effect:* When they attack directly, you Special Summon this and **End the Battle Phase**.
*   *Synergy:* It stays on the field to be Tributed for *Lava Golem* (if they give it back) or *Caius*.
*   **3x Swift Scarecrow (Common)**
*   *Why:* Poor man's Battle Fader. Stops the OTK.
*   **2x Morphing Jar (Rare)**
*   *Why:* Burn decks run out of cards. This refills your hand and likely mills their combo pieces.

**The "Tech" Cards (The Anti-Meta):**
*   **Thunder King Rai-Oh:** (Stops them from searching decks).
*   **Dimensional Prison:** (Banishes attacking monsters instead of destroying them. Counters *Stardust Dragon*).

---

### 3. The "Timelord" Quest (The Ace Card)
You asked about **Metaion the Timelord**.
*   **Lore Check:** These cards technically belong to **Z-one** (the final villain from the future). They shouldn't exist in shops.
*   **The Glitch:** However, in the *World Championship* games, rare cards sometimes "leak" into the underground.
*   **How you get it:** You don't buy it. You find a "Ghost Myth."
*   *Rumor:* There's a crazed duelist in the deep Underground who claims to have seen the "End of the World."
*   *The Duel:* You hunt him down. He plays a weird Stall deck. You beat him.
*   *The Loot:* You take **1x Metaion, the Timelord**.
*   **Why it wins you the Fortune Cup:**
*   Cannot be destroyed by battle or card effects.
*   You take 0 battle damage.
*   **Effect:** At the end of the Battle Phase, return all monsters to the hand and inflict 300 damage for each.
*   *Result:* It bounces *Red Dragon Archfiend* and *Stardust Dragon* back to the Extra Deck. It is the perfect counter to Synchros.

---

### 4. The Bike Upgrade: "The Interceptor"
Blister is a mechanic. Use him.
Your "Trash Bike" was held together by tape. Blister rebuilds it into a **Interceptor**.

*   **Frame:** **Omega-Frame (Defensive)**.
*   *Effect:* heavily armored. You barely lose Speed Counters when taking damage.
*   **The "Gun":** **Speed Spell - Speed Fusion** (if you run Fusion) or **Speed Spell - The End of Storm**.
*   *The End of Storm:* Requires 10 Speed Counters. Destroys all monsters on the field and inflicts damage. It is a nuke.
*   **Appearance:** Matte Black. No lights except the dashboard. Hard to track at night.

### 5. The Fortune Cup Invitation
With your **Disaster Burn** deck and **Interceptor Bike**, you accept the invitation.

**The Bracket:**

*   **How he beats you:** **Plot Armor.**
*   He will likely Synchro Summon **Stardust Dragon** again from the grave, or use a specific trap like **"Synchro Barrier"** to take 0 damage.
*   **Win Rate:** **40%**. Mathematically, you win. Narratively, he pulls a miracle.

### The Finals: The King (Jack Atlas)
If you somehow beat Yusei (or if the bracket is different), you face Jack.

*   He attacks into **Magic Cylinder** (if you found one).
*   He ends his turn with a big dragon. You **Metaion the Timelord** it back to his hand and burn him.
*   **Win Rate:** **60%**. Jack is easier to beat than Yusei because Jack refuses to play defensively.

In the real-world 2009-2010 era (the exact time of *5D's*), Blackwings weren't just "good." They were the **World Championship Winning Deck**. They were arguably the strongest "budget" deck in the history of the game.

You have stumbled upon the perfect "Satellite God Deck." Here is why Blackwings are your destiny.

### 1. The "Broken" Reality
You asked if they were "really, really broken."
**Yes.**
*   **Speed:** In an era where people Normal Summoned one monster and passed, Blackwings could put 3 monsters (2000+ ATK) on board in Turn 1 without losing card advantage.
*   **The Cheat Code:** **Blackwing - Gale the Whirlwind**.
*   *Effect:* Permanently halves the ATK/DEF of an opponent's monster.
*   *Result:* It can run over *Stardust Dragon* (2500 ATK -> 1250 ATK) by itself.
*   *Real Life:* This card was so broken it was Limited to 1 copy almost immediately.
*   **The Engine:** **Black Whirlwind** (Continuous Spell).
*   *Effect:* When you Normal Summon a Blackwing, add another one from your deck to your hand.
*   *Result:* You never run out of cards. You summon a monster, get a monster, special summon that monster, make a Synchro, and you still have a full hand.

### 2. The "Satellite" Lore Loophole
This is the best part for your roleplay.
In the anime, **Crow Hogan** plays Blackwings.
*   **Who is Crow?** A thief from the Satellite slums.
*   **How rich is he?** He is broke. He lives with orphans.
*   **The Implication:** In the *5D's* universe, Blackwings are **Common Cards**. They are not "Rich Kid" cards like *Blue-Eyes* or *Hero*. They are the "birds of the trash heap."

**You can build a Tier 1 World Championship deck using cards you found in the garbage.**

### 3. The Pivot: Burn -> Blackwings
This fits your "Disaster" arc perfectly.
*   **Old You:** "I play Burn because I can't afford big monsters."
*   **New You:** "I found a flock of birds that eat Dragons."

**The Decklist ("The Crow" Build):**

*   **3x Blackwing - Gale the Whirlwind:** (The God Card. Halves stats).
*   **3x Blackwing - Kalut the Moon Shadow:** (Hand Trap. Discard to boost a Blackwing by 1400 ATK during battle. *Nobody sees this coming in the anime*).
*   **3x Black Whirlwind:** (The infinite resource generator).
*   **1x Blackwing - Armor Master:** (Synchro Boss. Cannot be destroyed by battle, you take 0 damage).

### 4. The "Underground" Advantage
If you play this in the Underground or the Fortune Cup:
*   **The Opponent:** "Ha! A bunch of tiny birds? I summon **Red Dragon Archfiend** (3000 ATK)!"
*   **You:** "I Special Summon Gale. I halve your Dragon's ATK to 1500. My Bora (1700) attacks and kills it."
*   **The Crowd:** Goes insane. You just killed the King's ace monster with a little bird.

**Verdict:**
**Switch to Blackwings immediately.**
It is the ultimate "Rags to Riches" deck. It destroys the Synchro Meta (by halving their stats), it fits your Satellite origin, and unlike Burn, it earns you **Respect**.

In the real-world 2009 meta (the peak of the 5D's era), Blackwings were the best deck in the world. They were cheap, fast, and could destroy anything.
**But you are right:** Because **Crow Hogan** (a main character) plays them, you cannot touch them under your "NPC Rule." If you start playing Blackwings, Crow will sue you for copyright infringement (or just beat you up).

So, let's pivot to your second choice: **Gladiator Beasts**.

### 1. Are "Gladiator Beasts" safe to play?
**YES.**
*   **In 5D's Anime:** **Nobody** plays them. They are completely free.
*   **In GX Anime:** Nobody played them (they came out too late).
*   **In Arc-V (Years later):** A villain named "Battle Beast" uses them, but that is a different dimension/timeline.

For the purposes of 5D's, Gladiator Beasts are considered a **"Foreign Pro League Deck."** They are the deck of a faceless, elite mercenary. They fit your "Bounty Hunter" vibe perfectly.

### 2. Why Gladiator Beasts are the "Mercenary God Deck"
If Blackwings are "Aggro Swarm," **Gladiator Beasts are "Control/Toolbox."** They are arguably the most intelligent deck of the era.

**The Gimmick:**
They don't stay on the field. They attack, do their job, and tag out into the deck to summon a different Gladiator.
*   *Need to destroy a monster?* Tag in **Murmillo**.
*   *Need to destroy a Spell/Trap?* Tag in **Bestiari**.
*   *Need a 2100 ATK beater?* Tag in **Laquari**.

**The Boss Monster: Gladiator Beast Gyzarus**
This is the card that makes you rich.
*   **Summoning Condition:** "Contact Fusion." You don't need *Polymerization*. You just shuffle **Bestiari** + another Gladiator from your field into the deck.
*   **The Effect:** When summoned, **Target up to 2 cards on the field; destroy them.**
*   **The Loop:**
1.  Summon Gyzarus. Pop 2 cards.
2.  Attack (2400 ATK).
3.  End of Battle Phase: Tag Gyzarus out for 2 new Gladiators.
4.  Next turn: Contact Fuse them *again* to summon Gyzarus *again* and pop 2 *more* cards.

### 3. The "Mercenary" Deck List (Year 1 Satellite/Underground)
Since you are a Bounty Hunter living in the warehouse district, this deck is perfect. It looks like a pile of weird animals until you dismantle their entire board.

**Monsters (18):**
*   **3x Gladiator Beast Laquari:** (The Beater. 1800 -> 2100 ATK).
*   **2x Gladiator Beast Darius:** (The Reviver. Special summons a Glad from grave to enable fusion).
*   **2x Gladiator Beast Bestiari:** (The Sniper. Destroys backrow).
*   **1x Gladiator Beast Murmillo:** (The Assassin. Destroys monsters).
*   **1x Gladiator Beast Hoplomus:** (The Wall. 2100 DEF).
*   **3x Test Tiger:** (The Combo Piece. Tributes itself to shuffle your Glad and summon a new one instantly. This triggers their effects without battling).
*   **2x Rescue Cat:** (If you can find/afford it. Summons 2 Test Tigers. **Banned in real life later because it was too broken**).
*   **1x Cyber Dragon:** (Bait).

**Spells (12):**
*   **3x Gladiator Proving Ground:** (Searcher. Adds any Glad Beast to hand).
*   **3x Book of Moon:** (Defensive/Offensive. Flips monsters face down so Murmillo can pop them easily).
*   **2x Enemy Controller:** (Take their monster).
*   **1x Giant Trunade:** (Clear the backrow so you can attack safely).
*   **3x Shrink:** (Quick-Play. Halves opponent's ATK. Ensures your Gladiators survive battle so they can tag out).

**Traps (10):**
*   **3x Gladiator Beast War Chariot:** **(THE BEST CARD).**
*   *Effect:* Counter Trap. If you control a Gladiator Beast, **Negate the activation of an Effect Monster and destroy it.**
*   *Why it's broken:* If you have this set, *Stardust Dragon* cannot negate you. *Black Rose Dragon* cannot nuke you. You just say "No."
*   **3x Waboku:** (Protects your monsters so they can survive battle and tag out).

### 4. How you hunt Bounties with this
**Target:** A fugitive playing a Synchro Deck (e.g., X-Sabers).

**Turn 1 (You):**
*   Summon **Laquari**. Set 3 cards (*War Chariot*, *Waboku*, *Book of Moon*).

**Turn 2 (Target):**
*   **Target:** "I Synchro Summon **X-Saber Urbellum**!"
*   **Target:** "I activate effect!"
*   **You:** "Flip **War Chariot**. Negate and Destroy."
*   **Target:** "What?!"

**Turn 3 (You):**
*   **You:** "I summon **Test Tiger**. Tribute Tiger to shuffle Laquari. Special Summon **Bestiari**."
*   **Bestiari Effect:** Destroy his face-down Trap.
*   **You:** "Contact Fusion! Bestiari + Laquari... Combine!"
*   **Summon:** **Gladiator Beast Gyzarus**.
*   **Gyzarus Effect:** Destroy his last monster and his Field Spell.
*   **Battle:** Direct Attack (2400).
*   **Tag Out:** Gyzarus leaves. Summon **Darius** and **Laquari**. Darius brings back Bestiari.
*   **Result:** You cleared his board and established a full field loop.

### Summary
*   **Is it Meta?** Yes. It won the 2008 World Championship.
*   **Is it Allowed?** Yes. No main character uses it.
*   **Does it fit you?** Perfect for a cold, calculating Bounty Hunter. You don't overpower them; you dismantle them piece by piece.

1.  **Phase 1 (Trash Burn):** Cost: **0 DP**. Scavenged. Use this to escape Satellite.
2.  **Phase 2 (Macro Monarchs):** Cost: **3,000 DP**. Buy 3x Structure Decks. Use this to farm Bounties and kill Stardust Dragons.
3.  **Phase 3 (Gladiator Beasts):** Cost: **50,000+ DP**. Luxury upgrade. Only buy this when you are ready to turn Pro and need a deck that can play the long game.

**My Advice:**
Stay on **Phase 2 (Macro Monarchs)** for most of the story.

To answer your question: **Do you win more or less with Monarchs in the Fortune Cup?**

**You win SIGNIFICANTLY more.**
Your Win Rate jumps from "Lucky Cheese" (60%) to "Oppressive Domination" (95%).

Here is why **Macro Monarchs (Caius/Fissure)** is the absolute worst nightmare for every character in the Fortune Cup.

### 1. The "Graveyard" Mechanic Kill Switch
In *5D's*, almost every single duelist relies on the **Graveyard** to Synchro Summon.
*   **Yusei:** Uses *Junk Synchron* to revive a monster from the **Grave** to Synchro.
*   **Akiza:** Uses *Lonefire Blossom* (Tribute to **Grave**) and *Phoenixian Cluster Amaryllis* (Needs plants in **Grave**).
*   **Greiger:** Uses *Dark Flattop* to revive Reactors from the **Grave**.

**Your Card:** **Dimensional Fissure / Macro Cosmos**.
*   **Effect:** "Any monster sent to the Graveyard is Banished instead."
*   **The Result:** You don't just "beat" them. You **turn off their decks.**
*   Yusei summons *Junk Synchron*. He targets... nothing. The grave is empty. He ends his turn with a 1300 ATK monster.
*   Akiza tries to use *Lonefire*. She can't pay the cost (must send to Grave). Her plants wither.

### 2. The Matchup Analysis (The Cup Run)

#### **Round 1: Sir Gill Randsborg (The Knight)**
*   **His Deck:** Masked Knights (LV Monsters).
*   **His Play:** He protects his monsters from **Destruction** effects using traps.
*   **Your Monarch Play:** You summon **Caius the Shadow Monarch**.
*   *Effect:* **Banish** 1 card.
*   *Result:* Protection doesn't matter. Banishment bypasses "Cannot be destroyed."
*   **Status:** **Easy Win.**

#### **Round 2: Akiza Izinski (The Witch)**
This is where the Burn deck would have struggled (she nukes the field).
**Macro Monarchs DESTROY her.**

*   **Turn 1 (You):** Activate **Dimensional Fissure**. Set **D.D. Survivor**.
*   **Turn 2 (Akiza):** She draws. She looks at her hand full of *Gigaplant* and *Copy Plant*.
*   She realizes she cannot load her Graveyard.
*   She cannot summon **Black Rose Dragon** easily because her "Revival" combos are dead.
*   **Turn 3 (You):** Tribute *D.D. Survivor* for **Caius**.
*   Banish her face-down plant.
*   Attack directly.
*   End Phase: *D.D. Survivor* returns (because it was banished).
*   **Win Rate:** **99%**. You accidentally hard-countered her entire strategy. The crowd watches the dreaded "Black Rose Witch" get shut down by a guy playing a Structure Deck.

#### **Semi-Finals: Yusei Fudo (The Protagonist)**
*   **His Deck:** Junk/Stardust.
*   **The Problem:** Yusei is scrappy. He uses traps like *Scrap-Iron Scarecrow* to stop attacks.
*   **Your Solution:** **Mobius the Frost Monarch**.
*   You Tribute a *Survivor*.
*   Mobius pops his *Scarecrow* and his *Speed Spell*.
*   You activate **Macro Cosmos**.
*   **The Climax:** Yusei tries to play *Stardust Dragon*.
*   He manages to get it out (maybe using a spell you didn't banish).
*   He attacks.
*   You drop **Battle Fader**. (End Battle Phase).
*   Next Turn: Tribute *Battle Fader* for **Caius**.
*   **Target Stardust.**
*   *Yusei:* "I Tribute Stardust to negate... wait!"
*   *You:* "Caius doesn't destroy. He banishes. Stardust can't respond."
*   **Win Rate:** **90%**. You have him in a chokehold. The only way he wins is if he draws a specific non-graveyard removal spell (like *Raigeki Break* but discarding a spell) to pop your *Macro Cosmos*.

### 3. The "Jack Atlas" Factor
If you face Jack (The King):
*   **Jack:** "My Power is absolute!"
*   **You:** "I activate **Soul Exchange**."
*   **Jack:** "What?"
*   **You:** "I Tribute your **Red Dragon Archfiend** to summon **Caius**."
*   **Jack:** "YOU DARE USE MY SOUL AS FODDER?!"
*   **You:** "It's just resources, Jack. Banish your backrow. 1000 Damage."

### Conclusion: You are the Villain of the Tournament
By switching to **Macro Monarchs**, you become the **"Anti-Fun"** police.
*   The crowd wants to see cool Synchro Summons.
*   You are forcing everyone to play "Vanilla Beatdown" because their graveyards are locked.
*   **Akiza** hates you (her trauma is fueled by her powers, and you stopped her from using them).
*   **Yusei** respects you (tactically), but finds your style oppressive.
*   **Rex Goodwin** is fascinated. He sees you as a force of "Void" (Banishment) challenging the "Signers" (Life/Graveyard).

**Verdict:**
Switch to Monarchs. I feel like my win rate shouldn't be that highYou are absolutely right to be skeptical. In a real-world competitive environment (circa 2008-2009), a **90% win rate** is statistically impossible. Even the best decks in history (like TeleDAD or Zoodiac) hovered around 60-65% against other skilled players.

However, you are in the **5D's Anime**. Context is everything.

Here is the realistic breakdown of why your win rate is artificially inflated, but also where the "cracks" in your armor will actually show up.

### 1. Why you feel like a God (The Anime Factor)
In the anime, you are winning 90% of the time because you are bringing a gun to a knife fight.

*   **The "Main Phase 2" Glitch:** Anime characters almost **never** play optimally in Main Phase 2.
*   *Real Life:* A player attacks, you stop it, they use Main Phase 2 to set up defenses.
*   *Anime:* They attack, you use *Waboku*, they end their turn.
*   **Gladiator Beast Abuse:** Since they rarely set up post-combat defenses, your Gladiators (who tag out at the *end* of the Battle Phase) are almost guaranteed to resolve their effects safely.
*   **The "Battle Phase" Reliance:**
*   5D's decks (Blackwings, Synchros) are aggressive. They want to punch you.
*   Gladiator Beasts **thrive** on being attacked. If they attack your *Hoplomus* (2100 DEF) and fail, you get to tag out and pop their card. You punish them for playing the game.
*   **Gyzarus vs. No Protection:**
*   Anime boss monsters (like *Red Dragon Archfiend*) have **zero protection** against destruction.
*   **Gyzarus** destroys 2 cards for free. It is a "Delete Button" that anime decks simply cannot handle.

### 2. The Reality Check: Where you SHOULD lose
If you were playing against real 2009 meta decks, your win rate would be closer to **55-60%**. Here is why Gladiator Beasts are not invincible:

*   **The "Battle" Weakness:**
*   If you cannot declare an attack (e.g., *Threatening Roar*, *Gravity Bind*), or if you cannot destroy their monster by battle, **your deck does nothing.** You just sit there with a 1800 ATK vanilla monster.
*   *Crow Hogan* (Blackwings) is your hardest counter. **Blackwing - Kalut** (Hand trap that boosts ATK) means if you attack his bird, your Gladiator dies, and you don't get to tag out.
*   **The "Brick" Factor:**
*   Gladiator Beasts are famous for "bricking." If you draw all Spells/Traps and no Monsters, you die.
*   If you draw all Monsters and no protection (*Waboku* / *War Chariot*), you get run over by a Synchro monster.
*   **Tele-DAD (The Real Tier 0):**
*   If a Sector Security Chief pulls up with a **Tele-DAD** deck (Dark Armed Dragon), you will lose. They can blow up your field *before* the Battle Phase. You never get a chance to tag out.

### 3. Your "Real" Win Rate Estimate
*   **Vs. Grunts/Gangs:** **95%**. (They don't understand the Tag-Out mechanic).
*   **Vs. Competent Synchro Users (Yusei/Jack):** **60%**. (Your *War Chariot* carries you).
*   **Vs. Anti-Meta / Mirror Matches:** **50%**.

### 4. The "Imposter Syndrome"
The reason you feel like your win rate "shouldn't be this high" is because Gladiator Beasts are a **"Snowball"** deck.
*   If you resolve **one** successful attack/tag-out, you generate "Plus 1" advantage.
*   If you resolve **Gyzarus**, you generate "Plus 2" advantage.
*   In Yu-Gi-Oh, once you are "+2" cards ahead of an anime character (who discards their whole hand to summon one monster), the game is effectively over. It feels easy because the math has already guaranteed your victory.

**Conclusion:**
You aren't cheating. You are just playing a **Control Deck** in a world of **Aggro Players**. You are the guy bringing a shield to a fistfight. Enjoy the win streak while it lasts, because the moment the writers decide Yusei needs to "clear his mind," he will draw a card that negates your entire Battle Phase.ok I guess its a snowball deck, but its still a tier 1 deck. For **You**, however?
This season is a **Victory Lap**.

### 1. The Economy: Going Corporate

### 4. The Tournament Strategy (The WRGP)
The WRGP is a **Relay Survival Duel**.
*   When Player 1 loses, Player 2 takes over with the *same field and LP situation*.
*   **Speed World 2** is active.

**Your Deck Adaptation:**
Riding Duels ban "Normal Spells."
*   **Problem:** *Gladiator Proving Ground* (Searcher) is a Normal Spell. *Shrink* is a Quick-Play Spell (Requires Speed Counters to use Speed Spell version).
*   **Solution:** You pivot to **"Trap Glad Beasts"**.

*   **You:** "I have **Gladiator Beast Darius**. I shuffle my monsters back into the deck to Fuse. You can't deck me out."

*   **Hook the Hidden Knight:** Tries to crash your bike by locking your wheels.

*   **Result:** You are the only team that doesn't crash.

*   **Merch:** Kids are buying Gladiator Beast plushies.
*   **Status:** You are the "Anti-Synchro King." The "Man Who Refused to Evolve."
*   **Money:** You have enough DP to buy a penthouse in the City. You buy Blister a new shop.

### 6. The Finale: The Ark Cradle
At the end of the season, a giant floating city (**The Ark Cradle**) descends to crush Neo Domino City.
*   **The Threat:** **Z-one** and his **Timelords**.
*   **Timelords:** Cannot be destroyed. Return monsters to hand. Burn you.

**This is the only time you struggle.**
*   **Gyzarus:** "Destroy 2 cards."

You cannot beat Z-one. His deck counters yours (Destruction immunity > Gyzarus).

*   **The Legacy:** While Yusei flies into space to fight God, you are on the ground, using **Heraklinos** (Negate Spells/Traps) to protect the civilians from the robot army.

The yellow monarch would obviously play monarch, but Not sure what tim would play thoughTo answer your first question: **No, there is no Season 4.**
*Yu-Gi-Oh! 5D's* ends with the Ark Cradle (Season 3/Finale). After Yusei fights Z-one, the show ends. They planned more, but the production moved on to *Zexal*.

So, the **WRGP (World Racing Grand Prix)** is your final stage.

Now, let’s assemble **Team Tradition** (aka "Team Boomer").
The concept is hilarious. You have three guys entering a Formula 1 race driving 1960s Muscle Cars (Old Decks), and they are absolutely wrecking the futuristic F1 cars (Synchro Decks).

Here is the roster, the decks, and the "Get Off My Lawn" energy of your team.

***

### The Roster: Team Tradition

*   **Player 1 (First Wheel):** **Turtle Tim** (The Wall).
*   **Player 2 (Second Wheel):** **The Yellow Monarch** (Your Past Self / The King).
*   **Player 3 (Last Wheel):** **You** (Gladiator Beast / The Mercenary).

**The Gimmick:**
Everyone else is speeding up (Synchro Summoning). Your team’s goal is to **Stop the Car.**

---

### Player 1: Turtle Tim
**The Deck:** **"Rock Stun" (Anti-Meta Beatdown)**.
Tim used to play *Big Shield Gardna* (Passive Wall). He has realized that in a race, a stationary wall gets run over. He needs a **Spiked Wall**.

**The Decklist:**
*   **3x Fossil Dyna Pachycephalo:** (The MVP).
*   *Effect:* **Neither player can Special Summon.**
*   *Lore:* In a Synchro era, this card is illegal. Yusei tries to tune *Junk Warrior*? **Nope.** Jack tries to summon *Red Dragon*? **Nope.**
*   **3x Koa'ki Meiru Guardian:** (1900 ATK Rock).
*   *Effect:* Tribute to negate a Monster Effect.
*   **3x Neo-Spacian Grand Mole:** (The Annoyance).
*   *Effect:* Bounces any monster it battles back to the hand without damage calculation.
*   *Why:* It removes *Stardust Dragon* without destroying it.

**The Strategy:**
Tim drives out first. He Normal Summons **Fossil Dyna**. He sets 3 Trap Cards (*Dimensional Prison*, *Compulsory Evacuation Device*).
*   **Opponent (Team Unicorn):** "I activate Speed World! I will Synchro Summon—wait, why is my Extra Deck locked?"
*   **Tim:** "Speed Limit is 55. Slow down."
*   **Result:** Tim forces the opponent to play "Caveman Yugioh" (Set 1, Pass). He chips them down slowly.

---

### Player 2: The Yellow Monarch (Past You)
**The Deck:** **"Vanity's Monarchs"**.
Since Riding Duels ban Normal Spells, your old Monarch deck needs a pivot. You can't rely on *Soul Exchange* (Spell) as easily. You rely on **Floodgates**.

**The Decklist:**
*   **3x Caius the Shadow Monarch:** (Banishment).
*   **3x Raiza the Storm Monarch:** (Spin to top of deck - ruins their Draw Phase).
*   **3x Vanity's Fiend:** (The Ace).
*   *Stats:* 2400 ATK.
*   *Effect:* **Neither player can Special Summon.**
*   *Why:* It is a Monarch that doesn't need to return to hand. It just sits there and forbids Synchro Summoning.
*   **Engine:** **Treeborn Frog** (Still works in Riding Duels as long as you have no Traps).

**The Strategy:**
Tim eventually loses (or tags out). The opponent finally killed *Fossil Dyna*. They are ready to accelerate!
*   **Yellow Monarch:** "I Tribute Tim's *Grand Mole* to Summon **Vanity's Fiend**."
*   **Opponent:** "Oh come on! Another lock?!"
*   **Yellow Monarch:** "Respect your elders. No Synchros allowed."

---

### Player 3: You (The Gladiator)
**The Deck:** **"Trap-Glad" (Gyzarus Control)**.
By the time it gets to you, the opponent is exhausted. They have wasted all their resources trying to kill Tim's Rocks and the Monarchs.

**The Strategy:**
*   You come out on the fastest bike.
*   You contact Fuse **Gyzarus**.
*   **Pop 2 Cards.**
*   The opponent has no cards left.
*   You finish the race.

---

### The Scene: Team Tradition vs. Team 5D's

**The Crowd:**
"Who are these guys? Why do their D-Wheels sound like tractors? Why aren't they tuning?"

**The Matchup: Jack Atlas vs. Turtle Tim**
*   **Jack:** "I am the King! My Red Dragon Archfiend crushes all defenses!"
*   **Tim:** "I summon **Neo-Spacian Grand Mole**. Attack Red Dragon."
*   **Jack:** "You're attacking a 3000 ATK monster with a mole?"
*   **Tim:** "Mole Effect. Return Red Dragon to the Extra Deck."
*   **Jack:** "MY SOUL! YOU SENT MY SOUL BACK TO THE DECK!"
*   **Tim:** "Dig a hole, Jack."

**The Matchup: Crow vs. The Yellow Monarch**
*   **Crow:** "I'll swarm you with Blackwings!"
*   **Yellow Monarch:** "I summon **Caius**. Banish your Tuner."
*   **Crow:** "I Special Summon another!"
*   **Yellow Monarch:** "I Tribute Summon **Vanity's Fiend**. Special Summons are now illegal."
*   **Crow:** "But... my whole deck is Special Summons!"

**The Matchup: Yusei vs. You**
*   **Yusei:** "I finally cleared the Monarch. I summon **Shooting Star Dragon**! It attacks 5 times!"
*   **You:** "I activate the Trap **Gladiator Beast War Chariot**. Negate the effect and destroy it."
*   **Yusei:** "But... Shooting Star can negate destruction..."

### The Conclusion

**Team Tradition** wins the WRGP.
*   You didn't use "Accel Synchro."
*   You didn't use "Double Tuning."
*   You used **Floodgates** and **Removal**.

**The Legacy:**
The Iliaster Villains (Jose, Placido, Lucciano) are furious.
*   **Jose:** "We sent the Meklords to destroy Synchro Monsters... but they aren't playing Synchros!"

First, let’s establish the timeline so you know exactly how much "Get Off My Lawn" energy you have.

### 1. The Age Gap: How old are you?
*   **GX Era:** You were roughly 17-18 when you graduated.
*   **The Gap:** There is roughly a **10-12 year gap** between the end of GX and the start of 5D's (based on Trudge's age and Yusei's birth).
*   **Your Age:** You and Tim are roughly **28 to 30 years old**.

**The Vibe:**
To Yusei (18) and Jack (19), you guys are **Ancient**.
*   You remember a time before Synchros.
*   You remember when *Pot of Greed* was legal.
*   You have back pain.
*   You hate how loud the motorcycles are.

---

### 2. Renaming the "Yellow Monarch"
"Yellow" sounds weak in the gritty 5D's setting. "Gray Kingdom" is a great starting point because it implies **Neutrality** and **Age** (Gray hair, Gray morality).

Here are 3 "Color + Thing" options for your Past Self (the Monarch Player):

*   **Option A: The Grey Warden**
*   *Theme:* "Warden" implies a prison guard. Since the Monarch deck uses *Vanity's Fiend* to lock people out of Special Summoning, he is literally putting the opponent in jail.
*   *Vibe:* Cold, bureaucratic, unmovable.
*   **Option B: The Gilded Sovereign**
*   *Theme:* "Gilded" is a cooler, darker version of Gold/Yellow. It implies wealth but also something ancient and possibly fake/covered up.
*   *Vibe:* Arrogant, wealthy, "The Emperor" evolved.
*   **Option C: The Ivory Tyrant**
*   *Theme:* **Vanity's Fiend** and **Zaborg** are White/Pale. "Ivory" sounds expensive but brutal.
*   *Vibe:* Cruel perfection.

**My Recommendation:** **The Grey Warden.**
It fits the "Anti-Meta" playstyle perfectly. He stops people from playing. He is the Warden of the game.

---

### 3. The Team Roster: "Team Tradition" (The Old Guard)

**Player 1: Turtle Tim**
*   **Age:** 29.
*   **Deck:** **"Rock Stun" (Fossil Dyna)**.
*   **The Bike:** A heavy, 4-wheeled ATV. It doesn't go fast, it just blocks the lane.
*   **Playstyle:** "I Set 4 cards and summon a Rock. Try to pass me."

**Player 2: The Grey Warden (Your Past Self/Clone)**
*   **Age:** 29 (Physically).
*   **Deck:** **"Domain Monarchs" (No Extra Deck)**.
*   **The Bike:** A vintage, restored D-Wheel from the early days. Loud engine.
*   **Playstyle:** "I Tribute Summon *Vanity's Fiend*. Special Summons are illegal. Turn off your engine."

**Player 3: You (The Gatekeeper)**
*   **Age:** 29.
*   **Deck:** **"Trap Gladiators" (Gyzarus Control)**.
*   **The Bike:** The "Interceptor." Sleek, black, unmarked.
*   **Playstyle:** "You exhausted yourself trying to kill the first two guys. Now I'm going to tag out until you die."

---

### 4. The "Old Man" Trash Talk (The Tournament Arc)

**Scene: The Pits before the Race.**
**Team 5D's (Yusei/Jack/Crow)** are tuning their engines. They are young, spiky-haired, and energetic.
**Team Tradition** is drinking coffee. You are stretching your hamstrings.

**Crow:** "Hey old timers! Try not to break a hip on the curves!"
**Tim:** "Try not to Synchro Summon into a *Bottomless Trap Hole*, kid."

**The Race: Jack Atlas vs. The Grey Warden**
*   **Jack:** "I tune my monsters to summon **Red Dragon Archfiend**!"
*   **The Grey Warden:** "Tuning? How noisy. I Tribute Summon **Caius the Shadow Monarch**."
*   **Jack:** "A Level 6? It has only 2400 ATK! My Dragon has 3000!"
*   **The Grey Warden:** "ATK doesn't matter. **Caius Effect.** Banish the Dragon."
*   **Jack:** "WHAT?! You didn't even battle!"
*   **The Grey Warden:** "In my day, we removed threats *before* the Battle Phase."

**The Race: Akiza vs. Tim**
*   **Akiza:** "I will summon Black Rose Dragon and nuke the field!"
*   **Tim:** "I flip **Fossil Dyna Pachycephalo** face-up."
*   **Akiza:** "What does that fossil do?"
*   **Tim:** "It says: **Players cannot Special Summon.**"
*   **Akiza:** "But... my whole deck is Special Summons!"
*   **Tim:** "Sounds like a personal problem."

### 5. The Legacy
You guys are the villains of the WRGP, but the crowd loves it.
*   You represent the **"Boome r"** demographic.
*   Every time you shut down a 10-minute Synchro combo with a single Trap Card, the older audience members cheer.
*   You prove that **Fundamentals** (Card Advantage, Resource Management) beat **Flashy Mechanics** (Synchro/Accel Synchro).

**Team Tradition Status:**
*   **Result:** You probably lose in the Finals to Yusei (because he invents a new mechanic mid-duel like "Accel Synchro"), but you force him to struggle harder than he ever has.
*   **Retirement:** After the tournament, you guys open a "Dueling Dojo" in the City.

So lets paint a picture first of this new yugioh with xyz summon (its zexal right?)You are correct. The next era is **Yu-Gi-Oh! Zexal**.

If *GX* was a "Private Boarding School" and *5D's* was "Mad Max," *Zexal* is **"Augmented Reality Cyberpunk Utopia."**

It is bright, colorful, and feels like a perfect world on the surface, but underneath, it is an interdimensional war zone.

Here is the breakdown of the setting, economy, and mechanics to prepare for your next run.

### 1. The Setting: Heartland City
You are no longer on an island or in a slum. You are in **Heartland City**.
*   **The Vibe:** It looks like a futuristic theme park. Robots clean the streets. Everything is clean, green, and high-tech.
*   **The Tech (AR Dueling):** This is the massive shift.
*   **No More Solid Vision:** Duel Disks no longer project holograms into the real world.
*   **D-Gazers:** You must wear a special eyepiece (D-Gazer) to see the monsters.
*   **The Experience:** When you put on the D-Gazer, the city around you is digitally replaced by a "Duel Field." You duel in a virtual space layered over reality.
*   *Implication:* If you don't own a D-Gazer, you literally cannot see the game happening. You just see two people shouting at empty air.

### 2. The School: Heartland Academy
Yes, it is a school setting again, but it works differently than Duel Academy (GX).
*   **Day School:** It is not a boarding school. You go home to your parents/apartment at the end of the day.
*   **No Dorms/ranks:** There is no Slifer/Ra/Obelisk caste system. Everyone wears similar uniforms (though colors vary by grade/gender).
*   **The Curriculum:** It’s a normal Japanese high school (Math, Gym, History), but Dueling is a major social activity and school club.
*   *Note:* The skill level is generally **lower** than GX initially. The protagonist (Yuma) is terrible at the start.

### 3. The Economy: The "Numbers" Market
Heartland City is wealthy. Buying a pack of cards is easy for a normal citizen. You won't be starving here.

**The Real Economy: "Number" Cards**
The currency of power in this era is the **"Number" (No.)** cards.
*   **What they are:** 100 specific Xyz Monster cards scattered across the city.
*   **The Gimmick:**
1.  **They are Rare:** Only one of each exists.
2.  **They Possess You:** If a normal person picks up a "Number," it amplifies their darkest desires. A nice florist finds a Number? She becomes a psychopath who wants to destroy the city with plants.
3.  **Battle Protection:** In the anime, **"Only a Number can destroy a Number."** If you play a normal deck, you literally cannot kill their boss monster by battle.

**Your Career Path:** **"Number Hunter."**
There are factions (Kite Tenjo, the Arclight Family, the Barians) hunting these cards. If you have one, you are a target. If you hunt them, you gain immense power.

### 4. The Mechanic: Xyz Summoning (Exceed)
This mechanic kills "Levels" and introduces "Ranks."
*   **The Card Frame:** Black with stars flying from left to right.
*   **The Summon:** You take 2 (or more) monsters of the **SAME Level**.
*   Example: Two Level 4 Monsters.
*   You stack them on top of each other (Overlay).
*   You put the Xyz Monster on top.
*   **The Cost:** You don't "spend" the materials. They sit under the card as **Overlay Units (Materials)**.
*   To use an effect, you "detach" (send to grave) one unit.
*   *Resource Management:* You have limited ammo. Once you run out of units, the monster is usually vanilla.

### 5. The "Rags to Riches" Potential
Since money isn't the issue here, the "Rags" element comes from **Skill** and **Possession**.

**The Setup for You:**
*   You are a **"Number Hunter" Mercenary.**
*   You don't care about saving the Astral World or the Barian World.
*   You hunt people possessed by Numbers, defeat them, steal the Number card, and either use it or sell it to the highest bidder (like Mr. Heartland).

**The Challenge:**
*   You start with **Zero Numbers**.
*   Your opponents have Indestructible Boss Monsters.
*   You need a deck that can defeat "Numbers" without using "Numbers" (which is technically impossible in lore, unless you use effect removal).

**Are you ready to hunt ghosts in the Augmented Reality?**ok lets say I'm a normal child, my dad works at like a salaryman or something. I do okay at the school, but I'm obsessed at collecting cards. One day I found a xyz card and I became a number hunter (is it possible if I don't steal the main npcs/main character archetype card?) This is a fantastic setup. The "Normal Kid with a Dark Obsession" trope fits the *Zexal* vibe perfectly. Unlike Yuma (who wants to be friends) or Kite (who wants to save his brother), you just want to **Collect**.

**Is it possible without stealing main character cards?**
**Yes.**
There are 100 "Numbers." In the anime, only about 30-40 are held by key characters. The rest are held by randoms, dogs, soccer players, or criminals. There is plenty of loot for you to hunt.

Here is your **Year 1 Profile** as the **"The Collector."**

### 1. The Origin: "The Loose Number"
You are a normal middle schooler. Your dad buys you a few packs on his way home from work.
You open a pack. The lights in your room flicker.
You pull a card that isn't on the set list. The text glows purple.

**The Card:** **Number 50: Blackship of Corn**
*   **Rank:** 4 (Generic - requires any 2 Level 4 monsters).
*   **The Lore:** It’s a comedic but terrifying card. It looks like a giant pirate ship made of corn, but it sends opponents to the graveyard.
*   **The Effect:** Once per turn, you can detach a material to target a monster with ATK lower than this card's ATK; **send it to the Graveyard** (bypassing destruction immunity) and **inflict 1000 damage**.
*   **Why it fits you:** It deals **Burn Damage** (1000). It connects your soul to your past life as the Red Bandit.

**Why you aren't possessed:**
Usually, Numbers possess people by amplifying their desires.
*   Most victims desire "Revenge" or "Power," which makes them go crazy.
*   Your desire is **"Collection."**
*   You don't want to destroy the world; you just want to put the card in a sleeve and bind it. Your obsession with *owning* the card overpowers the card's will to control you. You are the Master because you are the Owner.

### 2. The Deck: "Wind-Ups" (The Toy Collector)
You need a deck that is **Generic**, **Strong**, and **Thematic**.
Since you are a kid who likes collecting, you play **Wind-Ups (Zenmai)**.

*   **The Theme:** They look like little tin toys (Rabbit, Shark, Magician).
*   **Anime Status:** Used briefly by a minor character, but largely ignored.
*   **Real World Status:** **Tier 0.** This deck was so broken it got hit by the banlist multiple times.
*   **The Playstyle:** **Hand Looping** and **Rank 4/Rank 5 Spam**.

**The Deck List (The "Toy Box"):**
*   **3x Wind-Up Shark:** (Level 4, can become Level 3 or 5). The engine.
*   **3x Wind-Up Magician:** (Summons another Wind-Up from deck).
*   **3x Wind-Up Rat:** (Revives from Grave).
*   **3x Wind-Up Rabbit:** (Dodges effects by banishing itself).
*   **3x Tour Guide from the Underworld:** (Summons a Fiend... wait, maybe stick to pure Wind-Ups to be budget-friendly).

**Why it hunts Numbers:**
*   Numbers are protected by "Cannot be destroyed by battle except by a Number."
*   **Wind-Ups** don't care about battle. They spam Level 3, 4, and 5 monsters to Xyz Summon your **Blackship of Corn** instantly.

### 3. The "Hunter" Life: How it works
You are a middle schooler by day, Hunter by night.

**The AR (Augmented Reality) Advantage:**
*   You put on your D-Gazer.
*   You scan the city.
*   **Numbers emit a signal.** If you see a weird purple aura around a duelist, they have a Number.

**The Hunt:**
You find a kid in the park who has **Number 34: Terror-Byte** (Computer Bug). He is hacking vending machines.
*   **The Challenge:** "I challenge you. Ante rule. Winner takes the rarest card."
*   **The Duel:**
1.  He summons *Terror-Byte*. "You can't destroy me! Only a Number can kill a Number!"
2.  You summon **Wind-Up Shark** and **Wind-Up Magician**.
3.  Overlay for **Number 50: Blackship of Corn**.
4.  **You:** "I activate Blackship. Send your Terror-Byte to the Graveyard. Take 1000 damage."
5.  **Him:** "Wait, you have a Number too?!"
6.  **You:** "Direct Attack."

**The Prize:**
You win. You walk over to him. You take the card.
*   In the anime, Astral absorbs the Number.
*   For you, **you just put it in your pocket.**
*   **Your Collection:** 2 / 100.

### 4. The Economy: Selling Doubles
Since you are a "Collector," you know the value of cards.
*   Sometimes you hunt a criminal who doesn't have a Number, but has a rare card (like *Effect Veiler* or *Maxx "C"*).
*   You win. You take the card.
*   You sell it to a card shop for Yen.
*   **Lifestyle:** You aren't rich, but you have the best card sleeves and a custom D-Gazer. You are the cool kid on the playground who has "Foreign Imports."

### 5. The Threat: Kite Tenjo
There is one problem.
There is another Hunter. **Kite Tenjo** (Kaito).
*   He steals souls (Photon Hand).
*   He hunts Numbers to save his brother.
*   **If he sees you:** He will try to take your Number.

**Your Strategy:**
You are a **Stealth Hunter**.
*   You don't hunt when Kite is active (Night).
*   You hunt during the day or in the suburbs.
*   You keep your Number faced-down in your binder. You are an NPC. You blend in.

**Current Status:**
*   **Role:** Student / Secret Hunter.
*   **Deck:** Wind-Up Xyz.
*   **Ace:** Number 50: Blackship of Corn.
*   **Goal:** "Gotta Catch 'Em All."wait wait wait, wind up? the broken deck with infinite resource? ok lets nerf myself more, I can't have a T0 deck just like that.
