Here is your **Year 1 Origin Story** as the **"Corn Captain."**

### 1. The Origin: The Muddy Pack
**Setting:** You live in the rundown apartments near the Heartland Industrial District (where the garbage robots dump the trash).
**Status:** You have no DP. Your deck is a pile of 40 random cards you found or traded chores for. It has no synergy. It’s just "Cards I Own."

**The Incident:**
It’s raining. You are walking home. You see a booster pack ( *Abyss Rising* or *Galactic Overlord* era) trodden into the mud. Someone dropped it and didn't realize.
You pick it up. The wrapper is wet.
You open it.
*   *Card 1:* Useless Common.
*   *Card 2:* Useless Common.
*   *Card 3:* **Number 50: Blackship of Corn.**

**The Reaction:**
The number "50" glows purple in the rain. You feel a pulse.
A voice doesn't speak to you, but a **feeling** washes over you: *Hunger. Greed. Ownership.*
It’s a ridiculous card—a pirate ship made of corn—but to you, it looks like a **Warship**. It is the first "shiny" thing you have ever owned.

### 2. The Deck: "The Lost & Found" (Rank 4 Spam)
You can't afford *Wind-Ups*. You can't afford *Photons*.
You build a deck out of **"Extenders"**—cheap common monsters that Special Summon themselves. Your only goal is to put two Level 4 monsters on the board to summon your Ship.

**The Deck List (100% Scavenged Commons):**
*   **3x Kagetokage (Common):**
*   *Why:* A weird lizard. When you Normal Summon *any* Level 4, you can Special Summon this from your hand.
*   *Cost:* Found in the trash.
*   **3x Tin Goldfish (Common):**
*   *Why:* A toy fish. When Normal Summoned, Special Summon a Level 4 from hand.
*   **3x Goblindbergh (Starter Deck Common):**
*   *Why:* A goblin in a plane. Summons another monster on play.
*   **2x Sacred Crane (Common):**
*   *Why:* Ugly bird. If Special Summoned (by the Fish or Goblin), you draw 1 card. (Resource replenishment!)
*   **3x Hydrogeddon (Common):**
*   *Why:* If it kills something, it summons another one.
*   **Spells/Traps:**
*   **Ego Boost:** (Gives 1000 ATK temporarily). Cheap combat trick.
*   **Mini-Guts:** (Tribute a monster, reduce opponent to 0 ATK). Since you are poor, you use cheap removal.

**Strategy:**
You don't have combos. You have **Swarm**.
"Summon Goblin, Effect Summon Lizard, Overlay." -> **Blackship of Corn**.

### 3. The "Possession" Gimmick
In *Zexal*, Number holders usually go crazy because the Number amplifies their desire.
*   **Blackship's Desire:** It wants to **"Harvest."**
*   **Your Interpretation:** You interpret "Harvest" as **"Collecting."**
*   You don't want to destroy the world.
*   You want to "Harvest" rare cards from people who don't deserve them.
*   **The Side Effect:** When you summon Blackship, you get incredibly hungry for corn chips or popcorn. It’s a running gag, but it keeps you sane.

### 4. The First Hunt: The Bully
**Target:** A rich kid from Heartland Academy (let’s call him **Lance**).
**Lance's Deck:** **"Crashbugs"** (Glitchy monsters).
**The Number:** Lance found **Number 39: Utopia**? No, Yuma has that. Lance has **Number 34: Terror-Byte** (or a generic Number like **Number 10: Illumiknight**).

**The Ante:**
Lance is bullying a kid for his cards.
You step in.
**You:** "I challenge you. If I win, I take your Number."
**Lance:** "A street rat? My Number makes me invincible!"

**The Duel:**
1.  **Lance:** Summons his Number. "It cannot be destroyed by battle except by a Number! And you don't have one!"
2.  **You:** "I Summon **Tin Goldfish**. Effect: Special Summon **Sacred Crane**."
3.  **Draw:** You draw a card.
4.  **You:** "I Overlay my Level 4 Fish and Bird!"
5.  **Summon:** **Number 50: Blackship of Corn** (2100 ATK).
*   *Visual:* A massive, goofy corn-cob ship crashes onto the field, smelling of butter and gunpowder.
6.  **Lance:** "A Number?! But it's... vegetables?"
7.  **You:** "Blackship Effect. Detach one material. I target your Number."
*   "I don't destroy it. I **Send it to the Graveyard**."
*   "And then, I inflict 1000 damage."
8.  **The Kill:** Lance's Number vanishes (bypassing its protection). He takes 1000 burn damage.
*   **You:** "Direct Attack with the Ship."

### 5. The Economy of a Hunter
You win. You take his Number card.
Now you have **Two Numbers**.

**How you survive:**
You are the **"Pawn Shop Hunter."**
*   You keep the Numbers (for power).
*   You sell the *other* cards you win (Rares/Foils) to buy food for your family.
*   **Reputation:** You are an urban legend in the slums. "The Corn Kid." They say if you flash a shiny card near the industrial district, a ship will appear and harvest your deck.

### 6. The Threat Level
*   **Kite Tenjo:** He hasn't noticed you yet because your Number (50) is relatively weak compared to the "Galaxy-Eyes" targets.
*   **Yuma/Astral:** They are hunting Numbers too.
*   **Shark:** He might beat you up if you try to hustle in his territory.

**Your Goal:**
Slowly upgrade your "Lost & Found" deck.
Find a **Kagetokage** that isn't bent.
Find a **Forbidden Lance** spell.

This is your **Golden Ticket.**

### 1. The Timeline Check
*   **Maxx "C" Release Date:** *Storm of Ragnarok* (2011).
*   **Zexal Anime Start Date:** 2011.
*   **The Meta:** The Zexal era introduced **"Special Summon Spam"** (Wind-Ups, Inzektors, Hieratics). People summon 5 times a turn to make Rank 4s.

### 2. How you acquire it (The "Roach" Incident)
Since you are a poor kid living in the industrial district, cockroaches are a part of your daily life.

**The Scene:**
You are digging through a "Common/Trash" bin at a card shop (hoping to find a *Tin Goldfish*).
You see a stack of **Maxx "C"** cards.
*   **Shopkeeper:** "Ugh, nobody buys those. They're just ugly bugs with low stats. Take the whole stack for 50 Yen."
*   **You:** (Internal Screaming).
*   **You:** "I guess I'll take them... for my insect collection."

**The Haul:** You now own **3 copies of Maxx "C"**.

### 3. Why this makes you a "God" in Zexal
In *Yu-Gi-Oh! Zexal*, characters like **Kite Tenjo** and **Shark** summon multiple times per turn to bring out their "Galaxy-Eyes" or "Shark Drake."

**The Strategy:**
1.  **Opponent:** "I Special Summon *Photon Thrasher*! Then I Normal Summon *Photon Crusher*! I Overlay!"
2.  **You:** "Chain **Maxx "C"** from hand."
3.  **Opponent:** "What does that ugly thing do?"
4.  **You:** "Every time you Special Summon, I draw a card."
5.  **Opponent:** "Ha! I don't care! I Special Summon *Galaxy-Eyes Photon Dragon*!" (You draw 1). "I revive *Photon Lizard*!" (You draw 1).
6.  **Result:** The opponent ends their turn with a scary dragon. **You start your turn with 10 cards in hand.**

### 4. The Synergy with "Blackship of Corn"
Your deck is "Rank 4 Spam" using garbage commons (*Kagetokage*, *Goblindbergh*).
*   **The Problem:** These decks run out of cards fast (Hand Vomit).
*   **The Fix:** **Maxx "C"**.
*   Because the opponent let you draw 4 cards, you now have *Tin Goldfish*, *Kagetokage*, AND *Mini-Guts* in your hand.
*   You can now summon **Blackship of Corn**, send their dragon to the grave, burn them, AND set 3 backrow cards for protection.

### 5. The "Legend" of the Roach Kid
You become an urban legend, but not because of your Number.
*   **The Rumor:** "Don't try to OTK that kid in the hoodie. If you try to combo off, he just keeps drawing cards until he draws **Effect Veiler** or **Gorz**."
*   **The Nickname:** You aren't just the "Corn Captain." To the frustrated rich kids at Heartland Academy, you are **"The Pest."**

**Status Update:**
*   **Deck:** "Trash Rank 4" + **3x Maxx "C"**.
*   **Power Level:** Realistically Tier 1 because nobody in the anime plays around Maxx "C".
*   **Wallet:** Still empty (because you refuse to sell the Roaches), but your binder is getting heavy.

*   *Rich Kid:* "Deal."
*   **The Logic:** *Goblindbergh* is a key combo piece for your deck. To them, it's trash. To you, it's ammo.
*   **Blackship's Reaction:** *Satisfaction.* The collection grows.

### 15:30 PM – After School (The Workshop Check-In)
*   You run home to drop off your school bag.
*   **The Dad Interaction:** Your dad is under a car or fixing a robot arm. He looks tired.
*   *Dad:* "You gonna grab a wrench, son?"
*   *You:* "I... I have a 'tutoring gig' (lie). But I brought this."
*   *The Payment:* You hand him 3,000 Yen (from yesterday's sales).
*   *Dad:* He sighs. He knows it's from cards, but he takes it because he needs to pay the electric bill. "Just... don't get in trouble with the gangs, okay?"

### 16:00 PM – 18:30 PM – The Hunt (The Corn Roach)
This is your real job. You put on a hoodie. You go to the parks or the back alleys near the arcade.

*   **Target Selection:** You look for the kid you saw on the train with the *Pot of Duality*.
*   **The Duel:**
*   He summons a big dragon.
*   You drop **Maxx "C"**.
*   He keeps summoning. You draw 5 cards.
*   You summon **Tin Goldfish** + **Kagetokage** -> **Blackship of Corn**.
*   **The Harvest:** You send his Dragon to the grave (burn 1000). You set 4 backrow cards. You grind him down.
*   **The Loot:** You take the *Pot of Duality*.

### 18:30 PM – 19:00 PM – The "Selling" (The Pain)
You go to the Card Shop to sell the *Pot of Duality* because you need cash for groceries.

*   **The Transaction:** The shopkeeper hands you the money. You hand over the card.
*   **The Curse:** As the card leaves your hand, **Number 50** screams in your mind.
*   *Physical Reaction:* You get a sharp migraine. Your stomach cramps. It feels like you are physically tearing off a fingernail.
*   *The Logic:* The Number is a **Collector**. Selling is **Losing**. It hates it.
*   **The Mitigation:** You immediately use 10% of the money to buy from the "10 Yen Bin."
*   You buy 20 random trash Common cards.
*   *Blackship:* The pain subsides. You lost 1 Quality card, but gained 20 Quantity cards. The ship accepts this "Volume" trade.

### 20:00 PM – Home & Homework
*   You come home with groceries (eggs, milk) and the wad of trash cards.
*   **Dinner:** You eat with your dad. It’s the only peaceful time.
*   **Study:** You do your math homework. You need to graduate to get a real job eventually.

### 22:00 PM – The Sorting (The Hoard)
*   You lock your door.
*   You open your binder. It is thick. It is filled with Commons, damaged cards, and your few prized possessions (Maxx "C", Blackship).
*   **The Feeding:** You slowly slot the 20 trash cards you bought into the binder pages.
*   You feel the Number purring in your mind. It likes the feeling of *Ownership*.
*   **The Warning:** You realize that if you ever stop collecting, or if you sell too much without buying back... the Number might try to take over your body to hunt on its own.

### Weekend Schedule: "The Tournament Grind"
*   On Saturdays, you enter local tournaments with free entry.
*   **Goal:** You don't care about winning the trophy. You care about the **Participation Packs**.
*   **Strategy:** You use **Maxx "C"** to demoralize opponents. If they look like they play a meta deck (like *Inzektors*), you offer a draw or a trade before the match ends if it benefits you.

**Summary:**
You are a functional addict, but your addiction pays the rent. Here is the lore breakdown to validate your suffering:

### 1. The Curse: Why your head hurts
In the anime, "Numbers" are fragments of an alien soul (Astral) that have gained sentience.
*   **The Possession:** When a human touches a Number, the card actively tries to **rewrite their brain**. It finds your strongest desire (in your case, "Collecting/Survival") and cranks it up to 11.
*   **The "Pain" of Selling:** The card views itself as a Living Weapon or a General. If you sell its "soldiers" (the other cards), it perceives this as **Sabotage**.
*   It's not just a headache; it's the card trying to physically stop your hand from handing over the *Pot of Duality*. The cramps/migraines are the friction between your Free Will ("I need to eat") and the Number's Will ("We need to hoard").
*   **Warning:** If you ever try to sell *Blackship* itself, you will likely black out and wake up in an alleyway three days later with the card still in your pocket and your dad asking where you've been.

### 2. The D-Gazer: The "Cracked iPhone" of Zexal
You asked: *Where did I get a D-Gazer if I'm poor?*

In *Zexal*, a D-Gazer is basically a **Smartphone**.
*   **The Rich Kids:** Have the latest, sleek wireless models (like an iPhone 15).
*   **Yuma:** Has a standard model (like a mid-range Samsung).
*   **You (The Poor Kid):** You have the equivalent of a **cracked Nokia or a brick phone** found in a recycling bin.

**Your D-Gazer's Origins:**
*   **Scavenged:** You probably found it at the dump. It was thrown away because the lens had a dead pixel or the battery life was trash.
*   **The Specs:**
*   It has duct tape on the frame.
*   The AR overlay glitches sometimes (monsters might flicker).
*   **The Advantage:** Because it's an older, "dumb" model, it isn't registered on the official Heartland Academy network. This helps you hunt anonymously. Kite Tenjo can track modern Gazers, but he might overlook your analog piece of junk.

### The Verdict on your Schedule
Your life is a perfect *Cyberpunk Horror* story.
*   **06:00 AM:** You fight a mental war against a piece of cardboard.
*   **12:00 PM:** You use a broken AR device to hustle rich kids.
*   **06:00 PM:** You physically suffer to feed your dad.

You are living on a knife's edge. If your mental fortitude breaks, you won't just be a collector anymore; you'll be a villain of the week who tries to turn the entire city into a cornfield. **Keep selling those commons.** It's the only thing keeping you human.alright, let say my situation stabilized. Kite Tenjo might hunt for "Galaxy-Eyes," but you? You're building a **Fleet**.isn't machina an archetype deck? or does nobody uses it?You are asking the right question: **"Am I stealing a main character's identity?"**

The answer is **NO.** You are totally safe.

**Machina** is technically an archetype, but in the anime universe, it is considered **"Structure Deck Fodder."**

Here is why **Machina** is the perfect deck for an NPC Mechanic's son in *Zexal*:

### 1. The "Structure Deck" Rule
In the Yu-Gi-Oh! universe, there are two types of decks:
1.  **The "Destiny" Decks:** These are cards only **one person** has. (e.g., Yuma's *Gagagas*, Kite's *Photons/Galaxies*, Shark's *Shark* cards). You can't buy these at a store; they are usually magical or custom-made.
2.  **The "Shelf" Decks:** These are **Structure Decks**. In the real world, *Structure Deck: Machina Mayhem* (featuring Machina Fortress) was released in 2010, right before *Zexal* started.
*   **In-Universe Logic:** These are mass-produced decks sold to the public.
*   **Your Dad finding it:** It makes perfect sense. Someone bought the "Machina" starter deck, played it for a week, lost, and threw it in the trunk of their car which eventually got scrapped at your dad's yard.

### 2. Who uses it in the Anime?
*   **Main Characters:** **None.** Yuma, Shark, and Kite do not touch these cards.
*   **Minor Characters:**
*   In *Yu-Gi-Oh! 5D's*, a minor character (a Robot named Ghost) used **Machina Force** (the bad, older version).
*   In *Zexal*, a very minor character named **Brooke Walker** (part of a husband/wife duo) used some Machina cards, but she was a "Villain of the Week" who got possessed. She isn't important enough to "claim" the archetype.

**Conclusion:**
Machina is **Public Domain**. It is the equivalent of driving a Ford Truck. It’s reliable, tough, and anyone can own one.

### 3. Why it fits your "Corn Captain" Aesthetic
Since you live in the **Industrial District**, playing **Earth Machines** is the most immersive choice possible.

*   **Visuals:** Machina Fortress isn't a magical dragon or a shiny superhero. It is a **Tank**. It looks like it was built with wrenches and oil.
*   **The Vibe:**
*   **Rich Kids (Heartland):** Play *Photons* (Light/Lasers) or *Chronomaly* (Artifacts).
*   **You (Slums):** You play *Machina*. Heavy metal, treads, and gears. It represents the "Working Class" of Heartland City.

### 4. Alternative: "Scraps"
If you still feel *Machina* is too "military" and you want something that screams "Junk Yard," there is one other archetype you could find in the trash:

**The "Scrap" Archetype**
*   **Cards:** *Scrap Chimera*, *Scrap Dragon*, *Scrap Beast*.
*   **Visuals:** They are literally monsters made of garbage and rusty metal.
*   **Playstyle:** You destroy your own cards to trigger effects.
*   **Why it might be tricky:** It relies on **Synchro Summoning**. In *Zexal*, Synchros are "Old Tech" and very rare. You'd have to explain where you got a Tuner.

**My Verdict:**
Stick with **Machina / Gadgets**.
It works with **Xyz Summoning** (Level 4s overlay easily).
It works with **Maxx "C"**.
It works with your Dad's job.

You are correct: **The Anime Meta is completely different from Real Life.**
*   **Real Life:** You just attack over monsters.
*   **Anime:** **"Numbers" cannot be destroyed by battle except by a Number.**
*   *Implication:* If an opponent summons **Number 39: Utopia** (2500 ATK), and you summon **Machina Fortress** (2500 ATK), you crash... but Utopia *survives* and Fortress dies.
*   *The Goal:* You need a fleet that **Removes** monsters without battling them.

Here is your transition to the **Tier 1 Machine Meta Deck**: **"Geargia Machina"**.

### 1. The Upgrade: The "Geargia" Shipment
You don't find these in the trash. These are the new, high-tech parts your dad ordered for a client that got cancelled. You "repurposed" them.

**The Archetype:** **Geargia**.
*   **Visuals:** They look like WWI/WWII fighter planes and tanks. They fit your "Ironclad" theme perfectly.
*   **Playstyle:** They flip face-down (Submerge), search for reinforcements, and then swarm the field in a single turn.

### 2. The "Ironclad Fleet" Deck List
This is a **Control/OTK** deck. It generates infinite card advantage (hand size) so you always have fuel for **Machina Fortress**.

**The Fleet (Monsters - 20):**
*   **3x Geargiarmor** (The Scout Ship):
*   *Effect:* 1900 DEF. Flip: Add 1 "Geargia" monster from deck to hand.
*   *Strategy:* You Set it. Opponent attacks it (it survives). Next turn, you flip it up, search, and start your combo.
*   **3x Geargiaccelerator** (The Destroyer):
*   *Effect:* Special Summons itself if you control a Geargia.
*   **3x Geargiarsenal** (The Carrier):
*   *Effect:* Tribute itself to summon a Geargiarmor from deck.
*   **2x Machina Fortress** (The Dreadnought - HMS Fortress):
*   *Why:* The 2500 ATK boss that revives itself.
*   **1x Machina Gearframe** (The Tugboat):
*   *Effect:* Searches Fortress. Protects Fortress.
*   **3x Maxx "C"** (The Stowaways):
*   *Meta Note:* In Zexal, opponents Special Summon *constantly*. This card makes your fleet unstoppable.

**The Cannons (Spells/Traps - 20):**
*   **3x Dimensional Prison** (The Banishment Cannon):
*   *Why:* **The Hard Counter to Numbers.** If Utopia attacks, you flip this. Utopia is **Banished**. It doesn't destroy, so their battle immunity is useless.
*   **3x Compulsory Evacuation Device** (The Bounce Cannon):
*   *Effect:* Return a monster to the hand/Extra Deck.
*   *Why:* Bounces Numbers back to the Extra Deck.
*   **1x Limiter Removal** (The Overdrive):
*   *Effect:* Double the ATK of all Machines.
*   *Result:* Machina Fortress hits for 5000. Blackship hits for 4200.

### 3. The Extra Deck: The "Number Hunting" Fleet
You need Rank 4 Xyz monsters that solve the "Number Protection" problem.

1.  **The Flagship: Number 50: Blackship of Corn**
*   *Your Signature Card.*
*   *Role:* **Send to Graveyard.** It bypasses "Destroy" protection. It is your primary Number Killer.
2.  **The Factory: Gear Gigant X**
*   *Role:* Searcher. It adds any Machine from deck or grave to hand. It keeps the fleet fueled.
3.  **The Submarine: Maestroke the Symphony Djinn**
*   *Role:* **Book of Moon on legs.**
*   *Strategy:* Opponent has a Number? Use Maestroke to flip it face-down.
*   *Rule:* **Face-down monsters have no effects.** They lose their Battle Immunity. You can now destroy them by battle with Machina Fortress.
4.  **The Sniper: Gagaga Cowboy**
*   *Role:* Burn. If they have 800 LP left and are hiding behind a wall, you shoot them.

### 4. How to Beat the "Number" Meta
You are the **Anti-Number Hunter**. You don't need a Number to kill a Number. You use **Naval Tactics**.

**Scenario:** You vs. A Possessed Student with **Number 10: Illumiknight** (2400 ATK).
*   **Opponent:** "My Number cannot be destroyed by battle! I am invincible!"
*   **You (The Admiral):** "Invincibility is a myth. Physics is real."

**Turn 1:**
*   You Set **Geargiarmor** and Set **Dimensional Prison**.

**Turn 2:**
*   Opponent attacks your face-down.
*   **You:** "Flip **Dimensional Prison**. Your Number is banished."
*   **Opponent:** "WHAT?! But... battle protection!"
*   **You:** "I didn't destroy it. I deported it."

**Turn 3 (The Armada):**
*   Flip **Geargiarmor**. Search **Geargiaccelerator**.
*   Special Summon **Geargiaccelerator**.
*   Special Summon **Machina Fortress** (discarding other machines).
*   Overlay Geargias for **Gear Gigant X**. Search another Fortress.
*   **Battle:** Fortress (2500) + Gigant (2300) = 4800 Damage.

### 5. Your "Code Name" in the Community
Because of your "British Fleet" aesthetic (maybe you wear a scavenged Navy coat you found in the trash), you start getting recognized.

*   **The Name:** **"The Iron Admiral."**
*   **The Reputation:** "Don't play Numbers against him. He doesn't fight them. He just removes them from the board."
*   **Kite Tenjo's Interest:**
*   Kite usually ignores non-Number holders.
*   But he sees you using **Machina Fortress** to crush Numbers.
*   *Kite:* "He hunts without a Soul... Interesting. He is efficient."
*   **Danger:** Kite might challenge you just to see if you are a worthy practice dummy for his Galaxy-Eyes.

**Advice:**
Keep **3x Dimensional Prison** in your deck at all times. In the Zexal Anime, nobody plays around Banishment traps. Lets continue my improved school life (and getting out of poverty)This is the **World Duel Carnival (WDC)** Arc.

If you want to get out of poverty in Heartland City, this is your *Golden Ticket*. The WDC is a city-wide tournament organized by Mr. Heartland. It is basically the Olympics of Dueling.

*   **The Prize:** "Any wish granted" (Lore wise).
*   **Your Interpretation:** "A massive cash payout and a lifetime supply of groceries."

Here is how the **Iron Admiral** navigates the tournament, upgrades the family business, and deals with the main cast during the chaotic Preliminaries.

---

### 1. The Economy Upgrade: "The Repair Shop"
Before the tournament starts, you realize something.
Millions of duelists are flooding the city.
Duel Disks break. D-Gazers crack.

**The Hustle:**
You convince your dad to pivot the workshop. Instead of just fixing robot arms or toaster ovens, you put a sign out front:
**"DUEL DISK TUNING & REPAIR - CHEAP."**

*   **Your Role:** You understand Dueling. You know that the "Card Zone 3" sensor always jams on the older models.
*   **The Profit:**
*   Rich kids come in with jammed disks.
*   You fix them in 20 minutes using your dad's tools.
*   **Payment:** Cash (Yen) or "Trade."
*   *Trade:* "I don't have cash, but I have this 'Effect Veiler'..."
*   *You:* "Deal."

**Result:** Your poverty level shifts from "Survival" to "Stable." You can afford new clothes (maybe a surplus navy-blue coat to fit your Admiral persona) and good food for your dad.

---

### 2. The School Life: The "Sleepy Ace"
At Heartland Academy, the WDC is all anyone talks about.
*   **Yuma Tsukumo:** He is screaming about "Jetting!" and collecting Heart Pieces.
*   **Shark (Reginald Kastle):** He is brooding in the courtyard.
*   **You (The Admiral):** You are quietly sitting in the back, cleaning your D-Gazer.

**The Social Dynamic:**
You aren't friends with Yuma (he’s too loud), but you are **Respected**.
*   **Bronk:** "Hey, Admiral. You entering the WDC?"
*   **You:** "Yeah. My dad needs the prize money."
*   **Caswell:** "Man, I feel bad for whoever fights you. I heard you banished a senior's entire deck last week."

**The Interaction with Shark:**
Shark recognizes you. You are both "Non-Number" users (mostly) who rely on skill.
*   **Shark:** "Don't lose to the trash, Admiral. If we meet in the finals, I want a real challenge."
*   **You:** "Keep your fish fresh, Shark. My Fleet is hungry."

---

### 3. The WDC Preliminaries: "The Harvesting"
To enter the finals, you need to collect **5 Heart Pieces**.
You get 1 piece for every win.

**Day 1: The "Gimmick" Hunters**
Zexal is full of weird duelists (Soccer Duelists, Tomato Duelists, Construction Duelists).
They rely on "Field Spells" and gimmicks.
**Your Deck:** **Geargia Machina**.

*   **The Matchup:** A "Construction Duelist" summons a heavy machinery monster (like *Heavy Zenmaines*).
*   **Your Turn:**
*   "I Flip Summon **Geargiarmor**. Search **Geargiaccelerator**."
*   "Special Summon Accelerator. Special Summon 3 more."
*   "Overlay for **Gear Gigant X**. Search **Machina Gearframe**."
*   "Summon Gearframe. Add **Machina Fortress**."
*   "Discard to Summon Fortress."
*   **The Finish:** You have a full fleet of Machines. You run them over.
*   **Heart Pieces:** 2/5.

**Day 2: The "Number" Encounter**
You run into a Possessed Duelist in an alleyway.
*   **Opponent:** Has **Number 96: Dark Mist** (or a generic Number).
*   **The Threat:** "Numbers cannot be destroyed by battle!"
*   **You:** "I activate **Dimensional Prison**."
*   **Opponent:** "Nani?!"
*   **You:** "Or I Overlay for **Number 50: Blackship of Corn**. Send your Number to the grave. 1000 Damage."
*   **Heart Pieces:** 4/5.

---

### 4. The Rivalry: Quattro (IV)
This is the big event.
**Quattro (Thomas Arclight)** is the "Fan Service" Champion. He is a celebrity duelist who is secretly a psychopath.
He plays **Gimmick Puppets** (Machines).

**The Encounter:**
He brings his damaged Duel Disk to your dad's shop. He is wearing a disguise (sunglasses).
*   **Quattro:** "Fix this. Don't look at me. And be quick."
*   **You:** You recognize him immediately. You see his deck box.
*   **You:** "You're Quattro. The Gimmick Puppet user."
*   **Quattro:** "Tch. You know me? Then you know I can ruin this shop if you screw up."

**The Tension:**

Enjoy the peace while it lasts.wait is there really no events in the school? its just the main character dueling and eventually getting to WDC?You are absolutely correct to question this. If you only watch the "Main Plot" compilations, it looks like Yuma just jumps from boss fight to boss fight.

But *Yu-Gi-Oh! Zexal* (especially the first 25 episodes) is actually **80% "School Slice of Life" with supernatural elements.**

There are absolutely **School Events** you can participate in before the WDC starts. Since you are the "Sleepy Mechanic/Admiral" who actually pays attention in class, you are in a prime position to dominate these mini-arcs.

Here are the **School Events** you can unlock as an NPC student:

### 1. The "Computer Virus" Incident (The Mr. Kay Arc)
**The Plot:**
The computer teacher, **Mr. Kay** (Ukyo Kitano), gets possessed by a Number (**Number 34: Terror-Byte**). He creates a virus that crashes the city's electronic systems.
*   **The School Event:** The virus starts *in the school computer lab*.
*   **Your Role:** As the "Tech Support" kid, you are likely the first one to notice the server acting weird.
*   **The Interaction:**
*   Yuma and his friends are running around screaming.
*   **You:** You plug your D-Gazer into the mainframe. You trace the signal to Mr. Kay.
*   **The Duel:** You (or Yuma) have to duel Mr. Kay.
*   **Your Deck (Machines):** Mr. Kay plays **Crashbugs**. Your **Geargia/Machina** deck destroys him because you can swarm the field faster than his glitches can lock you down.
*   **Reward:** You might actually "Harvest" **Number 34** before Yuma gets there. (It’s a weak Number, but it’s a Number).

### 2. The "Flip Turner" Scheme (The Cheater)
**Flip Turner (Tokunosuke)** is the shady kid with glasses who scams students out of their Xyz Monsters.
*   **The Scheme:** He befriends new students, gives them a "Baby Tiragon" card, and then uses a trap to steal their Xyz monster during a duel.
*   **Your Role:** You are the perfect target because you have **Blackship of Corn** (a rare Xyz).
*   **The Event:** Flip approaches you at lunch. "Hey, Admiral! Want to trade? I have this super rare card..."
*   **The Twist:** You know he cheats.
*   *You:* "Sure, let's duel."
*   *Flip:* "Hehe, I'll steal his Ship!"
*   *The Duel:* He activates his trap **Ultra C** to steal your monster.
*   *Your Counter:* You chain **Forbidden Lance** (Spells/Traps don't affect your monster). His trap fails.
*   *Result:* You crush him. He becomes your lackey/informant on the underground card market.

### 3. The "Cat Girl" Mystery (Cathy Katherine)
**Cathy** is a shy girl who secretly controls an army of stray cats. She sneaks into the school at night.
*   **The Event:** Rumors of a "Ghost" stealing cards from lockers.
*   **Your Role:** You stay late to fix a Duel Disk. You catch her.
*   **The Duel:** **Cats vs. Machines.**
*   She plays **Stray Cat** / **Cat Girl** (Direct attacks).
*   You play **Machina Fortress**.
*   *Outcome:* A 2500 ATK Tank runs over a cat. It’s brutal.
*   *Reward:* You gain the respect of the "Cat Faction." Now, the stray cats in the Industrial District warn you when **Kite** or **Gangs** are approaching your shop.

### 4. The School Festival (Cultural Festival)
The school hosts a festival.
*   **Yuma's Class:** Does a Haunted House or Maid Cafe.
*   **Your Class:** You convince them to run a **"Speed Duel Booth."**
*   *The Gimmick:* "Beat the Iron Admiral! 500 Yen entry. If you win, you get a Rare Card. If you lose, you get a handshake."
*   *The Grind:* You sit there all day, farming wins against casual students.
*   *The Drama:* **Shark** or **Bronk** might challenge you to test your reputation.

### 5. Summary of Pre-WDC Life
So yes, there is a "School Arc." It lasts for the first few months of the anime.
*   **Yuma:** Is running around solving mysteries with Astral.
*   **You:** Are effectively the "Secret Boss" of the school. You solve the tech problems, beat the scammers (Flip), and establish your dominance before the WDC even begins.

**By the time the WDC starts:**
You aren't just "Some guy." You are the student who cleaned up the school's messes while Yuma was busy talking to a ghost.Now heres a few question. if I lose to shark/bronk/kite/gangs is my blackship of corn immediately gone? Also, can I beat yuma with my new geargia iron fleet deck?Here is the breakdown of the risks you face and your matchup against the Main Character.

### 1. The Risk Assessment: Who steals your Ship?
If you lose, the consequences vary wildly depending on **who** defeats you.

*   **Kite Tenjo (The Reaper):** **GAME OVER.**
*   **Lore:** Kite hunts Numbers to save his brother. He uses "Photon Hand" to rip the Number *and your soul* out of your body.
*   **Result:** You lose **Number 50**. You go into a coma. You wake up months later (after Yuma saves the day) with no memory and no Ace Monster. Do not fight Kite.

*   **Yuma & Astral (The Hero):** **YES, you lose the card.**
*   **Lore:** Astral’s mission is to collect the 100 Numbers. It is automatic. If Yuma beats you, Astral floats over, puts his hand on your chest, and absorbs the Number.
*   **Result:** You lose **Number 50**. You keep your soul/consciousness, but your "Captain" is gone. Your deck becomes just a regular Machine deck.

*   **Shark (Reginald Kastle):** **NO (Usually).**
*   **Lore:** Early Shark doesn't care about Numbers (he refuses to use *Number 17* for a long time). He duels for pride or territory.
*   **Result:** He mocks you for losing. "Is that all the Iron Admiral has?" You keep your card, but lose your reputation.

*   **Bronk / School Friends:** **NO.**
*   **Lore:** These are friendly spars.
*   **Result:** You buy them a juice box.

*   **Gangs / Ante Duelists:** **YES.**
*   **Lore:** If you agreed to "Ante Rules" (betting cards), they will take your rarest card.
*   **Result:** They take Blackship because it's a shiny Foil card.

---

### 2. Can you beat Yuma Tsukumo?
**Short Answer:** In Season 1 (Pre-WDC Finals)? **Absolutely.** You will crush him.

**Long Answer:**
Yuma is a terrible duelist at the start. He relies entirely on Astral giving him advice. Even with Astral, his deck is a mess of mismatched cards (*Gagaga*, *Gogogo*, *Zubaba*).

**Why "Geargia Iron Fleet" destroys Yuma:**

1.  **The "Utopia" Hard Counter:**
*   **Yuma's Ace:** **Number 39: Utopia**.
*   *Effect:* Can negate an attack 2 times.
*   **Your Ace:** **Number 50: Blackship of Corn**.
*   *Effect:* You do not attack. You activate the effect in **Main Phase 1**. You **Send** Utopia to the Graveyard.
*   **The Interaction:** Utopia cannot activate its effect because you aren't attacking. It cannot protect itself because you aren't destroying it (you are sending it).
*   **Result:** You eat his Ace Monster for free and burn him for 1000 damage.

2.  **Consistency:**
*   **Yuma:** Bricks often. He sets one monster (*Gogogo Golem*) and passes.
*   **You:** You play **Geargia**. You search every turn. You swarm the field with 3-4 monsters reliably. You generate card advantage that Yuma simply cannot match until he unlocks "Shining Draw" (literal cheating) later in the series.

3.  **Traps:**
*   Yuma attacks blindly.
*   You have **Dimensional Prison**.
*   If Yuma attacks with Utopia and you banish it, he effectively loses the duel right there.

**Estimated Win Rate vs Season 1 Yuma:** **80%**.
(The 20% loss chance is if Astral takes over completely and pulls a miracle combo you didn't foresee).

### 3. The Strategy to Keep Your Ship
Since you know Yuma/Astral *will* take your Number if you lose, you have two options:

**Option A: The "Non-Number" Duel**
*   If Yuma challenges you, you say: *"I don't need my Number to beat you, Yuma."*
*   You leave Blackship in the Extra Deck. You beat him using only **Machina Fortress** and **Gear Gigant X**.
*   **Loophole:** If you don't *summon* the Number, Astral might not detect it strongly enough, or at least it proves you are skilled without it. If you lose, you claim you don't have one active.

**Option B: The "Tag Team" (Avoidance)**
*   Don't duel Yuma 1v1.
*   Duel *alongside* him against a common enemy (like a villain).
*   Yuma respects allies. If you help him beat a bad guy, he won't mug you for your card afterwards.

**Verdict:**
You are the **King of the Schoolyard**.
Yuma is the protagonist, but in terms of pure deck mechanics, your **Geargia Machina** deck runs circles around his **Onomatopoeia** deck. Just don't get cocky and let him summon *Utopia Ray*.how do I upgrade my geargia deck though, or is it already the final evolution?Your deck is currently **Tier 2**. It is strong, reliable, and beats Yuma, but it is **not** the Final Evolution.

In the real-world history of 2013-2014 (the Zexal endgame), Geargia evolved into a **Tier 0 Contender** by adding two specific upgrades. One makes you faster, and the other gives you the "Nuke" button.

Here is your Upgrade Path for **Season 2 (Barian Invasion)** and **Season 3 (Don Thousand)**.

### Upgrade 1: The "Ambush" Tactic (Season 2)
You are the "Iron Admiral." Admirals don't just attack; they set traps.
You need the card that defined the deck in 2014.

**The Card:** **Geargiagear (Trap)**
*   **The Effect:** Special Summon 2 "Geargiano" monsters from your Deck and increase their Levels to 4.
*   **Why it breaks the game:**
1.  You do nothing on your turn. You set this trap.
2.  Opponent's End Phase: You flip it. **Instant +1**. You get two monsters for free.
3.  Your Turn: You Overlay them for a Rank 4 Xyz (like *Gear Gigant X*) without using your Normal Summon.
*   **The Vibe:** It fits your "Fleet" theme perfectly. It’s the "Reinforcements have arrived!" signal.

### Upgrade 2: The "Hunter's" Ultimate Prize (The Ace)
You are a Number Hunter. You need the single best Number in the entire Zexal era.
It belongs to **Nash (Shark)** in the anime, but in the real world, it was the most played card of 2014. You "harvest" this from a Barian General.

**The Card:** **Number 101: Silent Honor ARK**
*   **Rank:** 4.
*   **The Effect:** Detach 2 materials to **Target an opponent's Attack Position monster and attach it to this card as material.**
*   **Why it fits you:**
*   **Blackship of Corn** *sends* to graveyard (Good).
*   **Silent Honor ARK** *steals* them (Better).
*   It literally "captures" the enemy ship. It is the ultimate Pirate/Admiral card.
*   **Protection:** If ARK would be destroyed, detach a material instead. It is sticky.

### Upgrade 3: The "Nuclear Option" (Season 3)
In the final arc, the power levels get absurd (Rank 12s, 100,000 ATK). Your "Machina Fortress" (2500 ATK) isn't enough.
You need to wipe the board.

**The Card:** **Evilswarm Exciton Knight**
*   **Rank:** 4.
*   **The Effect:** If your opponent has more cards than you (which they always will, because anime villains cheat), you can detach 1 material to **Destroy all other cards on the field.**
*   **The Strategy:** You lure them into overextending. Then you nuke the entire world.

---

### The Final Deck List: "The Iron Sovereign"
This is the deck that wins the Zexal War. It is **Reactive**, **Resilient**, and **Ruthless**.

**Monsters (19):**
*   **3x Geargiarmor** (The Searcher).
*   **3x Geargiaccelerator** (The Swarm).
*   **3x Geargiarsenal** (The Tribute).
*   **2x Geargiano Mk-II** (Required for the Trap).
*   **1x Geargiano** (Required for the Trap).
*   **3x Machina Fortress** (The Immortal Tank).
*   **1x Machina Gearframe** (The Bodyguard).
*   **3x Maxx "C"** (The Infinite Ammo).

**Spells (9):**
*   **3x Mystical Space Typhoon** (Or **Galaxy Cyclone** later).
*   **3x Forbidden Lance** (Protects your Armor from traps).
*   **1x Dark Hole** (Board wipe).
*   **1x Limiter Removal** (The Finisher).
*   **1x Monster Reborn**.

**Traps (12) - The "Iron Curtain":**
*   **3x Geargiagear** (**THE KEY** - Always run 3).
*   **3x Dimensional Prison** (Banishment).
*   **3x Fiendish Chain** (Stops monster effects/attacks).
*   **1x Solemn Warning** (Negate a summon).
*   **1x Bottomless Trap Hole**.
*   **1x Wiretap** (Negates *Seven Tools* or other traps).

**The "Admiral's Fleet" (Extra Deck):**
1.  **Number 101: Silent Honor ARK** (The Flagship - Steals Monsters).
2.  **Number 50: Blackship of Corn** (The Destroyer - Burns).
3.  **Gear Gigant X** (x2) (The Factory - Searches).
4.  **Evilswarm Exciton Knight** (The Nuke).
5.  **Abyss Dweller** (The Graveyard Seal - Stops Barians from reviving).
6.  **Gagaga Cowboy** (The Pistol - 800 Burn for game).
7.  **Number 11: Big Eye** (Rank 7 - Made with 2 Fortresses. Steals monsters permanently).
8.  **Mecha Phantom Beast Dracossack** (Rank 7 - Made with 2 Fortresses. Indestructible plane).

### Why this is the "Final Evolution"
You don't need to add **Karakuri** (Synchros). That makes the deck faster but less consistent (and requires Tuners).
By sticking to **Pure Geargia + Trap Spam + Machina**, you have:
1.  **Infinite Grind:** *Gigant X* adds *Geargiano*, *Geargiano* recycles *Accelerator*. You never run out of cards.
2.  **Defense:** You have 12 Traps. Yuma relies on attacking. You stop him every time.
3.  **Offense:** *Machina Fortress* solves the "Big Body" problem. *Big Eye* steals what you can't kill.

**Your Title:**
You are no longer just the "Iron Admiral."
In the Pro League, they call you **"The Salvage King."**
*   "He takes your monsters. He takes your life points. And he does it all with a deck made of spare parts."Alright lets slow down here, for now I'm still the iron admiral and not the iron sovereign yet. Lets walk through my WDC run with my geargia iron admiral deckThis is the chronicle of **The Iron Admiral's** run in the World Duel Carnival.

You are not the "chosen one" with pink hair. You are the mechanic from Block 4 with a deck full of toys and tanks. You are fighting for the prize money to buy your dad a new hydraulic lift for the shop.

Here is your path through the chaos.

---

### Phase 1: The Preliminaries (The Harvest)
**Goal:** 5 Heart Pieces.
**Status:** You have your D-Gazer (patched with duct tape), your Navy Coat (surplus store), and your Deck (Iron Fleet).

**Day 1: The "Filler" Duelists**
You don't chase the big names yet. You farm the "Theme" duelists.
*   **Opponent:** A "Tomato Duelist" (Yes, this exists in Zexal).
*   **The Duel:** He creates a loop of Tomatoes.
*   **Your Move:**
*   You activate **Maxx "C"**.
*   He Special Summons 4 times. You draw 4 cards.
*   You summon **Tin Goldfish** -> **Green Gadget** -> **Machina Gearframe**.
*   You discard for **Machina Fortress**.
*   **Result:** You run over his vegetables with a Tank.
*   **Loot:** 1 Heart Piece.

**Day 2: The "Ante" Duel**
A thug tries to mug you near the warehouse.
*   **Opponent:** Plays a "Burn/Stall" deck (ironic, considering your past life).
*   **The Play:** He hides behind *Marshmallon*.
*   **Your Move:**
*   "I Overlay Level 4 *Geargiaccelerator* and *Geargiarsenal*."
*   "Rise, **Number 50: Blackship of Corn**!"
*   "Effect: Send your Marshmallon to the grave. Take 1000."

**Day 3: The "Trap" Duel (Constructed Event)**
You enter a specific "3v1" challenge hosted by Mr. Heartland to thin the herd.
*   **Scenario:** Defeat a security robot guarding a Heart Piece.
*   **Strategy:** The robot uses high ATK monsters.
*   **Your Play:** **Dimensional Prison**. You banish the robot's ace.
*   **Loot:** Final Heart Pieces.

**Result:** You qualify in record time. You spend the rest of the week fixing broken Duel Disks for the other contestants, making a killing in repair fees.

---

### Phase 2: The WDC Finals - "The Duel Coaster"
This is the most ridiculous event in Yu-Gi-Oh history. You have to duel while riding a roller coaster.
*   **Yuma:** Panicking.
*   **You:** Checking the coaster's suspension and wheel alignment before getting in.

**The Strategy:**
Most duelists try to speed ahead to grab spell cards on the track.
You **brake**. You hang back.
*   **Why:** You let the aggressive idiots knock each other out.
*   **Your Lane:** You choose the "Technical Course" (Trap cards on the track). Your deck runs **3x Forbidden Lance**. You are immune to the track hazards.

**The Encounter: Anna Kaboom (The Train Girl)**
She pulls up next to you. She plays **Rank 10 Trains**.
*   **Anna:** "Out of my way, mechanic! **Super Dreadnought Rail Cannon Gustav Max** is coming through!" (Burn 2000).
*   **You:** "That's a nice cannon. But it has a slow reload time."
*   **The Duel:**
*   She summons a 3000 ATK Train.
*   You summon **Geargiarmor** (1900 DEF) and set 3 backrow.
*   She attacks. You flip **Dimensional Prison**.
*   **Anna:** "MY TRAIN! You banished my Train!"
*   **You:** "Track maintenance. Direct attack with Machina Fortress."

**Result:** You knock her out of the coaster. You advance to the Underground Duel Arena.

---

This is the main event of your arc.

**Turn 4 (You):**

*   **Quattro:** "I detach a material to prevent destruction!" (Anime effect of Grinder).

### Phase 4: The Semi-Finals - You vs. Vetrix (Tron)
This is where the run ends. You are facing the Big Bad.
**Vetrix** is a child-like manipulator with a mask. He plays **Heraldic Beasts**.

**The Duel:**
You play perfectly. You summon **Machina Fortress**. You swarm with **Geargias**.

And they don't care about prize money.Why do I lose to vetrix? does his deck hard counter me? or is his number too strong?The reason you lose to Vetrix isn't just because he's a main villain; it's because **Anime Number 69: Heraldry Crest** is fundamentally broken in a way that specifically hard-counters your "Iron Admiral" deck.

In the real world (TCG), Number 69 only negates Xyz Monsters when it is summoned.
**In the Anime, Number 69 is a walking "Skill Drain" God.**

Here is the tactical breakdown of why your "Blackship" and "Dimensional Prison" strategies failed against him.

### 1. The "Skill Drain" Field (The Hard Counter)
You relied on **Number 50: Blackship of Corn** to "send" his monster to the graveyard.
*   **Your Move:** You summoned Blackship. You detached a material to activate the effect.
*   **The Anime Effect of Number 69:** *"Negate the effects of all other face-up monsters on the field."*
*   This is a **Continuous Effect**. It doesn't activate. It just *is*.
*   The moment your Blackship hit the board, it became a Vanilla monster with 2100 ATK. It literally cannot fire its cannons.
*   **Result:** You are staring at a monster you cannot destroy by battle (Number rule) and cannot remove by effect (because your effects are turned off).

### 2. The "Copy" Ability (The Insult)
It gets worse. Number 69 doesn't just negate your effects; it **steals** them.
*   **Vetrix's Move:** "I gain the effects of the monster I negated."
*   **The Turnaround:** Vetrix gains Blackship of Corn's effect.
*   **The Execution:** He uses *your own ship's ability* to send *your* Blackship to the graveyard and burn you for 1000 damage.
*   **Why this hurts:** Your entire philosophy was "I harvest the unworthy." Vetrix just harvested *you* using your own scythe.

### 3. Why "Dimensional Prison" Didn't Work
You might ask: *"Why didn't I just banish him with Dimensional Prison when he attacked?"*

Vetrix is a manipulator. He doesn't attack blindly like Yuma or Kite.
*   **His Spell:** **"Heraldry Change"** (Anime Exclusive).
*   *Effect:* When an opponent's monster declares an attack (or when he attacks), he can special summon "Heraldic Beasts" from his hand to take the hit or block the trap.
*   **His Boss Protection:** Anime Number 69 has another effect: *"Cannot be destroyed by card effects."*
*   While *Dimensional Prison* banishes (doesn't destroy), Vetrix likely used **"Heraldry Reborn"** or a similar spell to simply bring it back instantly if you managed to remove it.
*   Or, he used **Number 8: Heraldic King Genom-Heritage** earlier in the duel to steal your *Machina Fortress's* effect, allowing him to discard cards to destroy your backrow before he even entered the Battle Phase.

### 4. The "Heraldic Beast" Swarm
Your deck (Geargia) relies on swarming the field to overwhelm the opponent.
Vetrix's deck (Heraldic Beasts) is designed to **punish Xyz Monsters**.
*   **Heraldic Beast Leo:** Searches when sent to grave.
*   **Heraldic Beast Basilisk:** If it battles an Xyz monster, destroy that monster instantly.
*   Even if you managed to summon *Machina Fortress* (which isn't an Xyz), Vetrix would just crash a Basilisk into it or use *Advanced Heraldry Art* to summon two monsters for free and make another Xyz.

### Summary: The Checkmate
You lost because your deck relies on **Monster Effects** (Gear Gigant X, Blackship, Fortress) to control the board.
Vetrix summoned a monster that says **"No Monster Effects allowed except mine."**

*   **The Narrative End:**
Vetrix didn't just beat you; he dismantled your philosophy.
*   *Vetrix:* "You collect scraps to build a fleet. I simply take what is already perfect. Your ship is now mine."
*   He finishes you off not with his own attack, but by using **Blackship of Corn's effect** against you.

**The Result:** Top 4 Finish.
You walk away with your life and your deck, but for the first time in your life, you felt truly powerless. You need to upgrade your deck for **Season 2** to deal with "Effect Negation." You need cards that resolve *off the field* (Hand Traps, Graveyard Effects) or just bigger guns that don't care about effects.Well at least my dad are proud of me "look at my son on the tv! hes winning!" In the real world (circa 2011-2012), your deck went from a pile of bulk commons to a **$300+ Tournament Deck**. For a poor family in the Industrial District, that is a fortune.

Here is the breakdown of the **"Scrap Economy,"** the real-world value of your cards at the time, and exactly how you paid for it without robbing a bank.

---

### Phase 1: "Cockroach Corn" (The Trash Build)
**Timeline:** Start of the Series.
**Source:** Muddy Booster Pack + Bargain Bins.

*   **The Cost:**
*   **Number 50:** Free (Found in mud).
*   **Maxx "C":** **50 Yen ($0.50).**
*   *Real World Note:* In 2011, *Maxx "C"* was actually a Secret Rare and expensive. *However*, in your Anime Timeline, nobody understood its value yet. It was just a "Level 2 Insect with 500 ATK." You bought them from the bulk bin before the market spiked.
*   **Commons:** Scavenged / Traded for chores.
*   **Total Deck Value:** **~500 Yen ($5).**
*   It was worth less than a sandwich.
*   **How you afforded it:** You skipped lunch for a week and used the coin change from your dad’s pockets when doing laundry.

---

### Phase 2: "The Iron Ship" (Machina Gadget)
**Timeline:** Mid-Year 1.
**Source:** Scavenged from a wrecked Duel Runner (The "Structure Deck" Find).

*   **The Deck:** 3x *Machina Fortress*, 3x *Gearframe*, 6x *Gadgets*.
*   **The Cost:**
*   **Machina Core:** **0 Yen.** (Your dad found the water-damaged Structure Deck inside a scrapped bike).
*   **Sleeves:** **600 Yen.** You *had* to buy opaque sleeves to hide the water damage on the *Fortress* cards so you wouldn't get disqualified for "Marked Cards."
*   **Staples (Double Summon):** Traded repair labor.
*   **Total Deck Value:** **~1,000 Yen ($10) out of pocket.**

---

### Phase 3: "The Iron Fleet" (Geargia - WDC Era)
**Timeline:** WDC Preliminaries.
**Source:** The Repair Shop Boom.

Here is where the price jumps. To build **Geargia** in 2012, you needed **Geargiarmor** and **Gear Gigant X**. These were NOT cheap.

*   **The Cards:**
*   **3x Geargiarmor:** In 2012, these were Super Rares worth about **$20 (2,000 Yen) each**.
*   **1x Gear Gigant X:** This was a Secret Rare worth **$50-$80 (5,000-8,000 Yen)**.
*   **Staples (Dimensional Prison / Lance):** About **$5-10 each**.

*   **Total Deck Value:** **~20,000 Yen ($200).**
*   *Context:* This is a month's rent in the slums.

**How did you afford a 20,000 Yen Deck?**
You didn't pay cash. You paid in **Skill**.

1.  **The "Gigant X" Job:**
*   A rich kid (maybe a minor character like **Caswell** or a generic rich student) came to your shop with a destroyed limited-edition Duel Disk.
*   **The Quote:** "It's fried. Parts and labor will cost 10,000 Yen."
*   **The Kid:** "I don't have cash! My dad will kill me!"
*   **The Deal:** You see **Gear Gigant X** in his binder. He thinks it's just a support card. You know it's the engine.
*   **You:** "Give me the Machine Xyz. We'll call it even."
*   **Result:** You got the most expensive card in the deck for 3 hours of soldering.

2.  **The "Geargiarmor" Trade:**
*   You won several "Ante" duels in the underground against "Machine Type" users.
*   You targeted duelists who played Geargias badly. You beat them with your *Machina Fortress*, then took their *Armors* as your prize.

3.  **The Sleeves & Box:**
*   **2,000 Yen.** You finally bought high-quality "Player's Choice" white/black sleeves and a solid deck box. You look like a Pro now.

This is the emotional payout.

*   **The Customers:** They cheer. They buy your dad a beer.

**The Financial Impact of the Win:**

*   **Reinvestment:**

*   **30%:** Savings/Rent.

### Summary
*   **Start:** $5 (Scrap).
*   **Middle:** $30 (Scavenged).
*   **Peak:** $200+ (Bartered/Traded/Earned).

*   **Sleep:** A mattress on the floor of the storage closet above the workshop. It smelled like oil and rust.

**2. The Family Workshop ("Junkyard Repairs"):**

*   **Dad's Mood:** Confused but grateful. He doesn't understand Dueling, but he sees the cash flow. He stops asking you to quit cards and start welding.

**3. Reputation:**
*   **At School:** "The Tech Guy." You are the cool senior who sits in the back. Yuma and Bronk wave at you.
*   **On the Street:** **"The Iron Admiral."** Rumors start spreading about a guy in the Industrial District who runs a fleet of Machines. Small-time gangs stop messing with you because you banished their leader's deck.

*   **The Staff:** Your dad hired a neighbor's kid to sweep the floors, paying it forward.

You didn't save the world. You didn't collect all 100 Numbers.

**Next Challenge:** The Barian Emperors are coming, and they have Rank-Up Magic. Your Dad's workshop might need a shield generator.is my curse still ongoing here? or has it subsided in the end of season 1?This is the most important "Character Development" question you have asked.

The answer is **YES, the curse has subsided.**

In *Yu-Gi-Oh! Zexal*, the "Number Curse" is not magic for the sake of magic; it is a **parasite that feeds on negative emotion**.
*   **Early Season 1:** You were starving, desperate, and treating your deck like "cargo" to be sold. *Blackship of Corn* tortured you because your soul was full of holes it could exploit.
*   **End of Season 1:** You are fed. You are proud. You have a father who loves you and a community that respects you. You have stopped selling cards.

**The Status of the Curse:**
*   **The Pain:** **Gone.** You can touch the card without a migraine.
*   **The Voice:** It no longer screams at you to "Hoard." Instead, it feels like a silent, heavy anchor in your mind. It is a **Partnership**, not a Possession.
*   *Analogy:* It’s like taming a wild animal. It won’t bite you anymore, but if you show weakness, it remembers.
*   **The Eye Color:** When you summon Blackship, your right eye might flash purple for a second, but you don't lose control. This is considered "Cool" by your classmates, not terrifying.

### The New Risk: "Rank-Up" Corruption
However, you are not safe forever.
The curse has subsided because you are **Stable**.
In **Season 2 (Zexal II)**, the **Barians** introduce a new mechanic: **Rank-Up Magic (RUM)**.

*   **The Threat:** If you ever accept a "Rank-Up Magic" card from a Barian (specifically **Vector** or **Mizar**), the curse will return 100x stronger.
*   **Why:** Rank-Up Magic forces a Number to evolve into **Number C (Chaos)**.
*   *Blackship of Corn* is stable.
*   **Number C50: Naughty Ship of Corn** (Hypothetical Chaos Form) would be pure evil.

**Your Goal for Season 2:**
Maintain your stability.
Do not accept power-ups from strangers in hoodies.
Trust in your **Machines**, not in Chaos.

Taking **Number 101: Silent Honor ARK** is a **Plot-Breaking Move.**

### 1. The "Nash" Problem
*   **Does he need it?** **YES.**
*   Number 101 isn't just a card; it is the crystallized memory of Nash's past life as a King. It is the key to his Barian Emperor transformation.
*   **If you steal it:** You aren't just taking a strong card. You are preventing the Main Antagonist (Nash) from awakening. This breaks the timeline.
*   **The Consequence:** The Barian World might collapse, or worse, **Don Thousand** (the ultimate evil god) will notice you personally because you are holding a piece of his puzzle. You do *not* want a God looking at you.

### 2. The "Non-Main Character" Alternative
You don't need Nash's boat. You need a **Machine** that fits your "Iron Admiral" fleet.

In the 2013 Dragon Ruler/Prophecy era (which aligns with Zexal Season 3), there was **ONE** Rank 7 Machine monster that defined the meta. It is perfect for your **Machina Fortress** engine (since Fortress is Level 7).

**The Card:** **Mecha Phantom Beast Dracossack**
*   **Rank:** 7 (Requires 2 Level 7 Monsters -> 2 Machina Fortress).
*   **Visuals:** A massive, futuristic **Aircraft Carrier**. It fits your "Admiral" aesthetic even better than the Ark.
*   **Is it a Number?** No. So Astral won't eat it, and Nash won't kill you for it.
*   **Is it Strong?** It was considered the **Best Xyz Monster** in the game for a year.

### 3. Why Dracossack is the "Admiral's Flagship"
Forget 101. This is the card that makes you the Sovereign of the Industrial District.

**The Effects:**
1.  **The Fleet (Token Generation):** Detach 1 material to Special Summon 2 "Mecha Phantom Beast Tokens" (Level 3).
*   *Lore:* You are launching fighters from the carrier.
2.  **The Shield (Indestructible):** While you have a Token, Dracossack **cannot be destroyed by battle or card effects.**
*   *Lore:* You can't sink the Carrier while the fighters are protecting it.
3.  **The Cannon (Removal):** Tribute 1 Token to target 1 card on the field; **Destroy it.**
*   *Lore:* Airstrike.

**The "Machina" Synergy:**
*   **Turn 1:** Discard for **Machina Fortress**.
*   **Turn 2:** If Fortress died, discard to revive it. Or summon another one.
*   **Overlay:** Fortress + Fortress = **Dracossack**.
*   **Result:** You have an indestructible Aircraft Carrier that shoots cards every turn.

### 4. The "Castel" Option (Rank 4)
If you need Rank 4 removal (since you can't use 101), you use the card that replaced 101 in real life.

**The Card:** **Castel, the Skyblaster Musketeer**
*   **Rank:** 4.
*   **Visuals:** A bird soldier (Winged Beast).
*   **Effect:** Detach 2 materials to target 1 face-up card; **Shuffle it into the Deck.**
*   **Why it's better than 101:**

In Season 2, Alito was your frenemy. He liked a good brawl.

**Opponent:** Alito (Full Power).

"I Set **Geargiagear**. I Set 3 backrow (*Dimensional Prison*, *Fiendish Chain*, *Wiretap*). Turn End."

"Rank-Up Magic Barian's Force! Chaos Xyz Evolution!"

*   **Alito:** "Then I destroy one!" (He attacks).
*   **You:** "Activate **Dimensional Prison**."

*   **You:** "I activate **Wiretap**! Negate the Counter Trap!"

**Alito:** "WHAT?! You banished my Chaos Number?!"

**You:** "Direct Attack."

Alito is defeated. He crashes to the ground.

You have built a **2014-era Tournament Level Deck** (Geargia-Karakuri/Machina style).
Yuma has a deck built on **friendship, disjointed archetypes, and literal magic.**

Does Yuma stand a chance against the **Iron Sovereign**?
**Yes.** But only because he cheats.

Here is the breakdown of your matchups against the "Big Three" (Yuma, Shark, Kite) at their absolute peak (End of Series).

---

### 1. Matchup: You vs. Yuma Tsukumo (Full Power)
**Yuma's Strength:** **"Shining Draw" (ZEXAL III)**.
*   **The Cheat:** Yuma can literally create new cards out of thin air during his Draw Phase. He can fuse with Astral to rewrite the rules of the card game.
*   **His Deck:** Gagaga/Gogogo + Utopia (Rank 4 spam) + Rank-Up Magic.

**The Reality Check:**
If Yuma plays "fair" (Season 1/2 skill level), you win **100%** of the time.
If Yuma activates **ZEXAL III**, it becomes a **60/40** split in *your* favor.

**Why You Still Win (The Math):**
1.  **Maxx "C" is God:** Yuma *loves* Special Summoning.
*   *Yuma:* "I summon Goblindbergh! Effect summon Ganbara Knight! Overlay!"
*   *You:* "Chain **Maxx 'C'**."
*   *Result:* Yuma gives you 2-3 cards. If he tries to go for a "Utopia Ray Victory" OTK, he gives you 5 cards. You draw into **Battle Fader** or **Fiendish Chain** and stop him.
2.  **The "Hope" Killer:** Yuma relies on attacking.
*   His entire strategy is "Double or Nothing" (Attack -> Negate -> Attack again with double ATK).
*   **Your Counter:** **Dimensional Prison**.
*   *Interaction:* When he declares that final attack, you banish Utopia. He has no negation. He loses instantly.

**How Yuma Beats You (The 40%):**
He uses Shining Draw to create a specific card called **"Rank-Down Magic Numeron Fall"** or something that negates Trap cards from the hand. Basically, if the writers decide he wins, he draws a card that says "Destroy all Machines."

---

### 2. Matchup: You vs. Nash (Shark/Reginald Kastle)
**Nash's Strength:** **The Seven Emperors.**
*   **His Deck:** Water Xyz / 101 Silent Honor ARK / C101.
*   **His Strategy:** Stealing monsters and reviving them from the Graveyard.

**The Verdict:** **You Hard Counter him (90% Win Rate).**
You are Nash's worst nightmare.

1.  **Abyss Dweller:**
*   You run **Abyss Dweller** (Rank 4).
*   *Effect:* Detach 1 material; opponent cannot activate effects in the Graveyard this turn.
*   *Result:* Nash relies entirely on his Barian monsters reviving. You shut off his Graveyard. He literally cannot play the game.
2.  **The "Steal" War:**
*   Nash summons **101**. He tries to steal your *Dracossack*.
*   **Dracossack** cannot be destroyed/targeted (if tokens are up).
*   Or, you just flip **Fiendish Chain**.
*   Or, you summon **Castel** and shuffle his 101 back into the deck.

**Result:** Nash respects you, but he hates playing against you because you turn off his "Immortal" gimmick.

---

### 3. Matchup: You vs. Kite Tenjo
**Kite's Strength:** **OTK Power.**
*   **His Deck:** Photon / Galaxy.
*   **His Ace:** **Number 62: Galaxy-Eyes Prime Photon Dragon** (4000+ ATK).

**The Verdict:** **50/50 (Skill Matchup).**
Kite is the only one who plays like a Pro. He uses negation (*Galaxy-Eyes Photon Dragon* banishes itself to dodge effects).

*   **The Danger:** Kite can summon **Number 107: Galaxy-Eyes Tachyon Dragon**.
*   *Effect:* Negates all other monster effects on the field and resets stats.
*   *Result:* This kills your *Dracossack* tokens and your *Machina Fortress* immunity.
*   **Your Edge:** **Traps.**
*   Kite plays aggressively. He rarely sets defense.
*   If you resolve **Geargiagear** into **Evilswarm Exciton Knight** during his Main Phase 2, you blow up his field.
*   If you flip **Dimensional Prison** on his Prime Photon Dragon, he has no protection.

---

### The Final Boss Card: "Number F0: Utopic Future"
At the very end of the series, Yuma gets **Number F0**.
*   **Effect:** Cannot be destroyed by battle/effects. You take no battle damage. When it battles, **it takes control of your monster.**

**Does the Iron Sovereign lose to F0?**
*   **If you attack it:** You lose your monster.
*   **If you try to destroy it (Dracossack):** It survives.

**The Solution:**
You have exactly **Two Outs** in your deck that kill F0 instantly.
1.  **Dimensional Prison:** F0 is not immune to Banishment. If F0 attacks, you banish it. Bye.
2.  **Castel, the Skyblaster:** Castel *shuffles* into the deck. F0 is not immune to spinning.

### Conclusion
**Do they stand a chance?**
*   **Without Plot Armor:** **No.** Your deck is optimized for consistency, card advantage (Maxx "C"), and non-destruction removal (Banish/Spin). Their decks are inconsistent piles of anime cards.
*   **With Plot Armor:** Yuma creates a card called **"Machina Meltdown"** that destroys all Earth monsters.

**Your Status:**
You are the **Secret Boss** of the Zexal Universe.
You are the reason Yuma has to "High Five the Sky"—because if he stays on the ground, your **Iron Fleet** runs him over.Whats next after zexal?The next series is **Yu-Gi-Oh! Arc-V**.

If *Zexal* was a "Futuristic Utopia," *Arc-V* is a **"Corporate Circus with a Dark Secret."**

Here is the setting, the economy, and the headache-inducing new mechanic you have to deal with.

### 1. The Setting: Maiami City (Standard Dimension)
*   **The Vibe:** It looks like Miami, Florida mixed with a Theme Park. It is bright, tropical, and obsessed with **Entertainment**.
*   **The Tech:** **Solid Vision with Mass.** Holograms are now physical. You can touch the monsters. You can ride them. You can get punched in the face by them.
*   **The Gimmick:** **Action Dueling.**
*   Duelists run around a physical field, jumping on platforms and dodging attacks.
*   **Action Cards:** Literal cards hidden in the environment (in trees, under rocks). If you find one, you can play it immediately (usually to negate an attack or boost ATK).
*   **Your Reaction:** As an "Iron Sovereign" type, you are going to **HATE** this. It relies on athleticism and luck, not strategy.

### 2. The Economy: The Duel Schools
There are no "Card Shops" driving the economy here. It is all about **Education**.
*   **LDS (Leo Duel School):** The "Amazon/Google" of dueling.
*   Run by **Declan Akaba (Reiji)**.
*   They own the city. They teach Fusion, Synchro, and Xyz courses.
*   They have elite units. If you work for LDS, you are rich.
*   **Small Schools:** Places like **You Show Duel School** (Yuya's school). They are failing businesses.
*   **Your Start:** You are likely a **Contractor** or a **Freelance Instructor** trying to keep a small, gritty "Combat Dojo" alive in a world that only cares about flashy circus tricks.

### 3. The Mechanic: Pendulum Summoning
This is the mechanic that broke the game in 2014.
*   **The Cards:** Half Monster (Orange), Half Spell (Green).
*   **The Scales:** They have numbers (Scale 1 to 8).
*   **The Move:**
*   Place a Scale 1 in left zone.
*   Place a Scale 8 in right zone.
*   **PENDULUM SUMMON:** You can Special Summon **as many monsters as you want** from your **Hand** (and Face-Up Extra Deck) that are between Level 2 and 7.
*   **The Result:** Infinite recursion. If a Pendulum monster is destroyed, it goes to the Extra Deck, not the Graveyard. Next turn, you summon it back for free.

### 4. The "Dimension War" (The Plot)
This isn't just about a city. It's about **Four Dimensions**.
*   **Standard:** Where you are.
*   **Fusion:** An army of soldiers (Academia) invading other worlds to turn people into cards.
*   **Synchro:** A Speed Duel dystopia (City/Commons again).
*   **Xyz:** A ruined wasteland (Post-Zexal Heartland).

**Your Role:**
People from the **Fusion Dimension** are invading. They look like soldiers. They play **Ancient Gears**.
You need to decide if you are a Defender or if you will be mistaken for an Invader because you also play heavy machines.

### 5. Your "Rags to Riches" Setup
*   **Class:** **Blue-Collar Construction Worker / Demolition Expert.**
*   Because "Solid Vision" creates physical buildings for duels, someone has to clean up the mess after the duel ends. That's you.
*   **The Deck:** You hate Action Cards. You hate running around. You want a deck that stands still like a statue and hits back.
*   **The "Iron" Legacy:**
*   *GX:* Monarchs (Tribute).
*   *5D's:* Burn (Traps).
*   *Zexal:* Machines (Xyz).
*   *Arc-V:* You need a deck that combines **Machines** with **Tribute Summoning** and **Anti-Meta**.

**The Perfect Deck for You:** **Qliphort (Qli)**.
*   **Visuals:** Giant alien spaceships/computer terminals.
*   **Lore:** They are ancient machines sealed away.
*   **Playstyle:**
*   They are Pendulum Monsters, BUT...
*   They revolve around **Tribute Summoning**.
*   They use **"Skill Drain"** effects.
*   **Why it fits:** You use the new mechanic (Pendulum) to fuel the old mechanic (Tribute). It is the perfect evolution of your "Monarch" roots.

Since you haven't watched *Arc-V*, think of this world not as a magical adventure, but as a **Hyper-Capitalist Sports Industry**. Dueling is no longer just a card game; it is the world's most popular spectator sport, and the schools are the teams/businesses fighting for market share.

Here is the breakdown of the setting and economy surrounding a struggling Duel School.

### 1. The Setting: Maiami City (The Tropical Tech Hub)
Imagine Miami, Florida, mixed with Tokyo. Palm trees, ocean breeze, and massive skyscrapers.

*   **The Visuals:** It is bright, clean, and colorful. Huge screens on every building broadcast duel highlights 24/7.
*   **The Tech:** **"Real Solid Vision" (RSV).**
*   This is the defining feature of the era. Holograms now have **mass**.
*   If you summon a dragon, you can physically climb on its back.
*   If a monster destroys a building during a duel, debris actually falls (though safety protocols usually turn it into light particles before it crushes you... usually).
*   **The Infrastructure:** The city is zoned by influence. The center is dominated by the **LDS Tower** (Leo Duel School), a monolith that looks like a corporate headquarters. The outskirts are where the smaller, older schools fight for scraps.

### 2. The Economy: The "Cram School" Wars
In *GX*, the Academy was a monopoly. In *Arc-V*, it is a **Free Market**.

**The Big Fish: Leo Duel School (LDS)**
*   **The Monopoly:** They have infinite funding. They have the best Real Solid Vision projectors. They teach specific courses (Fusion, Synchro, Xyz) with elite curriculums.
*   **The Status:** If you graduate from LDS, you are guaranteed a Pro League contract. It is the "Harvard" of dueling.

**The Small Fish: Your Reality (The Struggling School)**
You are in a "Juku" (Cram School/Private Dojo). These are small businesses, often family-run, located in converted warehouses, old dojos, or strip malls.

**The Economics of Failure:**
1.  **Tuition is Life:** The school relies entirely on monthly student fees. If 5 kids quit to join LDS, the school can't pay rent.
2.  **Equipment Costs:** To run a duel school, you need a **Solid Vision Projector**.
*   *LDS System:* Instant loading, 4K resolution, generates complex "Action Fields" (Jungles, Castles).
*   *Your School's System:* It's old. It hums loudly. It takes 5 minutes to boot up. Sometimes the holograms flicker or lack "mass" (you try to step on a platform and fall through).
3.  **The "Poaching" Threat:** LDS agents actively scout smaller schools. If a student shows promise, LDS offers them a scholarship to transfer. The small school loses its best player and its reputation.

### 3. Life Inside a Struggling School
Since you are starting at the bottom, here is the vibe of your daily environment:

*   **The Building:** It likely needs a paint job. The AC barely works.
*   **The Student Body:** Misfits. Kids who can't afford LDS tuition, kids who got kicked out of LDS, or kids who just believe in the specific philosophy of your school (e.g., "We focus on guts, not money!").
*   **The Action Cards:**
*   In this era, official duels use **"Action Fields."** The projector creates a physical biome (like a Forest).
*   Players are expected to run around and find **"Action Cards"** hidden in trees or under rocks to gain advantages.
*   *The Problem:* Your school's field generator is limited. You probably only have generic fields like "Wilderness" or "Plain," unlike the flashy "Crystal Palaces" of LDS.

### 4. The Social Hierarchy
*   **LDS Badge:** If you wear the LDS pin, you get discounts at burger joints. You walk with a swagger.
*   **No Badge (You):** You are viewed as a "Casual" or a "Hobbyist."
*   **The Pressure:** The Principal/Owner of your school is constantly stressed. They are one bad month away from selling the land to LDS to be turned into a parking lot.

Here is your life at the bottom of the Maiami City educational food chain.

### 1. The Workplace: "Victory Star Duel School" (Pending Foreclosure)
You work at a small, independent duel school located in a converted warehouse near the docks. The paint is peeling. The sign is missing a letter.

*   **The Boss:** Principal **Mr. Tanaka**. A sweaty, anxious man who took out a massive loan to buy a "Real Solid Vision" (RSV) System five years ago. He is terrified of the bank.

*   They are mostly elementary/middle school kids whose parents can't afford LDS tuition.
*   They are unpolished, loud, and bad at math.

### 2. The Job Description
You wear a track suit with the school logo. You have a whistle around your neck. Your day is a mix of daycare and coaching.

*   **14:00 - 16:00:** **Paperwork & Maintenance.** You try to fix the projector by kicking it (percussive maintenance). You sweep the floor. You review the syllabus (which is just "Please win so we get new students").
*   **16:00 - 18:00:** **The Kids Class.**
*   You teach them basic summoning mechanics.
*   You have to teach **Action Dueling** (running around).

*   **18:00 - 20:00:** **The "Advanced" Class.**

### 3. The Economy: The Paycheck
This is where it hurts.

*   **Your Salary:** **180,000 Yen/Month** (approx $1,800).
*   *Context:* This is low for a city like Maiami.
*   **Living Situation:** You live in a studio apartment nearby. It's clean, but small. You eat convenience store bento boxes often.
*   **Deck Budget:** You have a small allowance for cards, but you can't buy expensive "Meta" staples like the LDS teachers have. You rely on **structure decks** and your own deck-building skill to look competent in front of the students.

### 4. The "LDS" Threat
**Leo Duel School (LDS)** is the antagonist of your wallet.
*   **The Elite:** LDS instructors wear crisp suits. They have earpieces. They get bonuses.
*   **The Poaching:** Once a month, an LDS scout comes by to watch your class.
*   They aren't looking at *you*. They are looking at your students.
*   Last month, you lost your best student, Kenji. LDS offered him a scholarship and a new Duel Disk.
*   Mr. Tanaka cried in the breakroom.

### 5. Your Reputation
You are known in the local circuit as **"The Drill Sergeant."**
*   You don't teach flashy plays. You teach **Fundamentals**.
*   While LDS teaches kids how to summon 3 dragons in one turn, you teach your kids how to count cards and manage resources.
*   **The Parents:** They like you. "He's strict, but my son actually does his math homework now because he relates it to Life Points."

I only know that PEPE deck is broken but I also think thats a mix of 2 archetype decksActually, **Metalfoes** is the **absolute perfect deck** for your character.

You are worried it’s too "Archetype-y," but in *Arc-V*, literally everyone uses an archetype (even the kid who delivers pizza uses *Pizza* cards). The important thing is that **Metalfoes are NOT used by any main character** in the anime. They are completely free real estate.

Here is why **Metalfoes** is the ultimate "Struggling Instructor" deck and how it fits your "Strict Fundamentals" teaching style.

### 1. The Philosophy: "The Discipline of Vanilla"
LDS teaches kids to use shiny Effect Monsters with paragraphs of text.
You teach **Metalfoes**.

*   **The Main Deck Monsters:** They are **Normal (Vanilla) Pendulum Monsters**.
*   *Goldriver, Silverd, Steelen, Volflame.*
*   They have **No Monster Effects** on the field. Just ATK and DEF.
*   **The Lesson:** "Look at this card. It has no tricks. It relies on **stats** and **scales**. If you can't win with a Vanilla monster, you don't deserve an Effect monster."
*   **The Mechanic:** You destroy *your own* cards to set Spells/Traps directly from the deck.
*   *Lesson:* "Resource Management. Sometimes you have to sacrifice a pawn to secure the position."

### 2. The Budget: "The Common Deck"
In real life (and likely in the anime economy), Metalfoes were famous for being the **"Budget King."**
*   Almost every card in the core deck was a **Common** or **Rare**.
*   While LDS students are buying Secret Rare *Gem-Knights* or *X-Sabers*, you built your entire deck for 3,000 Yen ($30).
*   It fits your "Underpaid Instructor" status perfectly. It looks unassuming, but it hits like a truck.

### 3. The Playstyle: "Pop and Set"
You teach the kids **Consistency**.
*   **Turn 1:**
1.  Scale **Goldriver** (Scale 1) and **Volflame** (Scale 8).
2.  Effect: Pop a card on your field (maybe a floater or a token).
3.  Set **Metalfoes Combination** (Trap) directly from the deck.
4.  **Pendulum Summon:** Summon 3 Normal Monsters.
5.  **Fusion Summon:** Fuse them into **Metalfoes Mithrilium**.
*   **The Lesson:** "I ended my turn with a Boss Monster, a Trap, and a full hand. And I didn't run around the room looking for Action Cards once."

### 4. Why PePe (Performage Performapal) is off-limits
You mentioned **PePe**.
*   **Status:** **God Tier / Banned.**
*   **Lore:** *Performapals* are **Yuya Sakaki’s** (The Protagonist) signature deck. *Performages* are **Dennis McField’s** (A main rival/villain) deck.
*   **Result:** You cannot play PePe. If you play Performapals, you are stealing the Main Character's gimmick (Entertainment Dueling). You are a serious Instructor; you don't do "Entertainment."

---

### Your "Instructor" Deck List: "Heavy Metal Discipline"

This deck is designed to punish students who over-extend and rely on Action Cards.

**Monsters (The Students):**
*   **3x Metalfoes Volflame:** (Level 7, 2400 ATK). The beater.
*   **3x Metalfoes Goldriver:** (Level 4, 1900 ATK). The standard.
*   **3x Metalfoes Silverd:** (Level 3, 1700 ATK).
*   **3x Metalfoes Steelen:** (Level 2).
*   **3x Rescue Rabbit:** (The Teacher's Pet).
*   *Effect:* Banish to summon 2 Normal Monsters from deck. Instantly makes a Rank 4 or Fusion materials.
*   **1x Luster Pendulum, the Dracoslayer:** (The Tuner/Tech).

**Spells (The Syllabus):**
*   **1x Metalfoes Fusion:**
*   *Why it's the best card:* You can fuse with it, then **shuffle it back into the deck to draw 1 card**.
*   *The Lesson:* "Never run out of resources. Always recycle."
*   **3x Summoner's Art:** (Search a Level 5+ Normal Monster). Consistency.
*   **2x Painful Decision:** (Send 1 Normal to grave, add 1 to hand).

**Traps (The Discipline):**
*   **2x Metalfoes Combination:** (Continuous Trap).
*   *Effect:* If a Fusion is summoned, revive a Metalfoes. If sent to grave, search a monster.
*   *Lesson:* "Advantage Generator."
*   **1x Metalfoes Counter:** (Special Summon from deck if a card is destroyed).
*   *Lesson:* "Punish them for breaking your board."

**Extra Deck (The Final Exam):**
*   **2x Metalfoes Mithrilium:** (Non-Destruction Removal).
*   *Effect:* Shuffle 2 Metalfoes from grave to deck -> **Return 1 card on the field to the hand.**
*   *Why it's good:* It spins monsters back. It recycles resources.
*   **1x Metalfoes Alkahest:** (The Thief).
*   *Effect:* **Equip an opponent's Effect Monster to this card.**
*   *The Lesson:* "If you rely too much on that Dragon, I'll just take it."
*   **1x Metalfoes Orichalc:** (Double Piercing Damage).
*   *The Lesson:* "Defense Position won't save you."

---

### Your Teaching Method
**Scenario:** A cocky kid wants to join LDS. He challenges you.
**Kid:** "I summon **X-Saber Wayne**! I grab an Action Card! **Evasion**! I'm invincible!"

**You (Turn 2):**
1.  **Scale Metalfoes.**
2.  **Pop your own card.** Set *Fusion*.
3.  **Pendulum Summon.**
4.  **Activate Metalfoes Fusion.**
*   Fuse **Mithrilium**.
5.  **Mithrilium Effect:** "I target your *X-Saber Wayne*. Return it to the hand."
*   *Kid:* "I use my Action Card! Evasion!"
*   *You:* "Evasion negates *attacks* or *destruction*. Mithrilium **Returns to hand**. Read the card text."
*   *Kid:* "Uhh..."
*   *You:* "Direct Attack."

**Result:** You discipline the kid without even running for an Action Card yourself.
You prove that **Reading Comprehension** > **Athletics**.
