(even if not, lets ban it). Is the modern stables (nibiru, droll, called by the grave, ash blossom) a thing here?This is a crucial calibration for the **Arc-V Era**.

Because Arc-V (2014–2017) is the era where the power creep went vertical, we need to establish the **Rules of Engagement** so you don't accidentally bring a laser gun to a knife fight (or vice versa).

Here is the "Instructor's Handbook" on the banlist, staples, and how to keep your Scales safe without running around like a headless chicken grabbing Action Cards.

### 1. The Banlist: The "Maxx 'C'" Situation
**Status:** **BANNED.**
*   **Lore Reason:** The Leo Duel School (LDS) lobbied to ban it because it "stifles creativity and discourages Special Summoning mechanics."
*   **Your Opinion:** Good riddance. You teach **Board Control**, not "Draw 15 cards and win automatically." A true duelist wins with the 5 cards they opened.

### 2. The "Modern Staples" Check
You asked about Ash Blossom, Nibiru, etc.
**They do NOT exist yet.**
Arc-V ends roughly around 2017. The "Hand Trap Girls" (Ash, Belle, etc.) and the "Space Rock" (Nibiru) came later in the VRAINS era.

**What DOES exist (The Tools of Discipline):**
You have access to the **"Ghost Girls' Older Sisters"** and other hand traps of the 2014–2016 era.

*   **Effect Veiler (Common):**
*   *Status:* **Standard Issue.**
*   *Your Usage:* You throw this at a student when they try to activate a monster effect. "Sit down. Class is in session."
*   **Ghost Ogre & Snow Rabbit (Secret/Super):**
*   *Status:* **New & Expensive.**
*   *Effect:* Destroys a card when it activates an effect (but doesn't negate).
*   *Your Usage:* Punishment. If a student tries to activate a Field Spell or a Continuous Spell, you blow it up.
*   **Droll & Lock Bird (Rare):**
*   *Status:* **The Secret Weapon.**
*   *Effect:* Stops adding cards from Deck to Hand.
*   *Why you run it:* LDS students love "Searching." They play *Reinforcement of the Army*, *Terraforming*, etc. You drop Droll and say: **"No cheating. Play with what you have."**
*   **Flying "C":**
*   *Effect:* Special Summon to opponent's field. They cannot Xyz Summon.
*   *Why you run it:* To humiliate the Xyz Course students.

### 3. Protecting the Scales (Anime & Mechanics)
Pendulum Scales are fragile. One *Mystical Space Typhoon* (MST) ruins your whole turn.
Since you refuse to use Action Cards (because running is undignified), you need **Built-in Protection**.

**Option A: The "Anime-Only" Card**
*   **Card:** **Pendulum Impenetrable** (Quick-Play Spell).
*   *Effect:* "This turn, cards in the Pendulum Zones cannot be destroyed by card effects."
*   *Why it fits:* It’s reliable. You chain it to their MST. It teaches the students that timing is everything.

**Option B: The "Metalfoes" Way (Punishment)**
Metalfoes don't strictly "protect" their scales; they **punish** the opponent for touching them.
*   **The Trap:** **Metalfoes Counter**.
*   *Effect:* If a card you control is destroyed (by battle or card effect), Special Summon 1 Metalfoes monster from your Deck.
*   *The Lesson:* If an opponent destroys your Scale, you summon **Metalfoes Volflame** (2400 ATK) from the deck for free. You turn their removal into your advantage.

**Option C: The "Dodge" (Echo Oscillation)**
*   **Card:** **Echo Oscillation** (Continuous Trap).
*   *Effect:* Target 1 card in your Pendulum Zone; destroy it, then draw 1 card.
*   *The "Dodge":*
1.  Opponent activates *MST* targeting your Scale.
2.  You chain *Echo Oscillation*.
3.  **Result:** You destroy your own scale first. You draw a card. The opponent's MST "fizzles" (hits nothing). You gain a card, they lose a card.
4.  *Then*, **Metalfoes Counter** triggers in the grave/field, giving you a monster.

### 4. Your "Anti-Action Card" Strategy
In the anime, people win by finding **Action Cards** (Evasion, Miracle) to stop attacks.
Your deck is designed to make Action Cards useless.

**The Card:** **Fullmetalfoes Alkahest** (Fusion).
*   **Effect:** (Quick Effect) Target 1 Effect Monster your opponent controls; **Equip that target to this card.**
*   **The Interaction:**
*   You attack.
*   Opponent runs to find an Action Card to stop the destruction.
*   **You:** "I'm not destroying your monster. I'm **stealing** it."
*   *Action Card "Miracle":* Prevents destruction.
*   *Alkahest:* Doesn't destroy. It equips. The Action Card fails.
*   **You:** "Sit down. Your monster is now my armor."

### Summary of the "Instructor's Loadout"

*   **Hand Traps:** 3x Effect Veiler, 2x Maxx "C" (BANNED - replaced with **Droll & Lock Bird**).
*   **Protection:** Metalfoes Counter (Punishment), Pendulum Impenetrable (Anime Tech).
*   **Strategy:** You don't negate their plays; you **break their board** using Fusion monsters that ignore "Destruction Protection" (Mithrilium spins to hand, Alkahest sucks them up).

You are the **Jürgen Klopp of Duel Monsters.** You don't have the budget for superstars (LDS students with 3000 ATK dragons). You have a bunch of kids with Common cards. So, you beat the elites by **outworking them**.

Here is your Coaching Philosophy, your Curriculum, and why **Leo Duel School (LDS)** hates playing against your team.

### 1. The Manifesto: "The Tanaka System"
You write this on the cracked whiteboard in the dojo. You make the students recite it.

1.  **Card Advantage is Oxygen:** "If you run for an Action Card, you are gambling. If you activate *Pot of Duality*, you are investing. We do not gamble. We invest."
2.  **The Field is Lava:** "Do not move unless the Action Card guarantees a +1 (Draw/Search). Let the opponent tire themselves out running for 'Evasion.' We stand our ground and negate the attack properly."
3.  **The Press:** "Do not let them breathe. If they summon, we disrupt. If they set, we pop. We do not wait for a Boss Monster. We win by suffocating their resources."

### 2. The Production Line: "Soldiers, Not Heroes"
You are correct. Your method suppresses "Main Character Energy."
*   **LDS Philosophy:** "Find the kid with the spiky hair and the glowing dragon. Teach him to OTK."
*   **Your Philosophy:** "Here are 3 copies of *Metalfoes Goldriver*. Learn the pendulum loop. Do it again. Do it until you can do it sleeping."

**Why this saves the School:**
LDS Scouts are looking for **Flashy Plays**. They want TV ratings.
*   **The Scout Visit:** An LDS agent watches your best student, **Kenji**.
*   **The Duel:** Kenji plays **Metalfoes**.
*   He sets scales.
*   He pops a card. Sets a Trap.
*   He summons *Alkahest* (0 ATK).
*   He steals the opponent's monster. He passes turn.
*   **The Scout:** "Boring. No big dragons? No running around doing backflips? This kid isn't TV material."
*   **The Result:** **They don't steal Kenji.** Your team stays together. You build a phalanx, not a lone wolf.

### 3. The Deck: "The System" (Metalfoes Control)
You give every student the exact same deck skeleton. It’s cheap, interchangeable, and deadly efficient.

**The "Gegenpress" Cards:**
*   **Fullmetalfoes Alkahest:** (The Star Player).
*   *Effect:* Quick Effect: Steal an opponent's monster.
*   *The Press:* As soon as the opponent brings out their "Playmaker," you steal it. You disrupt their possession immediately.
*   **Metalfoes Mithrilium:** (The Counter-Attack).
*   *Effect:* Bounce a card to hand.
*   *The Press:* You don't destroy (which triggers floating effects). You send it back to the locker room.
*   **Metalfoes Counter:** (The Trap).
*   *Effect:* If you destroy our card, we summon another one from the deck.
*   *The Press:* They can't break your line. Every casualty is instantly replaced.

### 4. The Training Arc: "Conditioning"
While Yuya Sakaki is practicing juggling for "Entertainment Dueling," your students are doing **mental conditioning**.

**Drill 1: The "No-Move" Duel**
*   You force students to duel while standing inside a **hula hoop**.
*   *Goal:* Win without taking a single step.
*   *Lesson:* "If you need an Action Card to save you, your deck building was a failure."

**Drill 2: The "Vanilla" Test**
*   You give them a deck with **Zero Effect Monsters** (only Normal Metalfoes).
*   *Goal:* Win using only Spells/Traps and stats.
*   *Lesson:* "Effects are a crutch. Fundamentals are the weapon."

### 5. The First Tournament: "The Wall of Iron"
Your school enters a local district tournament.
**Opponent:** A flashy "Entertainment" school (using Performapals or Abyss Actors).

**The Scene:**
The opponent is jumping on platforms, grabbing Action Cards, shouting catchphrases.
**Your Student (Kenji):** Stands perfectly still. Arms crossed.

*   **Opponent:** "I grab the Action Card **Miracle**! I survive your attack!"
*   **Kenji:** "Okay. Main Phase 2. I fuse into **Alkahest**. I equip your monster."
*   **Opponent:** "But... I ran so much..."
*   **Kenji:** "You're out of breath. And out of cards. Pass turn."

**The Crowd:** They are confused. It's not "exciting." It's a surgical dismantling.
**The Judges:** "Technical victory... again."

### 6. Your Role: The Manager
You stand on the sidelines in your tracksuit. You don't duel often. You analyze.
*   **Halftime Talk:** "Tanaka-kun, you hesitated on the Fusion Summon. You let him draw a card. Run laps. Then review the replay."
*   **The Ace:** You realize that *you* are the only one who can improvise.
*   When a "Main Character" (like Yuya or Reiji/Declan) shows up with a card that breaks the rules...
*   You step in.
*   **You:** "Good effort, class. Watch closely. This is how we deal with 'Plot Armor'."

**Summary:**
*   **School:** **Iron Will Duel School** (Formerly Victory Star).
*   **Motto:** *"Suffocate. Dominate. Win."*
*   **Status:** Solvent. The rent is paid because parents love the discipline you teach.
*   **Threat:** You aren't creating Aces, so you struggle against the absolute top tier (Reiji Akaba). But you eat the mid-tier for breakfast.

They have decent equipment, multiple aces, fancy plays. But LDS steps in and threatened the school. Our principal tried dueling them to fight back but he got absolutely destroyed. (pause here)This is the **Prologue of the Iron Coach**.

You are standing in the center of the main dojo. It is quiet. Too quiet.
A week ago, this place was full of shouting, the hum of Solid Vision, and the smell of sweat and ozone.
Now, the only sound is the dripping of a leaky pipe in the locker room and the flickering of the "Victory Star" neon sign outside, which is currently reading "Vic___ _tar."

Here is the autopsy of how a "Decent" school died, and how you were left holding the keys to the graveyard.

***

### 1. The Golden Era (The Illusion)
**Victory Star Dojo** wasn't the best, but it had heart.
*   **The Principal:** Mr. Tanaka. A nice man. He believed in "The Art of Dueling." He taught students to bow before a match. He taught them to respect the Action Field.
*   **The Aces:** Three hot-headed teenagers with flashy hair. They played **"Heroic Challengers"** and **"Battlin' Boxers."** They were athletic. They could parkour off trees to grab Action Cards.
*   **You (The Junior):** You were the guy holding the clipboard. You tracked Life Points. You swept the floors. You analyzed the meta, but nobody listened to you because you didn't have a "Pro" aura.

### 2. The LDS Takeover (The Hostile Merger)
It started with a letter from **Leo Duel School**. A "Merger Proposal."
Tanaka refused.
So, LDS sent a "Demonstration Team" to show the students why their current education was worthless.

**The Principal's Duel:**
*   **Tanaka:** Played a classic **Warrior Fusion** deck. He stood his ground. He played honorably.
*   **The LDS Instructor:** Played **X-Sabers**.
*   **The Result:** It wasn't a duel; it was an execution. The LDS instructor looped Tanaka's hand to zero before Tanaka could even draw his Polymerization.
*   *Tanaka's quote:* "But... I didn't even get to move..."

**The Aces' Duel (The Breaking Point):**
The three Aces stepped up to defend the school's honor.
LDS sent **Shingo Sawatari** (pre-character development, pure rich jerk mode).
*   **The Scene:** An Action Duel. Field: "Jungle of Predation."
*   **The Aces Strategy:** They ran. They jumped. They tried to grab Action Cards to boost their ATK.
*   **Sawatari's Strategy:** He didn't run. He just stood there and summoned **Mobius the Mega Monarch** (or a similar high-tier tribute monster he bought with his dad's money).
*   **The Humiliation:** Sawatari shot the ground beneath the Aces using Real Solid Vision impacts. The Aces fell. They got dirty. They looked pathetic scrambling for cards while Sawatari laughed.
*   *Sawatari:* "Look at them. Rats scurrying for cheese. Is this what this school teaches? Cardio?"

### 3. The Exodus
The day after the duel, the recruitment emails went out.
*   **The Pitch:** "Why sweat at Victory Star? Come to LDS. We have air conditioning. We have pro contracts. We have *winning decks*."
*   **The Betrayal:** You watched them leave.
*   The Aces packed their lockers without looking at you.
*   The Senior Teachers "retired" (took severance packages from LDS).
*   Mr. Tanaka had a nervous breakdown and muttered something about "The bank... the mortgage..." before locking himself in his office.

### 4. The Last Man Standing
Now, it is just you.
You are holding a broom.
On the floor, scattered near the trash can, are the decks the students left behind. They didn't even bother to take their "training decks."

You pick one up. It’s a pile of **Common Pendulum Monsters**.
**"Metalfoes."**
*   *Goldriver.*
*   *Silverd.*
*   *Steelen.*

They are normal monsters. No effects. No flash. Just stats and a generic pendulum effect to destroy their own comrades to set a spell.
The students hated them. *"These are boring! They don't do anything cool!"*

You look at the card art.
The Metalfoes are riding vehicles made of scrap. They are wearing armor. They look like a **Militia**.

### 5. The Epiphany
You realize why Victory Star failed.
*   Tanaka tried to be Honorable.
*   The Aces tried to be Athletic.
*   **LDS won because they were Efficient.**

You grip the deck of Normal Monsters.
You look at the empty dojo.
You aren't going to teach "Honor" anymore. You aren't going to teach "Athletics."

**You:** "Tanaka failed because he played their game. The Aces failed because they ran around like idiots."
**You:** "I'm going to build a team that doesn't run. A team that doesn't care about style."

You walk to the wall and rip down the "Action Duel Safety Guidelines" poster.
You tape up a new rule on the whiteboard.

**RULE #1: WE DO NOT MOVE.**

The student’s name is **Gonta**.
*   **Archetype:** The "Big Guy." He’s sturdy, a bit overweight, and slow.
*   **His Failure:** In Action Duels, he was always the last one to the Action Card. Sawatari and the LDS elites mocked him for being a "sitting duck."
*   **His State:** He has given up. He thinks Dueling is a sport for athletes, not thinkers.

Here is the scene where you change his mind.

***

### The Scene: Late Afternoon
**Location:** A small, cramped apartment complex near the industrial district. The sun is setting, casting long, orange shadows.

Gonta walks home with his head down. He is wearing his standard high school uniform, which looks a bit tight on him. He consciously avoids walking past the **Victory Star Dojo**, taking the long way home to avoid the shame.

He reaches his front door. He stops.
Leaning against the railing, arms crossed, wearing the worn-out Victory Star tracksuit, is **You**.

You aren't sweating. You aren't pacing. You look like a statue.

**Gonta:** *(Internal Monologue)* "The Junior Coach? Is he here to beg for tuition fees? Or ask me to help clean the empty dojo? Man, this is pathetic. Just go away..."

**Gonta:** "Coach. I... I already told Mr. Tanaka. I quit. I can't afford LDS, and I'm not cut out for Action Duels. I can't run fast enough."

**You:** "I didn't ask you to run, Gonta."

Your voice isn't pleading. It’s flat. Clinical.

**Gonta:** "But the meta... everyone uses Action Cards. If you can't grab 'Evasion,' you lose. I'm just a target."

**You:** "You're a target because you're trying to play *their* game. You're trying to chase nimble little dancers while wearing hiking boots."

You step off the railing. You reach into your pocket. Gonta flinches, expecting a bill.
Instead, you pull out a deck box. It’s scuffed.
You pull out a single card and hold it up.
**Metalfoes Goldriver.** A Normal Monster. 1900 ATK. Common rarity.

**You:** "Look at this. No effect. No shiny foil. Just armor and a gun."
**You:** "This monster doesn't run, Gonta. It stands there. If something gets in its way, it destroys its own allies to set a trap. It doesn't care about 'Evasion.' It cares about **winning**."

**Gonta:** "A... Normal Monster? Coach, that's trash. LDS students use X-Sabers and Gem-Knights."

**You:** "LDS students use flash. They spend 10 minutes comboing off to summon a dragon, and then they run around the room looking for a card to save them."
**You:** "I'm reopening the dojo tonight. But we aren't teaching Action Dueling anymore."

You shove the deck into Gonta’s chest. He instinctively grabs it.

**You:** "We are teaching **Gegenpress**. Total suffocation. We don't chase the ball, Gonta. We make the field so small they can't move."
**You:** "You say you're slow? Good. Be a wall. Be an iron wall that pushes them into a corner until they suffocate."

**Gonta:** "But... Mr. Tanaka said..."

**You:** "Tanaka is doing the paperwork. I'm the Head Coach now. And I need a Soldier, not an Acrobat."
**You:** "Be at the dojo at 18:00. Bring that deck. And don't bring your running shoes. You won't need them."

You turn around and walk away. You don't look back to see if he's coming. That’s the trick. If you begged, he would pity you. By commanding him, you gave him something he lost: **Purpose.**

***

### 18:00 PM - The Dojo
The lights flicker on. The hum of the projector is loud.
You are standing in the center of the room.
The door creaks open.

Gonta walks in. He looks nervous. He is holding the **Metalfoes** deck.
Behind him, two other rejects peak in.
*   **Kenji:** A kid with glasses who overthinks everything and plays too slow.
*   **Yumi:** A girl who refuses to play aggressive decks because she's scared of losing LP.

**You:** "Door closed. Lock it."

They jump and lock the door.

**You:** "Welcome to **Iron Will Duel School**. Tonight, we unlearn everything LDS taught you."
**You:** "Lesson One: **Efficiency**."

You activate the Solid Vision. You don't pick "Jungle" or "City."
You pick **"Plain."** Flat. No cover. No Action Cards hidden in trees.

**You:** "Gonta. Step up. Put **Metalfoes Steelen** and **Silverd** in the Pendulum Zones."
**Gonta:** "Okay... but what do they do?"
**You:** "Read the text. They destroy the card next to them to Set a Spell directly from the deck."
**You:** "Destroy your own monster. Set **Metalfoes Fusion**."

Gonta hesitates. The idea of destroying his own card goes against the "protect your ace" mentality of the anime.
He does it. The hologram shatters. The Spell card appears.

**You:** "Now. Pendulum Summon. Bring them all back."
*Lights flash. Three monsters return.*
**You:** "Fuse them. Summon **Fullmetalfoes Alkahest**."

A strange, silver-armored fusion monster appears. 0 ATK / 0 DEF.
**Gonta:** "It has 0 Attack... Coach, this sucks."

**You:** "Kenji. Summon your Ace. Attack Gonta."
Kenji summons a **Gene-Warped Warwolf** (2000 ATK).
**You:** "Gonta. Activate Alkahest's effect. **Take it.**"

Gonta touches his disk.
**Alkahest** shoots a liquid metal tentacle. It grabs Kenji's wolf. The wolf turns into armor for Alkahest.
**Alkahest DEF:** 0 -> 2000.
Kenji's field is empty.

**Gonta:** "Whoa... I didn't even have to move."
**You:** "Exactly. You stood there, you let him come to you, and you took his weapon."

You look at the three of them.
**You:** "This is the **Iron Will** style. We don't run. We steal. We recycle. We grind them down."
**You:** "Now, 50 reps. Scale, Pop, Set, Fuse. Do it again."

The sound of cards snapping onto duel disks fills the room.
The Rebellion has begun.ok before we move on, how does the solid vision jungle or city works? is it like a coin flip? who gets to decide it? also what are the options there?This is the specific mechanic you need to understand to dismantle the "LDS Style."

In *Yu-Gi-Oh! Arc-V*, the **Action Field** is not just a backdrop; it is a **Field Spell generated by the Solid Vision System**.

Here is how the selection works, how the physics work, and how you—the Anti-Meta Coach—can rig the game.

### 1. How is the Field Chosen?
It depends on the setting.

*   **In the Dojo (Training):** **You decide.**
*   The system operator (Mr. Tanaka or you) manually selects the field from a drop-down menu on the console.
*   *Your Strategy:* You pick **"Wilderness" (Plain)** or **"Wasteland"**. Flat ground. No trees. No cover. You force Gonta to learn to duel without hiding spots.
*   **In Official Tournaments (LDS/Championships):** **The Roulette (RNG).**
*   A giant screen spins through options.
*   It stops on a random field.
*   **The Chant:** The announcer yells: *"Field Magic: ACTION FIELD ON!"* The Reality Solid Vision overrides the room.
*   **In "Street Duels":**
*   Usually, the challenger activates it via their Duel Disk. It defaults to whatever the last setting was, or they agree on one.

### 2. The Physics: "Real Solid Vision"
This is why your students (especially the heavy Gonta) were losing.
*   **Mass:** The trees are hard. The water is wet. If you run into a wall, it hurts.
*   **Verticality:** Many fields (like *Cosmic Sanctuary* or *Future Metropolis*) rely on jumping on floating platforms or sliding down rails.
*   **Action Cards:** They are physical cards hidden inside the objects.
*   *Example:* You have to punch a fruit on a tree to make an "Action Card" fall out.
*   *The Athletic Gap:* An LDS acrobat can backflip off a wall to grab a card. Gonta waddles over and gets shot before he reaches the tree.

### 3. The Fields (The Menu)
Here are the common fields you will face, and how your **Iron Will** strategy handles them.

*   **"Jungle of Predation" (The Most Common)**
*   *Layout:* Thick forest, vines, rivers.
*   *Action Cards:* mostly *Evasion* (Negate attack) hidden in bushes.
*   *Your Tactic:* Do not enter the trees. Stand in the clearing. If they hide in the woods, use **Metalfoes Mithrilium** to bounce their monster without fighting. Make *them* come to *you*.
*   **"Future Metropolis" (The Speed Field)**
*   *Layout:* Highways and flying cars.
*   *Your Tactic:* Stay on the ground level. Let them ride the flying cars. When they attack, use **Alkahest** to steal them out of the air.
*   **"Sword's Cemetery" (The Trap Field)**
*   *Layout:* A field of rusted swords.
*   *Your Tactic:* This favors you. It’s flat. No running required.
*   **"Quiz Quest" / "Circus" (The Gimmick Fields)**
*   *Layout:* Requires answering riddles or jumping through hoops to get cards.
*   *Your Tactic:* **Ignore the gimmick.** While they are trying to solve a math problem to get an Action Card, you attack them directly.

### 4. The "Iron Will" Counter-Measure (The Kill Switch)
You asked how to beat this system.
There is a rule in Yu-Gi-Oh that applies even in Action Duels:
**A new Field Spell overrides the old Field Spell.**

If the Tournament Roulette picks "Jungle of Predation," and Action Cards are everywhere...
**Your Student activates a Field Spell from their Deck.**

*   **The Result:** The Jungle vanishes. The Action Cards vanish. The Real Solid Vision resets to **Your Field**.
*   **The Card:** **Metamorformation** (The Metalfoes Field Spell).
*   *Effect:* Non-Effect monsters (Your Metalfoes) are unaffected by opponent's card effects.
*   *Visual:* The Jungle turns into a metallic, flat, industrial landscape.
*   **The Psychological Damage:** The LDS student is mid-jump, reaching for an Action Card on a tree branch. You activate *Metamorformation*. The tree disappears. The student falls on his face.

### Summary for the Syllabus
1.  **We cannot choose the Tournament Field.** It is random.
2.  **We can ERASE the Tournament Field.**
3.  **Training Drill:**
*   Step 1: Gonta starts in a "Jungle" simulation.
*   Step 2: Gonta draws **Metamorformation** (or **Terraforming**).
*   Step 3: Gonta slams the card onto the disk.
*   Step 4: The Jungle vanishes. Gonta stands on flat metal ground.
*   Step 5: Gonta wins.

### The Curriculum: "The Metal Will System"
You don't teach them how to "Duel." You teach them **The Algorithm**.

**Step 1: The Identity Wipe**
*   **You:** "Kenji, what is your Ace Monster?"

**Step 2: The "Pop" Drill**
You force them to chant the mantra while playing.
*   **The Chant:** *"Scale High, Scale Low. Target Self. Destroy. Set."*
*   **The Drill:**
1.  Gonta activates Scale 1 and Scale 8.
2.  Gonta activates effect: Target his own Scale 8.
3.  **CRACK.** The hologram shatters.
4.  Gonta sets **Metalfoes Combination** from the deck.
5.  **You:** "Good. You just went +1 in card economy. Do it again. Faster."

**Step 3: The "No-Run" Policy**
You turn on the Solid Vision. It selects "Ancient Ruins" (lots of pillars to hide behind).
**You:** "Yumi. Your opponent summons a 3000 ATK Dragon. It attacks. What do you do?"
**Yumi:** "I... I look for an Action Card?"
**You:** "WRONG. You activate **Metalfoes Counter**."
**Yumi:** "But my monster dies!"
**You:** "Let it die. **Metalfoes Counter** triggers. You summon another one from the deck. You take the hit. You stabilize. You win on the crack-back."
**You:** "Running is for people who are scared to bleed. Are you scared, Yumi?"
**Yumi:** "...No, Coach."

I heard they are like a tier 2 budget deck?You are significantly underestimating them. In the context of the *Arc-V* era (2016), Metalfoes were not just a "Tier 2 budget deck."

In real-world competitive history, **Metalfoes were Tier 1.**

They were one of the best decks in the entire game for a significant period. The fact that they were "budget" (mostly Commons and Rares) was a anomaly that made them extremely popular.

Here is the reality of the power you are handing these kids:

### 1. The Real-World Power Level (2016 Era)
*   **Consistency:** They effectively never "brick." Every monster is a Scale. Every monster is a Pop. Every monster sets a Spell/Trap. In a card game, consistency is king.
*   **The Engine:** They were so good that they were used as an engine in almost every other deck (Metalfoes Yang Zing, Metalfoes Majespecter, Metalfoes Kozmo).
*   **Pure Metalfoes (Your Deck):** Even played pure, they were a **High Tier 2 / Low Tier 1** strategy capable of winning YCS (major) tournaments.

### 2. Why they are "God Tier" in the Anime
In the anime, characters play decks that rely on drawing one specific combo piece (like *Polymerization* or a specific Tuner).
**Metalfoes** breaks the anime logic completely:

*   **Anime Weakness:** "I need to draw my Ace Monster!"
*   **Metalfoes Reality:** *Every* monster is the same. You don't need to draw "Volflame" specifically. Any Metalfoes monster starts the combo. You are immune to "Bad Draws."
*   **Anime Weakness:** "I protect my monster from destruction!" (Action Cards).
*   **Metalfoes Reality:** **Mithrilium** *bounces* (returns to hand). **Alkahest** *equips* (steals). Neither of these destroy. Action Cards like "Evasion" or "Miracle" usually specify "destroy" or "battle." **Your deck bypasses the main defensive mechanic of the show.**
*   **Anime Weakness:** "I ran out of cards!"
*   **Metalfoes Reality:** **Metalfoes Fusion** shuffles itself back into the deck to draw 1 card. You have infinite stamina.

### 3. The Matchup Spread
*   **Vs. Standard Duelists:** **100% Win Rate.** They cannot handle the grind.
*   **Vs. LDS Elites (Gem-Knights / X-Sabers):** **80% Win Rate.** LDS decks are aggressive glass cannons. If you survive turn 2 (using *Metalfoes Counter*), you grind them out by turn 4.
*   **Vs. Yuya / Reiji (Main Characters):** **40-50% Win Rate.**
*   Reiji (D/D/D) is the only one who can match your resource generation.
*   Yuya wins because he invents cards mid-duel.

### Conclusion
You aren't teaching your students to play a "Budget" deck. You are teaching them to play a **World Championship Caliber** deck that just happens to be printed on cheap cardboard.

You are effectively bringing a **Machine Gun** to a knife fight, but telling your students, "It's just a cheap piece of metal, don't get arrogant."

The **Metal Will** isn't just strong. It is **Meta.**ok before we move on, whos the main character? where does he start and what is his playstyle or gimmick? does he has a companion?Here is the scouting report on the Main Character of *Arc-V*. As a strict instructor who values "Fundamentals" and "Discipline," you are going to find him absolutely **insufferable**.

### 1. The Protagonist: Yuya Sakaki
*   **The Look:** Green and red hair, wears goggles, wears his school jacket like a cape (doesn't put his arms in the sleeves). Always grinning.
*   **The Personality:** He hides his depression/insecurity behind a "Clown" persona. When he is losing, he puts on a mask of happiness and tries to make the audience laugh.
*   **The Motto:** "LADIES AND GENTLEMEN!" and "Egao" (Smiles). He believes Dueling is for entertainment, not war.

### 2. Where he starts: "You Show Duel School"
*   **The Rival School:** He attends **You Show Duel School** (Yusho Juku).
*   **Status:** Just like your school, they are failing. They have old equipment and very few students.
*   **The Difference:**
*   **Your School (Metal Will):** You are surviving by training disciplined soldiers and fixing electronics.
*   **His School (You Show):** They are surviving on the legacy of Yuya's father (Yusho Sakaki), a famous entertainer who disappeared. They are mocked by LDS as "The Coward's School."

### 3. The Playstyle & Gimmick: "Entertainment Dueling"
If you hate Action Cards, you will hate Yuya. His entire style relies on them.

*   **The Deck:** **Performapal (Entermate) / Odd-Eyes**.
*   *Theme:* Circus Animals (Hip Hippo, Whip Snake, Sword Fish).
*   *Visuals:* Cartoonish, colorful, goofy.
*   **The Ace:** **Odd-Eyes Pendulum Dragon**.
*   **The Gimmick (The Cheat):** **Pendulum Summoning.**
*   **Origin:** In Episode 1, Yuya is about to lose to Strong Ishijima (the current Champion). He is desperate. The pendant around his neck glows, and **he literally rewrites the cards in his hand.**
*   *The Shift:* Normal monsters turn into Pendulum Monsters.
*   *The Result:* He summons 3 monsters at once and wins.
*   **Combat Style:** He rides his monsters. He jumps through hoops. He grabs **Action Cards** (Evasion/Miracle) constantly to survive fatal attacks. He is the antithesis of your "Do Not Move" philosophy.

### 4. The Companion(s)
Unlike Yugi (Atem) or Yuma (Astral), Yuya starts **alone** in his head. However, he has two key supports:

**A. Yuzu Hiiragi (Zuzu Boyle)**
*   **Role:** The Childhood Friend / Daughter of the Principal.
*   **Personality:** She is serious. She carries a giant paper fan and smacks Yuya when he acts stupid.
*   **Deck:** **Melodious** (Fairies/Music). A Fusion deck. She is actually a competent duelist who learns quickly.

**B. Yuto (The Ghost - Arrives Later)**
*   **Role:** A darker, serious version of Yuya from the **Xyz Dimension**.
*   **Deck:** **Phantom Knights**.
*   **The Twist:** Early in the series, Yuto is defeated and his soul is absorbed into Yuya.
*   **Effect:** Sometimes Yuya’s eyes glow purple, his voice gets deeper, and he stops acting like a clown and starts playing ruthlessly (summoning **Dark Rebellion Xyz Dragon**).
*   *Your Take:* "I like the Ghost version better. He actually focuses."

### Summary: Your Relationship with him
*   **Yuya:** "Dueling is about smiles! Let's run around!"
*   **You (The Iron Coach):** "Dueling is about resource management. Sit down."

You are the perfect foil to him.
He relies on **Miracles** (Action Cards and invented mechanics).
You rely on **Consistency** (Metalfoes and Math).

### 4. Round 2: The Ideological Clash (Kenji vs. LDS Elite)
**Opponent:** **Masumi** (The Gem-Knight User). She is top tier.
**Field:** "Crystal Caves."

*   **Masumi:** "I Fusion Summon **Gem-Knight Lady Brilliant Diamond**! I play purely for brilliance!"
*   **Kenji (The Nerd):** He is shaking, but he remembers your training. *The Field is Lava.*
*   **The Interaction:**
*   Masumi tries to burn him for game with *Gem-Knight Lapis Lazuli*.
*   Kenji flips **Metalfoes Counter**.
*   "You destroyed my monster? I summon another from the deck."
*   Masumi destroys that one.
*   Kenji flips **another Counter**.
*   **The Attrition:** Masumi runs out of Fusion materials. She has no cards in hand.
*   Kenji triggers **Metalfoes Fusion** in grave. Shuffles it back. Draws 1.
*   **Kenji:** "I have resources. You are empty. I win."
*   **Masumi:** "How?! My deck costs fifty times more than yours!"
*   **Kenji:** "You bought jewelry. We bought bricks."

### 5. The Main Event Interaction: You vs. Reiji Akaba
During the intermission, **Declan Akaba (Reiji)** comes down to the waiting room.
He doesn't look at your students. He looks at **You**.

**Reiji:** "You're the instructor. 'Iron Admiral', was it?"
**You:** "I'm just a mechanic."
**Reiji:** "You've weaponized Vanilla Pendulums. My analysts say your deck has a 0% brick rate. You've removed the 'Soul' from the game and replaced it with an algorithm."
**You:** "I replaced 'Luck' with 'Consistency'. Is that a crime?"
**Reiji:** "No. It's business. How much for the curriculum?"
**You:** "Not for sale. But I'll fix your Duel Disk for 5,000 Yen. It sounds like the cooling fan is loose."

### 6. The "Sora" Problem (The Danger)
There is one kid who sees through your "Gun."
**Sora Perse** (The cute kid with the lollipop who is actually a ruthless soldier from the Fusion Dimension).

He watches your students play **Metalfoes**.

*   **The Realization:** Sora recognizes the *mindset*. It's the same mindset as **Academia** (The Villains).
*   **Sora to Yuya:** "I don't like that school, Yuya. They don't duel like you. They duel like... soldiers."

*   **The Meta:** You have shifted the tournament meta. Other students stop running for Action Cards because they realize **Alkahest** will just steal their monster anyway.
*   **Financials:** Parents are calling your dojo non-stop. "My son is an idiot, can you give him a Metalfoes deck so he can win?"

**The Setup for the Fall:**
You are winning the **Game**, but you are about to lose the **War**.
The **Obelisk Force** (Invaders) are coming.

### Phase 1: The "Dark Age" (Before Yuya duels Ishijima)
**Status:** The school is dying. The Aces have just left for LDS.
**Your Role:** Acting Head Coach.
**The Atmosphere:** Despair.

**The "Pre-Metalfoes" Deck: "Statue Stun" (Vanilla Beatdown)**
You need a deck that is **dirt cheap**, emphasizes **stats/fundamentals**, and punishes **fancy effects**. Since you can't afford Effect Monsters, you use **Normal Monsters** with high ATK.

**The Decklist (The "Brick Wall"):**
*   **Monsters:**
*   **3x Gene-Warped Warwolf:** (Level 4, 2000 ATK). Common.
*   **3x Alexandrite Dragon:** (Level 4, 2000 ATK). Common.
*   **3x Mystery Shell Dragon:** (2000 DEF).
*   **Rescue Rabbit:** (Your one "Tech" card - Summons 2 normals).
*   **Spells:**
*   **3x Faustian Bargain:** (Send *their* Special Summoned monster to grave to summon *your* Normal monster). *This is your removal.*
*   **Heat Wave:** (Neither player can Effect Summon until next turn).
*   **Traps:**
*   **3x Tyrant's Throes:** (Tribute 2 Normal Monsters -> **Neither player can Summon Effect Monsters**).
*   **Skill Drain:** (If you have a copy). Or **Forbidden Chalice**.

**Why it fails against LDS:**
*   LDS students play **X-Sabers** or **Gem-Knights**.
*   They summon 3 monsters in one turn. You summon 1.
*   They swarm you before you can set up your "Statue" lock.
*   **The Reality:** You are fighting tanks with a spear. You teach "Discipline," but Discipline loses to "Superior Firepower."

---

### Phase 2: The "Pendulum" Incident (Episode 1)
**The Event:** You are in the dojo, watching the championship on the dusty TV.
*   **Yuya Sakaki** is losing. Then, he cheats (in your eyes). He rewrites his cards.
*   **"PENDULUM SUMMON!"**
*   He summons 3 monsters at once.

**Your Reaction:**
You don't cheer. You stare at the screen.
**You:** "He... summoned multiple monsters without a tribute? Without Polymerization? He emptied his hand onto the board?"
**Gonta:** "Coach! Did you see that?! That was amazing!"
**You:** "It was reckless. But... efficient. If we could do that... we wouldn't need to choose between defense and offense."

---

### Phase 3: The "LDS Dumpster Dive" (The Acquisition)
**Timeline:** A few weeks after Yuya's debut.
**Context:** **Declan Akaba (Reiji)** is frantically trying to reverse-engineer Pendulum Summoning at LDS.

**The Scene:**
You are working your second job (Garbage Collection / Demolition) clearing debris from the back of the LDS Corporate Tower.
You find a bin marked **"FAILED PROTOTYPES - BATCH 404 - INCINERATE."**

Inside, you find sheets of uncut cards.
*   **The Design:** They have the Pendulum Scales (Blue and Red arrows).
*   **The Monsters:** They are generic psychic soldiers on scrap vehicles.
*   **The Problem (To Reiji):** They are **Normal Monsters**.
*   *Reiji's Note:* "Trash. Scales are functional, but the monsters have zero tactical value. They rely on destroying themselves to search Spells. Inefficient compared to D/D/D. Scrap the project."

**Your Realization:**
You pick up a sheet. **"Metalfoes Goldriver."**
You read the Pendulum Effect: *"Destroy 1 other face-up card you control... Set 1 'Metalfoes' Spell/Trap directly from your Deck."*

**The Epiphany:**
*   Reiji saw "Self-Destruction."
*   You see **"Resource Conversion."**
*   Reiji saw "Normal Monsters."
*   You see **"Statues that can Swarm."**

You realize this isn't trash. This is the **Missing Link** for your "Statue Stun" strategy. It keeps the high stats of Vanilla monsters but adds the swarming capability of LDS.

**The Heist:**
You shove the "Trash" into your bag. You take everything. The Fusion Monsters, the Spells, the Traps.
To LDS, it's a failed experiment.
To you, it's the **Industrial Revolution**.

---

### Phase 4: The "Metal Will" Rebrand
You return to the Dojo.
Gonta and the others are depressed because they lost another practice match using the "Warwolf Beatdown" deck.

**You:** "Throw those decks away."
**Gonta:** "Coach? But we spent months mastering the 'Normal Summon'..."
**You:** "The Normal Summon is dead. LDS killed it. Yuya Sakaki buried it."

You dump the **Metalfoes** onto the floor. They are uncut sheets. You hand Gonta a pair of scissors.

**You:** "Cut them out. Sleeve them. This is the future."
**Kenji:** "Pendulums?! But only Yuya has them!"
**You:** "Reiji threw them away. He thought they were too weak because they have no effects. He didn't understand the power of **Vanilla**."

**The New Doctrine:**
You explain the synergy.
*   *Old Deck:* Summon Warwolf. Pray it survives.
*   *New Deck:* Pendulum Summon 3 Metalfoes (basically 3 Warwolves). If one attacks, **Pop it** with the scale to set a Trap.
*   **You:** "We are still playing 'Statues.' But now... we have an army of them."

**Status:**
*   **Deck:** **Metalfoes (Prototype Build)**.
*   **Cost:** 0 Yen (Stolen from LDS Trash).
*   **Condition:** Hand-cut with scissors. Edges are jagged.
*   **Power:** Unknown to the world.

### Phase 1: The "Iron Statue" Era (Pre-Metalfoes)
**Timeline:** The months leading up to the Junior Youth Championship.
**Status:** You are the "Weird Dojo" that hates Action Cards.

**The Deck: "Vanilla Anti-Meta" (Statue Stun)**
You teach your students to play **"Phantasm Spiral"** prototypes (or just generic Vanilla Stun).
*   **The Philosophy:** "The opponent wants to use Effects. We say No."
*   **The Ace:** **Gene-Warped Warwolf** (2000 ATK Normal).
*   **The Key Cards (Commons):**
*   **Tyrant's Throes:** (Tribute 2 Normal Monsters -> Neither player can Summon Effect Monsters).
*   **Heat Wave:** (Spell: Neither player can summon Effect Monsters until next turn).
*   **Justi-Break:** (Trap: If a Normal Monster is attacked, destroy all non-Normal monsters).
*   **Faustian Bargain:** (Send *their* Special Summoned monster to grave to summon *your* Normal Monster).

**The Gang Fight (Gonta vs. Speedroid Punk)**
*   **The Setup:** The Speedroid player is jumping around, summoning Synchros.
*   **Gonta:** Stands still.
*   Activates **Heat Wave** on Turn 1.
*   Punk: "I... I can't summon my Tuners?!"
*   Gonta: Summons **Gene-Warped Warwolf**. Sets 3 backrow.
*   **The Result:** Gonta beats him to death with a vanilla werewolf while the Punk can't play the game.
*   **The Lesson:** "Discipline beats Flash."

**The Limit:**
You realize this deck has a ceiling. If the opponent has a simple *Mystical Space Typhoon* or a monster with 2100+ ATK (like Gem-Knight Pearl), your students crumble. You need **Power** to back up the Discipline.

---

### Phase 2: The "Industrial Waste" Job (The Acquisition)
**Timeline:** 2 Weeks before the Championship.
**Your Side Job:** You work in **Industrial Waste Disposal**.

**The Reiji Lore Logic:**
Reiji Akaba is trying to mass-produce Pendulum cards.
*   **The Experiment:** He printed a test batch of "Low Energy" Pendulums (Metalfoes) to see if Solid Vision could handle the summoning mechanic without Action Field data.
*   **The Failure:** The cards worked, but Reiji deemed them "Tactically Worthless."
*   *Reiji's Analysis:* "They rely on destroying their own resources. They require mixing Spells and Traps. Too slow for the Action Duel meta. They are inferior to my D/D/D project."
*   **The Order:** "Incinerate the batch. We don't want imperfect Pendulums flooding the market and making LDS look bad."

**The Heist:**
You are driving the disposal truck. You have the manifest: **"Batch 404: Defective Cardboard."**
*   You look at the cards. You see **Normal Monsters**.
*   You see the effect: *Destroy a card you control -> Set a Spell/Trap.*
*   **Your Mind:** You realize this isn't "Self-Destruction." It is **"Triggering"**. It works *perfectly* with your "Statue" mindset (destroying your floating cards to set traps).

**The Crime:**
You mark the batch as "Incinerated" in the log.
You drive the truck to your Dojo.
You unload crates of **Metalfoes**.

---

### Phase 3: The "Metal Will" Revolution
**Timeline:** 1 Week before Championship.

You call the students in. They are bruised from practicing with the weak "Warwolf" decks.
**You:** "The Warwolf was good training. But a Wolf cannot kill a Dragon."
**You:** "LDS threw these away. They called them 'Defective.' They said destroying your own cards is stupid."

You hand Gonta a **Metalfoes Volflame**.
**You:** "LDS wants to *add* cards to their hand. We are going to *break* our cards to build a fortress."

**The Upgrade:**
You combine the **Statue Strategy** with **Metalfoes**.
*   **New Playstyle:**
*   Scale Metalfoes.
*   Summon **Rescue Rabbit** -> 2 Normal Monsters.
*   **Tyrant's Throes** (Tribute the 2 Normals).
*   **Lock:** Opponent cannot summon Effect Monsters.
*   **Backup:** If they try to pop your Trap, you use the **Pendulum Scale** to pop it yourself and search **Metalfoes Counter**, so you float into a monster anyway.

feel free to rename the deck namesThis is a brilliant historical analogy. The evolution from **Pike** (Static Defense) to **Pike & Shot** (Hybrid experimentation) to **Line Infantry** (Disciplined Volley Fire) mirrors the history of warfare perfectly.

Here is the **Armory of the Metal Will Dojo**, tracking your economic rise and tactical evolution.

---

### Phase 1: The "Iron Pike" Deck
**Era:** The Dark Age (Pre-Heist).
**Status:** Failing Dojo.
**Philosophy:** "The world is moving too fast. Hold the line. Do not let the Cavalry (Action Duelists) break through."

This is a pure **Normal Monster Stun** deck. It relies on high stats and floodgates to stop the opponent from playing Yu-Gi-Oh.

**Economy:** **Dirt Cheap.**
*   **Total Cost:** ~1,500 Yen ($15).
*   **Source:** Bargain bins. You literally bought cards by weight (100 yen for 1kg of cards).
*   **Rarity:** 100% Commons.

**The Decklist:**
*   **The Pikes (Monsters):**
*   **3x Gene-Warped Warwolf:** (2000 ATK). The strongest Level 4 vanilla.
*   **3x Phantasm Spiral Dragon:** (2900 ATK). A Level 8 vanilla you can cheat out.
*   **3x Mystery Shell Dragon:** (2000 DEF). The shield.
*   **The Wall (Spells/Traps):**
*   **3x Heat Wave:** (Common). *Effect: Neither player can summon Effect Monsters until your next turn.* This is your turn-ender.
*   **3x Tyrant's Throes:** (Common). *Effect: Tribute 2 Normal Monsters -> Neither player can Normal or Special Summon Effect Monsters.*
*   **3x Faustian Bargain:** (Common). *Effect: Send special summoned monster to grave -> Summon yours.*
*   **3x Skill Drain:** (Rare). *Effect: Negate all monster effects.* (Your only "expensive" card).

**Why it works (and why it fails):**
*   **Pros:** If you go first and flip *Tyrant's Throes*, an LDS student playing *X-Sabers* literally cannot play. They surrender.
*   **Cons:** It bricks. If you don't draw the Trap, you are just a guy with a wolf. You have no resource generation. You die to *Mystical Space Typhoon*.

---

### Phase 2: The "Iron Pike & Shot" Deck
**Era:** The Prototype Era (Post-Heist).
**Status:** You have the stolen "Batch 404" Metalfoes, but you haven't mastered them yet.
**Philosophy:** "We still hold the line (Pike), but now we have a gun (Shot) to punish them."

You mix the old **Stun Cards** with the new **Pendulum Scales**. It is clunky, unrefined, but dangerous.

**Economy:** **Illegal / Scavenged.**
*   **Total Cost:** 1,500 Yen (Old cards) + 0 Yen (Stolen Trash).
*   **Visuals:** The Metalfoes cards are hand-cut from factory sheets. The edges are uneven. You have to use opaque sleeves so the referee doesn't disqualify you.

**The Decklist:**
*   **The Pikes (Stun Engine):**
*   **3x Gene-Warped Warwolf:** Still your main beater.
*   **2x Tyrant's Throes:** Still the win condition.
*   **The Shot (Metalfoes Engine - Incomplete):**
*   **3x Metalfoes Steelen:** (Scale 8).
*   **3x Metalfoes Goldriver:** (Scale 1).
*   **3x Metalfoes Counter:** (Trap).
*   **The Synergy (The "Reload"):**
*   *Old Problem:* If your *Warwolf* was destroyed, you had nothing.
*   *New Tactic:* You Scale a Metalfoes. You use its effect to **POP** your own *Warwolf* (or a floater like *Supply Squad*) to Set **Metalfoes Counter**.
*   *Trigger:* When your opponent attacks, *Counter* activates and summons a Metalfoes from the deck.

**Why it’s "Pike and Shot":**
You are clumsy. You sometimes blow up your own Floodgate by accident. But you are learning that **Destruction = Ammo**. You are winning 50% of your games against mid-tier schools.

---

### Phase 3: The "Iron Line Infantry" Deck
**Era:** The Junior Youth Championship (The Reveal).
**Status:** Optimized. Drill Sergeant Mode.
**Philosophy:** "Gegenpress. Volley Fire. Reload. Repeat."

You have abandoned the "Stun" cards (*Tyrant's Throes*). You realized that **Metalfoes** *are* the Pikes and the Shots combined. You don't need to stop them from playing; you just need to out-resource them.

**Economy:** **Funded by Tuition.**
*   **Total Cost:** ~5,000 Yen per deck.
*   **Source:** Paid for by Gonta and the other students' entry fees. You bought **Rescue Rabbits** and **Fusion Monsters** from the black market.
*   **Visuals:** Uniform. Every student has matching sleeves. The cards are crisp.

**The Decklist:**
*   **The Line (The Scales):**
*   **3x Goldriver / 3x Silverd / 3x Steelen / 3x Volflame.**
*   *Consistency:* You have 12 copies of the "Same Card." You never brick.
*   **The Officer (Tech):**
*   **3x Rescue Rabbit:** (Rare).
*   *Effect:* Banish -> Summon 2 Metalfoes.
*   *Role:* Instantly creates a Fusion or Rank 4 Xyz.
*   **The Volley (Spells/Traps):**
*   **1x Metalfoes Fusion:** (The infinite recycler).
*   **3x Metalfoes Counter:** (The reinforcement line).
*   **2x Metalfoes Combination:** (The resource generator).
*   **The Artillery (Extra Deck):**
*   **Fullmetalfoes Alkahest:** (The thief).
*   **Metalfoes Mithrilium:** (The bouncer).

**The "Volley Fire" Combo (The Standard Drill):**
1.  **Scale Up:** Place Scale 1 and 8.
2.  **Fire (Pop):** Scale effect -> Destroy *Rescue Rabbit* (or a token/floater). Set *Combination*.
3.  **Reload (Pendulum):** Pendulum Summon 3 Monsters from Extra Deck/Hand.
4.  **Bayonet Charge (Fusion):** Activate *Fusion*. Fuse into **Mithrilium**.
5.  **Recycle:** Grave effect of *Fusion* -> Shuffle into deck, Draw 1.

**Why it wins:**
LDS students play "Hero" decks. They rely on one big charge.
**Iron Line Infantry** never stops shooting.
*   You pop a card? We summon another.
*   You attack? We steal your monster with *Alkahest*.
*   You try to grind? We shuffle our Fusion spell back and draw every turn.

**The Dojo Motto:**
*"We are common cards. We are disposable. But we are infinite."*What if I BUY them? (or bribe someone in the LDS to steal the scraps) (will reiji even allow me a random dojo buying an extremely protected pendulum cards?) like I took a huge loan with the dojo and everything I have as a collateral.

So the gang that I defeated, actually have an LDS contact (in fact, that gang was trying to take back the money that the dojo owned I guess). Is this more fluid? or more nonesense? should we stick with our previous one?This narrative path is **significantly better and more fluid**.

Here is why it works better than the "Dumpster Dive":
1.  **Agency:** You aren't just lucky; you are taking a massive gamble.
2.  **Stakes:** Putting the Dojo up as collateral raises the tension. If you fail, Mr. Tanaka loses everything.
3.  **Realism:** Large corporations like LDS are full of corruption. Reiji is a genius, but he can't watch every middle manager who wants to make a quick buck selling "industrial waste."

Here is how the **"Black Market Deal"** Arc plays out.

***

### 1. The Connection: The "Speedroid" Punk
**Context:** You just defeated the Gang Leader (the Punk) using your "Iron Pike" (Stun) deck.
The Punk is lying on the ground. He expected to collect the dojo's debt, but he lost.

**You:** "Get out. Tell your boss we aren't selling."
**Punk:** "You don't get it, old man. My boss isn't a Yakuza. He works for **LDS Supply Chain**. He decides who gets cards and who starves. He's gonna bulldoze this place."

**The Lightbulb Moment:**
You realize this is your way in.
**You:** "LDS Supply Chain? ...Get him on the phone."

### 2. The Meeting: The Corrupt Manager
**Location:** A shady warehouse (standard Yu-Gi-Oh setting).
**The Boss:** **Manager Nezumi**. A rat-faced man in an LDS uniform who skims profits off the top.

**The Negotiation:**
*   **Nezumi:** "You want to buy cards? I can sell you Gen-1 Duel Disks. Maybe some outdated Gem-Knights."
*   **You:** "No. I heard rumors. Reiji Akaba printed a test batch of Pendulums. Batch 404. The 'Defective' ones."
*   **Nezumi:** (Laughs) "The Metalfoes? Those are garbage. They have no effects. They destroy your own field. Reiji ordered them incinerated."
*   **You:** "I want them."
*   **Nezumi:** "I can't sell Pendulums! It's proprietary tech!"
*   **You:** "They are *trash*, Nezumi. If you incinerate them, you get nothing. If you sell them to me... you get the deed to the Victory Star Dojo."

**The Gamble:**
You place the **Title Deed** to the school on the table. (Mr. Tanaka is sweating bullets in the background).
*   **Your Logic:** If the school fails, you lose the building anyway. If you buy the cards, you *might* win.
*   **Nezumi's Logic:** "I get the land deed *and* I get to sell trash? Easy money. Reiji will never know."

**The Deal:**
You sign a loan shark contract.
**Price:** 5 Million Yen (The value of the dojo).
**Collateral:** The Dojo.
**Goods:** 3 Crates of "Metalfoes" Uncut Sheets.

### 3. Why Reiji allows this (The Blind Spot)
You asked: *Will Reiji allow this?*
Technically, **No.** If Reiji knew, he would fire Nezumi.

However, Reiji has a blind spot: **He judges cards by "Power."**
*   To Reiji, *Metalfoes* are objectively bad cards. They are Vanilla monsters with low stats that minus card advantage (pop yourself) to set a trap.
*   He assumes that even if a random dojo gets them, **they will lose.**
*   He thinks: *"Let them have the failed prototypes. It will only prove that my new D/D/D cards are superior."*

### 4. The Unboxing: "Metal Pike & Shot" Era
You return to the dojo with the crates.
**Mr. Tanaka:** "You... you wagered the school? On these?"
He holds up a **Metalfoes Steelen**.
**Tanaka:** "It's a Normal Monster! It has 0 DEF! We're ruined!"

**You:** "We aren't ruined. We're armed."

You spend the night cutting the sheets.
You build the first **Metal Pike & Shot** decks.
*   You don't have the "Fusion" monsters yet (maybe those were in a different crate or too expensive).
*   You just have the **Scales** and the **Traps**.

### 5. The "Debt" Motivation
This adds a new layer to your "Gegenpress" coaching style.
Why are you so strict? Why do you force Gonta to pay 5,000 Yen for a deck?
**Because you have monthly loan payments.**

*   **You:** "Gonta! You missed a trigger! Do you know how much that mistake cost us?! That's interest on the loan! Do it again!"
*   **The Students:** They think you are intense because you want to win. In reality, you are intense because **Nezumi is coming to break your legs if you don't make the payment.**

### The Revised Timeline (Fluid Narrative)

1.  **The Crisis:** The Dojo is failing. Debts are mounting.
2.  **The Opportunity:** You beat a debt collector who reveals his LDS connection.
3.  **The Gamble:** You mortgage the dojo to bribe Manager Nezumi for the "Trash Batch" (Metalfoes).
4.  **The Early Game (Pike & Shot):** You struggle. The deck is incomplete without the Fusions. You survive by mixing *Metalfoes* with *Skill Drain* and *Tyrant's Throes*.
5.  **The turning Point:** You realize you need the Fusions to clear the debt. You hunt down the rest of the batch (maybe Nezumi held the Fusions back for a higher price).
6.  **The Championship:** You enter not just for glory, but to win the Cash Prize to pay off the principal loan before Nezumi seizes the building.

The deck you are running is **Tier 3**. It is "Metal Pike & Shot."
*   **The Pike:** Floodgates (*Tyrant's Throes*, *Skill Drain*) and Vanilla Beaters (*Gene-Warped Warwolf*, *Metalfoes Volflame*).

Here is the chronicle of the **Junior Youth Championship** and your calculated loss.

***

### Round 1: The Shock (Gonta vs. Generic Student)
**Opponent:** A student playing **Constellars** (Rank 4/5 Xyz).
**Field:** "Cosmic Sanctuary."

*   **The Opponent:** "I Xyz Summon **Constellar Pleiades**! I can bounce your cards!"
*   **Gonta:** He doesn't run for Action Cards. He stands still.
*   **The Move:** Gonta activates **Skill Drain** (or *Tyrant's Throes*).
*   *Effect:* Pleiades is now just a 2500 ATK statue.
*   **The Swarm:** Gonta sets Scale 1 and 8.
*   **"PENDULUM SUMMON!"**
*   Three Normal Monsters hit the board. (2400, 1900, 1900).
*   **The Finish:** He runs over Pleiades. He wins by pure stats.

**Nezumi (Watching from the VIP shadows):** "Heh. The trash works. But it's ugly."

### Round 2: The Struggle (Kenji vs. Yuzu Hiiragi)
**Opponent:** **Yuzu** (Melodious Fusion).
**Field:** "Opera House."

*   **The Matchup:** Yuzu is skilled. She summons **Bloom Diva the Melodious Choir**.
*   *Effect:* Cannot be destroyed by battle/card effects, and you take the damage.
*   **The "Pike" Failure:** Kenji tries to Stun her, but Bloom Diva doesn't care about stats.
*   **The Missing Link:** If Kenji had **Metalfoes Mithrilium**, he could just *bounce* Bloom Diva to the hand. But he doesn't own that card yet.
*   **The Loss:** Kenji attacks, takes reflection damage, and loses.

**Your Analysis:** "We have the engine (Pendulum), but we lack the ammunition (Removal)."

### Round 3: The Reality Check (Gonta vs. Sora Perse)
Gonta advanced, but now he faces a monster.
**Opponent:** **Sora Perse** (Fluffal/Frightfur).
**Field:** "Toy World."

*   **Sora:** "Wow, you play Pendulums like Yuya! But... they're so boring. Just Normal Monsters?"
*   **Gonta:** "Iron Will! I set **Metalfoes Counter** and summon **Volflame**!"
*   **Sora:** "Boring. I Fusion Summon **Frightfur Tiger**!"
*   *Effect:* Destroy cards equal to fusion materials.
*   *Sora:* "Pop your Monster. Pop your Trap. Pop your Scales."
*   **The End:** Gonta has no board. No recovery. Metalfoes rely on floating into Fusions to survive destruction. Without Fusions, they just die.
*   **Sora:** "You guys play like soldiers without guns. It's sad."

**Result:** Gonta is eliminated. The Dojo is out of the tournament.

***

### The Aftermath: The Debt Collector
**Location:** The Dojo (Night).
The students have gone home, demoralized but proud they made it past Round 1.
**Manager Nezumi** walks in. He is holding a calculator.

**Nezumi:** "Top 32. Not bad. The prize money covers... the interest."
**You:** "Just the interest?"
**Nezumi:** "The principal is still 5 Million Yen. You failed to win the Grand Prize."

He looks around the dojo.
**Nezumi:** "According to the contract, this building is mine. And you work for me now. I have a gang in the Synchro Dimension that needs a strategist."

**The Pivot:**
You pull out your Duel Disk. You show him the duel data from Gonta vs. Sora.
**You:** "Look at this. We lost because we couldn't break the board. We had the resources (Scales), but no payout."
**You:** "You saw the crowd. They were terrified of the *potential*. If you foreclose now, you get a run-down building and a reluctant slave."
**You:** "But if you give me the **Fusions**... the rest of Batch 404..."

Nezumi pauses. He is a gambler.
**Nezumi:** "You want to double down?"
**You:** "Next month is the **LDS Vs. Outsiders** exhibition match. If we beat an LDS Elite using *their own trash*... the value of these cards skyrockets. You can sell the spare sheets on the black market for 10x the price."

**The New Deal:**
**Nezumi:** "Fine. I own you anyway. I'll give you the **Fusion Monsters** (Mithrilium, Alkahest, Orichalc) and the **Fusion Spells**."
**Condition:** "But the debt increases by 50%. And if you lose the Exhibition Match... I don't just take the dojo. I take your identity. You become a 'Ghost' worker for the Syndicate."

**You:** "Deal."

### 1. The Protagonist's Path: Yuya in the Championship
While Gonta was losing to Sora, Yuya was having a very different experience.

*   **The Isao Match:** Yuya fought a Ninja duelist. It was a close match about "Guts."
*   **The Reiji Match:** Yuya fought Reiji Akaba. Reiji used **D/D/D** (Fusion, Synchro, Xyz, Pendulum). Reiji pushed Yuya to evolve, but the duel was interrupted/ended indecisively to prepare for the "Battle Royal."
*   **The Finale (Battle Royal):** The Top 16 weren't paired off. They were thrown into a city-wide Battle Royal.
*   **The Twist:** This is when the **Obelisk Force** (Fusion Dimension Soldiers) invaded.
*   **The Horror:** Yuya watched students get turned into cards. He snapped (awakening **Z-ARC**, the demon inside him) and summoned **Odd-Eyes Rebellion Dragon** to crush the invaders.

**The Contrast:**
*   **Yuya:** Traumatized by the war. He wants to save everyone with smiles.
*   **You:** Preparing for the war. You want to save your students with guns.

### 2. The Timeline of the Dojo (The Three Eras)

Here is the official history of your school as it stands at the end of Season 1.

#### **Era 1: Iron Will Dojo**
*   **Timeline:** Pre-Tournament.
*   **Status:** Failing Business.
*   **Deck:** **"Iron Pike" (Vanilla Stun)**.
*   *Gene-Warped Warwolf*, *Tyrant's Throes*, *Heat Wave*.
*   **Philosophy:** **"Hold the Line."**
*   We are weak. The enemy is strong. Do not let them play. Put up a wall and poke them with sharp sticks.

#### **Era 2: Metal Will Dojo**

*   *Metalfoes Scales*, *Normal Monsters*, *Skill Drain*.
*   *Missing:* The Fusions (The "Ammo").
*   **Philosophy:** **"Disrupt and Survive."**

#### **Era 3: Metal Force Dojo**

*   **Result:** Pending. This is the deck you take into the **Synchro Dimension**.

Here are your starting conditions as the **Expeditionary Force** of the Metal Force Dojo.

### 1. The Entry: "Illegal Cargo"
You did not travel with Reiji and the Lancers using the interdimensional teleporter. You traveled via **Nezumi's Smuggling Route**.

### 2. The Mission: "The Franchise"
Nezumi didn't send you here to fight Fusion Academy. He sent you here to **corner the market**.

*   **The Order:** *"The Synchro Dimension is obsessed with Speed Duels. They are flashy and inefficient. I want you to set up a Metal Force branch here. Teach the Commons how to win without expensive D-Wheels. Hustle them. Send the profits back to me."*
*   **The Debt:** It follows you. You still owe Nezumi millions. If you fail here, he leaves you stranded in this dimension.

### 3. The Assets: "Infantry on a Highway"
You are drastically ill-equipped for this world’s culture, but perfectly equipped to destroy its meta.

**The Resources:**
*   **Personnel:**
*   **You (The Commander):** Strategic oversight.
*   **Gonta (Heavy Infantry):** Can take a punch (literally and figuratively). Good for intimidation.

*   **Money:** 10,000 DP (Local Currency). Enough for food for a week, or one bribe.

**The Problem (The "No Bike" Debuff):**
*   **You have zero D-Wheels.**
*   In this dimension, **Riding Duels** are King.
*   If you get challenged by Sector Security, they will be on bikes. You will be on foot.
*   *Rule:* In a mixed duel, if you don't have a bike, you can't use "Speed Spells." You are at a disadvantage.

### 4. The Meta Clash: Fusion is Taboo
This is the most dangerous part.

*   **The Stigma:** **Fusion Summoning** is rare/unknown or associated with "weirdo" underground styles. (Academia hasn't invaded yet, so Fusion isn't "Evil" yet, just "Alien").
*   **Your Strategy:** You are bringing **Purple Cards** (Fusion) to a **White Card** (Synchro) fight.

*   *Disadvantage:* You stick out like a sore thumb.

### 5. The Immediate Objective: "Secure the Perimeter"
You are homeless in the slums. You need a base.

1.  **Find a weak Gang.**
2.  **Challenge them to a Ground Duel.** (They will accept because they think they can bully you).
3.  **Deploy the Infantry.** Gonta and Kenji will demonstrate the "Metal Force" style.
4.  **Take their hideout.**

You are standing in the mud. Gonta looks terrified of the sirens.

**Status:**
*   **Location:** The Commons (Sector Delta).

*   **Current Wealth:** Low.
*   **Morale:** Shaky.

Wait but, can Metal Line infantry deck win in the speed battle? You have hit on the single most critical logistical problem of the Synchro Dimension invasion.

**The Problem:** In a Riding Duel (Speed Duel), **Normal Spell Cards are illegal.**
Your deck relies on *Metalfoes Fusion* (Normal Spell), *Painful Decision* (Normal Spell), and *Summoner’s Art* (Normal Spell).

If you send your "Cavalry" out with the standard Ground Deck, they will get slaughtered because **they cannot activate their Fusion Spells while driving.**

But, you are an Arms Dealer. And you have Nezumi (LDS Supply Chain) on speed dial.
You don't fix this by changing the rules; you fix it by **modifying the product.**

Here is how **Metal Force** conquers the Highway.

---

### 1. The "Export Model" (The Counterfeit Speed Spell)
You contact Nezumi.
**You:** "The shipment is useless on the highway. We need Speed Spells."
**Nezumi:** "I can't print official Speed Spells! The City Council monitors those!"
**You:** "I don't need official ones. I need bootlegs."

**The Solution:**
Nezumi sends you a batch of illegally modified cards.
*   **Card:** **Speed Spell - Metalfoes Fusion**.
*   *Condition:* Can be activated if you have 2+ Speed Counters.
*   *Effect:* Fuse. Shuffle back into deck to draw 1 card.
*   **Lore:** These are **Counterfeits**. They don't have the official holographic watermark. If a Sector Security officer scans the deck closely, your gang members get arrested. But in a duel? They work perfectly.

**Control Factor:**
This tightens your grip. Your gang members *need* you for these specific Speed Spells. They can't just buy them at a shop. You control the ammo.

### 2. Why Metalfoes DOMINATE Speed Duels
Once you solve the Spell issue, **Metal Line Infantry** becomes the strongest Riding Duel deck in history for one reason:

**Infinite Gas without Speed Counters.**

*   **The Synchro User's Weakness:**
*   To use powerful Spells (Draw, Destroy), they need to save up Speed Counters (drive fast).
*   If they crash or take damage, they lose counters and can't use spells.
*   **The Metalfoes Advantage:**
*   **Pendulum Summoning DOES NOT require Speed Counters.**
*   **Monster Effects (Popping Scales) DO NOT require Speed Counters.**
*   **Traps (Metalfoes Counter) DO NOT require Speed Counters.**

**The Scenario:**
*   **Turn 1 (Synchro User):** "I summon one monster. I set a card. I need to wait 3 turns to get enough Counters for *Speed Spell - Pot of Greed*."
*   **Turn 1 (Metal Force):**
*   Scale 1. Scale 8.
*   **PENDULUM SUMMON 3 MONSTERS.**
*   *Synchro User:* "What?! How did you summon 3 monsters without spending Speed Counters?!"
*   **Metal Force:** "We don't need gas. We have Will."
*   Fuse into **Mithrilium**. Bounce their monster.
*   Set **Metalfoes Counter**.

**Result:** You swarm the board instantly. You overrun them before they can even get their D-Wheels up to speed.

### 3. The "Trap" Build (The Reliable Variant)
For the low-level gang members who aren't good enough to manage resources, you give them the **Trap Variant**.
Speed Duels allow Traps freely.

*   **The Deck:** 3x **Metalfoes Counter**, 3x **Metalfoes Combination**, 3x **Needle Ceiling** (or generic mass removal).
*   **The Strategy:**
1.  Scale Metalfoes.
2.  Pop a card to Set **Metalfoes Counter**.
3.  Pass Turn.
4.  When the opponent attacks? **Metalfoes Counter** triggers.
5.  **Summon a 2400 ATK Volflame from deck.**
6.  Beat them down on the next turn.

This requires **Zero Spells**. It is idiot-proof. It makes your "Cavalry" incredibly annoying to fight because they constantly float into big bodies.

### 4. The "Alkahest" Terror (The Anti-Synchro Weapon)
The scariest card in your arsenal for a Synchro Dimension duelist is **Fullmetalfoes Alkahest**.

*   **The Synchro Strategy:** They spend their entire hand tuning *Junk Synchron* + *Doppelwarrior* to make **Stardust Dragon**. They put all their eggs in one basket.
*   **The Metal Force Response:**
*   Summon **Alkahest** (0 DEF).
*   Wait for their turn.
*   **Quick Effect:** "I equip your Stardust Dragon."
*   **The Physics:** Imagine riding a motorcycle at 200 MPH, summoning a giant dragon, and then a guy standing still just **steals your dragon** and wears it as a coat.
*   **Morale Damage:** Catastrophic.

### Summary: Yes, you win.
You win because you are playing a **different game**.
*   They are playing "manage speed counters to buy spells."
*   You are playing "vomit 4 monsters onto the board every turn for free."

**Your Monopoly:**
1.  **The Scales:** Only you have them.
2.  **The Illegal Speed Spells:** Only you distribute them.
3.  **The Training:** Only you know how to teach the combo.

You are ready to expand.
**Target:** The **Friendship Cup**.

You are going to enter **Diesel** (your first convert). He will be your champion, proving that a Common with a "Trash Deck" can beat the Kings of the City.Is there a metlafoes T1 deck that uses synchro? if not I guess we can go with metalfoes dracoslayerYou have hit the nail on the head.

If you want to dominate the **Synchro Dimension** while keeping your "Metal Force" identity, you absolutely **should** pivot to **Metalfoes Dracoslayer**.

In real-world competitive history (2016), **Metalfoes + Dracoslayer + Gofu** was not just "Tier 1." It was one of the most oppressive decks in the format. It combines the infinite recursion of Pendulums with the explosive power of Synchros.

Here is the blueprint for your **"Synchro-Fusion Hybrid"** deck, which allows you to sell "Tuner Packages" to the locals while maintaining your monopoly.

---

### The Missing Part: "The Drill" and "The Radar"
To make Metalfoes work with Synchro, you need **Tuners** that don't suck. You find two specific cards in the Commons/Rares bins that everyone ignored because they didn't have "Speed Counters" on them.

**1. Luster Pendulum, the Dracoslayer (The Drill)**
*   **Role:** Tuner / Pendulum.
*   **Visuals:** A warrior in golden dragon armor. Fits the "Elite Soldier" vibe.
*   **The Effect:** Once per turn, destroy the *other* card in your Pendulum Zone, then search a copy of that card.
*   **Why it's broken:** It destroys your Metalfoes (triggering effects if you had *Counter* set, or just loading the Extra Deck) and searches *another* Metalfoes. Then, you can **Synchro Summon** using it.

**2. Blackwing - Gofu the Vague Shadow (The Radar)**
*   **Role:** Tuner.
*   **Visuals:** A masked pilot. Fits your "Aircraft/Fleet" aesthetic perfectly.
*   **The Effect:** Cannot be Normal Summoned. If you control no monsters, Special Summon this card. Then, summon 2 "Vague Shadow Tokens."
*   **The Combo:**
*   Summon Gofu (Level 5 Tuner).
*   Summon Tokens.
*   Use Metalfoes Scale to **POP the Tokens** (Free destruction fodder!).
*   Use Gofu + a Level 3 Metalfoes to Synchro Summon **Level 8s**.

---

### The Boss Monster: "Ignister Prominence, the Blasting Dracoslayer"
This is the card that makes you the King of the Synchro Dimension.
*   **Type:** Synchro Monster (Level 8).
*   **Materials:** 1 Tuner + 1+ Non-Tuner Pendulum Monsters.
*   **Effect:** Target 1 Pendulum Monster on the field or in the Graveyard; destroy it, and if you do, **shuffle 1 card on the field into the Deck.**
*   **Why it kills Synchro Users:**
*   It doesn't destroy. It **Spins** (Shuffles back).
*   **Stardust Dragon?** Spun.
*   **Red Dragon Archfiend?** Spun.
*   **Goyo Guardian?** Spun.
*   It is **Non-Targeting, Non-Destruction Removal**. It is the ultimate answer to "Indestructible" boss monsters.

---

### The New Deck: "Metal Force: Dracoslayer Division"

**Monsters (23):**
*   12x **Metalfoes** (The Infantry).
*   3x **Luster Pendulum, the Dracoslayer** (The Tuner).
*   3x **Master Pendulum, the Dracoslayer** (The Vanilla - Recruited by Rescue Rabbit).
*   3x **Rescue Rabbit** (The Instant Rank 4 / Synchro 8).
*   2x **Blackwing - Gofu the Vague Shadow** (The Tech).

**Spells (8):**
*   1x **Metalfoes Fusion**.
*   3x **Painful Decision**.
*   1x **Draco Face-Off** (Quick-Play Spell - Summons a Dracoslayer from deck. Extremely broken).
*   3x **Speed Spell - Mystical Space Typhoon** (Or generic removal).

**Traps (9):**
*   3x **Metalfoes Counter**.
*   3x **Metalfoes Combination**.
*   3x **Solemn Strike** (New Tech - Negates Special Summons. Necessary for Synchro Era).

**Extra Deck (The Armory):**
*   **Synchros:**
*   2x **Ignister Prominence** (The Ace).
*   1x **Scarlight Red Dragon Archfiend** (Nuke).
*   1x **Void Ogre Dragon** (Negates Spells/Traps if hand is empty - Good for top-decking).
*   1x **Ultimaya Tzolkin** (Requires Gofu + Level 5 Metalfoes. Cheats out Crystal Wing).
*   1x **Crystal Wing Synchro Dragon** (Monster Negate).
*   **Fusions:**
*   2x **Mithrilium**.
*   1x **Alkahest**.
*   **Xyz:**
*   1x **Majester Paladin** (Searcher).
*   1x **Number 38: Hope Harbinger** (Spell Negate - if you make Rank 8s).

### The Business Model: "Hybrid Vehicles"
This deck evolution solves your "Colonization" problem perfectly.

**The Problem:**
The Commons *love* Synchro Summoning. They don't want to give it up for Fusion. They think Fusion is weird.

**The Solution (Your Sales Pitch):**
**You:** "Keep your Synchros. I'm not telling you to stop Tuning."
**You:** "I'm selling you the **Engine** to make your Synchros faster."

1.  **The "Metalfoes Engine":** You sell them the Normal Monsters and Scales.
2.  **The Pitch:** "Use the Scales to summon your Tuners for free. Use the Scale Effect to pop your weak Tuners to set Traps. Then Synchro Summon **Ignister**."
3.  **The Result:** You aren't forcing them to change their culture. You are **Augmenting** it.
*   *Diesel (Scrap Iron Gang):* "Whoa! I can Pendulum Summon my Tuner, Synchro, and if it dies, I Pendulum Summon it back next turn?!"
*   *You:* "Exactly. Infinite Synchro materials."

**Market Dominance:**
You have effectively monopolized the Synchro Dimension's underground meta.
*   **The Tops:** Play pure Synchro (Glass Cannons).
*   **The Metal Force Gangs:** Play **Pendulum Synchro**. They have infinite resources. They recover every turn. They overwhelm the Security forces.

### Win Rate Update
With **Ignister Prominence** and **Crystal Wing Synchro Dragon** added to your fleet:
*   **Vs. Yusei:** Competitive. Crystal Wing negates his monster effects.
*   **Vs. Jack Atlas:** **90% Favored.** Ignister spins Red Dragon back to the deck. Jack cries.
*   **Vs. Security:** **100% Slaughter.** They cannot handle the resource loop.

### 2. The Hierarchy of Metal Force
You have structured your organization like a military. Your rank determines your deck power.

**Rank 1: The Infantry (Grunts)**
*   **Deck:** **Pure Metalfoes** (Metal Line Infantry).
*   **Strategy:** "Scale, Pop, Set, Fuse."
*   **Goal:** Defend the warehouse. Intimidate locals.
*   **Vehicle:** On foot or stolen bicycles.

**Rank 2: The Officers (Squad Leaders)**
*   **Deck:** **Metalfoes + Luster Pendulum**.
*   **Strategy:** They gain access to **Synchro Summoning**. They use *Luster* to destroy scales and search, setting up bigger boards.
*   **Goal:** Lead raids. Enforce territory.
*   **Vehicle:** Standard D-Wheels (Scavenged).

**Rank 3: The Warlords (Sector Bosses)**
*   **Deck:** **Metalfoes Dracoslayer (Full Power)**.
*   **Ace:** **Ignister Prominence**.
*   **Strategy:** "Spin to Win." They remove the opponent's boss monster without targeting or destroying it.
*   **Goal:** Win high-stakes turf wars against Sector Security.
*   **Vehicle:** Custom D-Wheels (Tuned by Kenji).

Here is how we rationalize this in **Anime Logic** vs. **Reality**.

### 1. The "Anime Age" Logic
In the *Yu-Gi-Oh!* universe, age is irrelevant to power or danger.
*   **Mokuba Kaiba:** Ran a global corporation at age 8.
*   **Reiji Akaba:** Is the CEO of the world's biggest tech/military company at age 16.
*   **Sora Perse:** Looks 10 years old, but is an elite soldier who hunts humans for sport.
*   **Crow Hogan's Gang (5D's):** Was mostly composed of orphans and teenagers.

**The Aesthetic:**
Imagine **Gonta** (14 years old, heavy set) standing over **Diesel** (30-year-old hardened biker gangster).
*   **Diesel:** "I... I lost to a kid?"
*   **Gonta:** "I'm not a kid. I'm Infantry. Hand over the warehouse keys."

It actually makes your faction **scarier**. It gives off major *Akira* or *Lord of the Flies* vibes. The locals aren't terrified of big men; they are terrified of the **"Children of the Corn"** (Metal Force) who show no emotion and ruthlessly dismantle adults.

### 2. The Organizational Split (Child Soldiers vs. Adult Grunts)
To make this feel less like child endangerment and more like a Crime Syndicate, you need to clarify the roles.

**The "Students" (Gonta, Kenji, Yumi): The Officers**
*   They are the **Elites**.
*   They don't patrol the streets at night. They stay in the Warehouse (HQ) and act as **Tacticians** or **Lieutenants**.
*   They hold the "Royal" cards (*Ignister Prominence*, *Alkahest*).
*   They instruct the adults.

**The "Recruits" (Diesel & The Gangs): The Grunts**
*   These are the **Adults** you conquered.
*   Diesel and his bikers are grown men in their 20s/30s.
*   They take orders from Kenji (age 14).
*   *Kenji:* "Your tuning was inefficient. Run the drills again."
*   *Diesel:* "Yes, sir!"
*   **Why they listen:** Because in this world, **Card Game Skill = Authority**. Kenji beat Diesel; therefore, Kenji outranks Diesel.

### 3. Your Role as "The Guardian"
You aren't just their boss; you are their **Father Figure** in this hellhole.
*   **The Commons:** It's a rough place. Adults steal from kids.
*   **Your Rule:** "Nobody touches my students."
*   If a Sector Security officer tries to rough up Yumi, you step in.
*   Or better yet, you send **Diesel** to step in.
*   *Diesel:* "Back off, cop. That's the Lieutenant."

### 4. The "Child Soldier" Aesthetic
This actually fits the *Arc-V* themes perfectly.
*   **Academia (Villains):** Uses child soldiers (Obelisk Force) who are brainwashed.
*   **Lancers (Heroes):** Uses child soldiers (Yuya/Sora) who are "heroes."
*   **Metal Force (You):** Uses child soldiers who are **Professionals**.
*   They don't fight for "The Professor" or "Smiles."

**Summary:**
Yes, they are kids.
Yes, it is terrifying.
But watching a 13-year-old Kenji lecture a gang of bikers on the importance of "Pendulum Resource Management" is the exact kind of absurdity that makes *Yu-Gi-Oh!* great.

Here is the **Finalized Hall of Fame** for your cross-dimensional character, with a revised GX title that captures the "Chaos" element perfectly.

### 1. The GX Era: "The Twilight Emperor"
*(Formerly The Red Bandit / Yellow Monarch)*

*   **Why this name?**
*   **Twilight:** In Yu-Gi-Oh terms, "Twilight" specifically refers to decks that mix **Light** (Zaborg, Cyber Dragon) and **Dark** (Caius, Chaos Sorcerer, D.D. Survivor). It perfectly describes your Tier 0 deck.
*   **Emperor:** You conquered Obelisk Blue. You aren't just a King (Monarch); you are an Emperor who rules over both Light and Dark.
*   **The Vibe:** Arrogant, untouchable, regal. The guy who wears the white coat but keeps a banned card in his pocket.

### 2. The 5D's Era: "The Ghost"
*(Formerly The Scrap Phantom)*

*   **Why this name?**
*   **The Ghost:** It fits your playstyle (Burn/Trap). You don't fight; you haunt the opponent until they die from effect damage.
*   **Status:** You were an illegal immigrant without an ID. You existed in the system as a ghost signal.
*   **The Vibe:** Survivalist, gritty, invisible. A legend of the highway that Sector Security can't catch.

### 3. The Zexal Era: "The Iron Admiral"
*(Formerly The Corn Captain)*

*   **Why this name?**
*   **Iron:** Represents your deck type (**Earth Machines**: Machina/Geargia) and your family business (Ironworks).
*   **Admiral:** You command a **Fleet** (Dracossack, Blackship, Silent Honor ARK). You don't duel 1v1; you launch naval bombardments.
*   **The Vibe:** Industrial, heavy, wealthy. The Blue-Collar Billionaire who runs the city's tech.

### 4. The Arc-V Era: "The Commander"
*(Formerly The Instructor)*

*   **Why this name?**
*   **Commander:** You aren't just a teacher; you are a Warlord. You give orders. Your students are soldiers.
*   **The Deck:** **Metalfoes**. They are literal soldiers/vehicles.
*   **The Vibe:** Strict, tactical, revolutionary. You are the leader of the "Metal Force" resistance/syndicate.
