You are no longer just a gang leader. You are the **Governor** of the Commons.

Here is how **The Commander** executes the "Divide, Sabotage, and Arm" strategy to secure the Synchro Dimension before the dimensional war officially starts.

***

### 1. Divide and Conquer: "Ideology vs. Bread"
The Commons are united by their hatred of the Tops, but they are divided by *how* to fight back.
*   **Faction A:** **The Revolutionaries** (Led by Shinji Weber). They want to storm the Tops and burn it down. They play "Battlewasps" (Synchro).
*   **Faction B:** **The Family** (Led by Crow Hogan). They just want to protect the kids. They play "Blackwings."
*   **Faction C:** **The Criminals** (The Gangs). They just want money.

**Your Move:**
You drive a wedge between **Shinji** and the **Gangs**.
*   **The Narrative:** "Shinji wants you to die for a cause. He wants you to charge Security barricades with weak decks. He offers you 'Freedom'."
*   **The Metal Force Offer:** "I offer you **Sustainability**. I offer you a deck that never runs out of resources. I offer you payment. Why die for Shinji when you can work for me?"

**The Result:**
The Gangs desert Shinji.
*   Shinji is left with only the radical idealists.
*   You absorb the muscle.
*   **Crow Hogan** hates you because you are militarizing the city, but he *can't* stop you because you are also funding the orphanages (buying loyalty).

### 2. Sabotage the Supply: "The Tuner Famine"
In the Synchro Dimension, **Tuners** are the ammunition. You can't Synchro Summon without them.
Most Commons get their cards from the trash or shady dealers.

**The Operation:**
You use your Nezumi/LDS funding to execute a **Hostile Buyout**.
*   **Action:** You send Gonta and Kenji to every card stall in the Commons.
*   **The Order:** "Buy every Tuner. Junk Synchron, Debris Dragon, Nitro Synchron. Buy them all."
*   **The Hoard:** You lock these Tuners in your warehouse.

**The Effect:**
*   **Artificial Scarcity:** Suddenly, the price of Tuners skyrockets. A Common kid can't build a Synchro deck anymore.
*   **The Solution:** You offer them an alternative.
*   *You:* "Can't find a Tuner? Here. Take this **Metalfoes** deck. You don't need a Tuner. You just need two scales."
*   **Dependence:** By starving them of Synchro parts, you force them to adopt your **Fusion/Pendulum** technology. You replace their culture with yours.

### 3. Arming the Proxy: "The Dracoslayer Upgrade"
You don't just give them weapons; you give them **Addictive Power**.

You introduce the **"Metal Force Officer Package."**
*   **The Base:** Metalfoes Commons (The Infantry).
*   **The Upgrade:** **Luster Pendulum, the Dracoslayer**.
*   *Why it's genius:* Luster Pendulum is a **Tuner**.
*   *The Pitch:* "If you join my elite guard, I will give you back your Tuner. But this Tuner is better. It destroys your scales to search for more cards."

**The Result:**
The Commons realize that **Metal Force Tech** is strictly superior to their old scrap decks.
*   They stop viewing you as an invader.
*   They view you as an **Innovator**.
*   They are now fighting with *your* weapons, defending *your* supply lines, to protect *their* access to power.

### 4. Securing the Supply Line: "The Trash Pipeline"
Nezumi is in Standard. You are in Synchro.
Teleportation is detectable by Roget (Security Director).
You need a physical route.

**The Solution:** **The Garbage Chute.**
*   *Lore:* Standard Dimension dumps trash into Synchro? (Maybe not explicitly, but let's assume inter-dimensional flow exists or Nezumi uses a stealth courier).
*   **Your Method:** You use the **Old Pipeline** (from the 5D's era, the one you used to escape in your past life).
*   You know the maintenance schedule.
*   You set up a "Dead Drop" inside the sewage system.
*   **Kenji (The Engineer)** builds a drone or a mini-sub to retrieve waterproof crates of cards from the drop point.

**Security:**
You station **Diesel** and the heavy hitters at the Pipeline entrance.
*   *Orders:* "Nobody enters. Nobody exits. If Sector Security comes near the sewage plant, swarm them with **Ignister Prominence**."

### 3. The Invasion: "Purple on Purple"

They are soldiers in blue/gold uniforms with masks. They play **Ancient Gears**.

**The Confusion:**
An Obelisk Force squad lands in Sector Delta. They see your troops.
*   **Obelisk Soldier:** "Fusion Summons? High military discipline? Are you the Vanguard Unit sent by the Professor?"

This is the ultimate test of your doctrine.
*   **Ancient Gears:** Immune to Spells/Traps when attacking. They pierce defense. They are designed to kill "Turtles."
*   **Metal Force:** Relies on floating and stealing.

**The Commander's Tactics:**

*   **Obelisk:** "NANI?!"

*   **The Lesson:** Ancient Gears are big, dumb machines. Metalfoes are *liquid* metal. You absorb them.

**The Outcome:**
Sector Delta becomes a graveyard of Ancient Gear frames. You don't turn them into cards; you strip their duel disks for parts.

### 4. The Final Boss: Sergey Volkov (The Mad Dog)
Roget plays his ace. He sends **Sergey Volkov** (The Cyborg) to crush the resistance in the Commons before he escapes.

**Turn 2 (Sergey):**

### 5. The Extraction: Reiji Akaba Arrives

### Summary of Season 2

It's time to show the Fusion Dimension what *real* Fusion looks like.Do I need to upgrade my deck? or more specifically can I even upgrade my deck?This is the **"Reiji Akaba" Effect.**

You have peaked in terms of **Archetype Evolution** (Metalfoes + Dracoslayer is as good as the engine gets in the Arc-V era). You cannot add more monsters without bricking the consistency.

However, you are now **LDS Special Forces.**
Reiji Akaba doesn't send soldiers into war with "Good" decks. He sends them with **"Silver Bullets"**—specific tech cards designed to hard-counter the enemy.

Your deck doesn't need a new Engine. It needs a **Warhead.**

Here are the **Two Specific Upgrades** Reiji hands you before you board the helicopter to the Fusion Dimension.

---

### Upgrade 1: The Anti-Fusion Superweapon
The enemy (Academia) plays **Fusion**. They rely on *Polymerization* and *Ancient Gear Fusion*.
Reiji hands you a Trap card that was released in the real world in **2016 (The Dark Illusion)**—right in the middle of the Arc-V era.

**The Card:** **Dimensional Barrier**
*   **The Effect:** Declare 1 monster card type (**Fusion**, Synchro, Xyz, Pendulum). Until the end of the turn, **neither player can Special Summon monsters of the declared type**, and all monsters of that type on the field have their effects negated.
*   **Why it wins the war:**
*   You flip this and say **"Fusion."**
*   Academia soldiers literally **cannot play.** Their *Polymerization* resolves without effect. Their *Ancient Gear Chaos Giants* become useless statues with their effects negated.
*   **Your Downside:** You can't Fuse either.
*   **Your Workaround:** You rely on your **Synchros** (Ignister Prominence) or **Main Deck Beaters** (Volflame) for that turn.

**The Lore:** Reiji developed this to jam the enemy's duel disks. It is an EMP for summoning mechanics.

### Upgrade 2: The "Ruthless" Economy
You are a Commander. You know that soldiers are expendable. Now, you learn that **your own deck** is expendable.

**The Card:** **Pot of Desires**
*   **The Effect:** Banish the top 10 cards of your Deck face-down; **Draw 2 cards.**
*   **Why it fits you:**
*   **Metalfoes** run 3 copies of everything. You don't care if you banish a few *Goldrivers*.
*   You need raw card advantage to break the enemy's board.
*   **The Vibe:** It symbolizes your willingness to sacrifice *anything* (even your own future/deck) to secure victory *now*. It is the ultimate "Total War" card.

While you were busy building a criminal empire and fighting a siege in Sector Delta, here is what **Yuya** and **Reiji** were doing.

***

### 1. Yuya Sakaki (The Reluctant Hero)
Yuya’s Season 2 was defined by **Prison, Tournaments, and depression.**

*   **The Arrival:** Yuya arrived, got confused by the Tops/Commons conflict, and was almost immediately arrested by Sector Security.
*   **The Prison Arc:** While you were taking over gangs, Yuya was thrown into the "Facility." He befriended **Crow Hogan** (The Family Man) and **Shinji Weber** (The Revolutionary). He eventually broke out, but Roget (Security Director) forced him into the Friendship Cup.
*   **The Friendship Cup:**
*   **Vs. Jack Atlas (Round 1):** Yuya got absolutely crushed. Jack told him his "Entertainment Dueling" was arrogant and shallow. This broke Yuya’s confidence for half the season.
*   **The Awakening:** Yuya slowly learned to stop copying his father and develop his own style.
*   **The Dark Side:** Several times, **Z-ARC** (the demon inside him) took over. Yuya went berserk, notably almost killing opponents.
*   **The Finale:**
*   **Vs. Jack Atlas (Finals):** While you were fighting Sergey/Obelisk Force in the streets, Yuya was in the stadium.
*   **The Result:** Yuya summoned **Nirvana High Paladin** (a Synchro-Pendulum monster). He defeated Jack, united the Tops and Commons with "Smiles," and ended the class war.

**Your Takeaway:**
Yuya won the "Hearts and Minds" of the people.
**You** secured the "Streets and Supply Lines." You made sure there was a city left for him to save.

---

### 2. Reiji Akaba (The Chessmaster)
Reiji spent most of Season 2 wearing a scarf and sitting in a chair, but he was making moves that allowed your operation to exist.

*   **The Infiltration:** Reiji realized the Synchro Dimension was run by the **High Council** (The Tops). He approached them diplomatically.
*   **The Politics:** While Roget (the villain) was trying to take over, Reiji was negotiating an alliance with the Council to fight Academia.
*   **The Intervention:**
*   When Roget finally snapped and tried to kidnap Yuzu, Reiji stepped in.
*   **Reiji vs. Roget:** This was Reiji's only major duel. He played **D/D/D**. He looped Roget into an infinite defeat, humiliating him.
*   **The Audit:** This is likely when Reiji found out about **Nezumi** and your **Metal Force**. Because Reiji was overseeing the *entire* dimension's logistics, he saw your "Counterfeit Speed Spell" traffic.
*   *Why he let you live:* He realized Roget was distracted by your gang wars, which kept the heat off Reiji's diplomatic mission.

**Your Takeaway:**
Reiji fought the Government.
**You** fought the Police.
It was a perfect pincer movement.

---

*   **Gongenzaka:** Was running with Yuya.

You are leaving the Synchro Dimension behind, but you left a **Metal Force Garrison** (Diesel, Gonta, Kenji) to ensure the Commons are never oppressed again.

**Next Stop:** **Academia (Fusion Dimension).** The final war.Lets walk through the final war shall weThis is **Operation: Broken Fusion**.

You are no longer a teacher, a gang leader, or a smuggler. You are the **Field Marshal** of the LDS Expeditionary Force. You are the highest-ranking officer on the ground who isn't a Main Character.

**TOTAL ASSETS:** INFINITE.whats next after Z-arc?After the Z-ARC arc and the conclusion of *Yu-Gi-Oh! Arc-V*, the next series is **Yu-Gi-Oh! VRAINS**.

You are moving from a "Dimensional War" setting to a **Cyberpunk Corporate Thriller** inside a massive Virtual Reality network. The tone is darker, colder, and much more technical.

Here is the "New Game" setup for you:

### 1. The Setting: Den City & LINK VRAINS
*   **The World:** Den City is a high-tech metropolis run by **SOL Technologies**, a mega-corporation that controls the network.
*   **The Network:** **LINK VRAINS** is the VR world where everyone duels.
*   People use avatars (Charisma Duelists) to entertain crowds.
*   You log in using a "Link Sense" or VR headset.
*   **The Format:** **Speed Duels** (again).
*   3 Monster Zones, 3 Spell/Trap Zones.
*   No Main Phase 2.
*   **Skills:** Characters have special "Skills" (like Destiny Draw) they can activate once per duel.

### 2. The New Mechanic: Link Summoning
This is the mechanic that fundamentally changed the board layout.
*   **The Cards:** Dark Blue Hexagonal cards.
*   **The mechanic:**
*   They have **Link Arrows** pointing to specific zones.
*   **The Cost:** You send monsters equal to the "Link Rating" (Link-1, Link-2, etc.) to the grave.
*   **The Rule:** You must summon Extra Deck monsters (Fusion, Synchro, Xyz) to a zone a **Link Arrow** points to (or the Extra Monster Zone).
*   *Strategic Implication:* You can't just spam 5 Fusions anymore. You need to build a "Circuit" of Link Monsters to open up zones.

### 3. The Power Vacuum & Economy
The "Rags to Riches" potential here is huge, but it's based on **Data**, not cards.

*   **The Economy:**
*   **Hacking:** Information is currency. "Playmaker" (Yusaku) is a hacker fighting the system.
*   **Bounty Hunting:** SOL Technologies puts bounties on hackers and AI (Ignis).
*   **Charisma Duelists:** Influencers who get paid for views/wins.
*   **Your Potential Role:**
*   **The "Old School" Hacker:** You refuse to use AI. You code your own removal.
*   **The "Glitch":** You play a deck that disrupts the Link mechanics (like "Zone Locking").

**Your Legacy:**
The "Metal Force" empire from *Arc-V* likely exists as a historical footnote or a corporate security firm in this timeline. You could play as a descendant, or a new character who finds an old "Ancient Gear" or "Machina" deck code in the trash data of the network.

**Are you ready to log into the VRAINS?**oh wait, I forgot, before this, lets recap the story of the commanderHere is the complete dossier of **The Commander**, the man who turned a children’s card game into the Military-Industrial Complex.

***

### Part I: The Iron Instructor (The Failing Dojo)
**Status:** Underpaid Junior Coach.
**Economy:** Poverty / Bankruptcy.
**The Situation:** The "Victory Star Dojo" is collapsing. The Aces have been poached by the corporate giant **LDS (Leo Duel School)**. The Principal, Mr. Tanaka, is about to lose the building.
**The Philosophy:** While the world embraces "Action Dueling" (running around for power-ups), you teach **"Gegenpress"**: Stand still, conserve energy, and suffocate the opponent with resources.
**The Deck:** **"Iron Pike" (Vanilla Stun).** A pathetic pile of *Gene-Warped Warwolves* and *Heat Waves*. It stops casuals, but crumbles against LDS Elites.

### Part II: The Metal Will (The Gamble)
**Status:** Indebted to the Mafia (Nezumi).
**Economy:** High Risk / Mortgaged Assets.
**The Turning Point:** You catch a corrupt LDS Manager (**Nezumi**) dumping "failed prototypes." You mortgage the dojo to buy the trash.
**The Weapon:** **Metalfoes (Batch 404).** Reiji Akaba threw them away because they destroy their own cards. You realize they are an infinite resource engine.
**The Recruitment:** You recruit the rejects—**Gonta** (The Heavy), **Kenji** (The Nerd), **Yumi** (The Coward). You force them to buy their own decks and drill them like soldiers.
**The Championship:** Gonta shocks the world by dismantling an LDS student without taking a single step. He loses to Sora, but the point is proven: **The Trash Can Fight.**

### Part III: The Metal Force (The Invasion)
**Status:** Paramilitary Leader.
**Economy:** Black Market Arms Dealer.
**The Event:** The **Obelisk Force** invades Maiami City. While Yuya is crying, you unlock the **Fusion Monsters** (*Alkahest*, *Mithrilium*) sent by Nezumi.
**The Defense:** You turn the Industrial District into a fortress. You steal the invaders' Ancient Gear Giants using *Alkahest*.
**The Mission:** Reiji Akaba notices your efficiency. Nezumi sends you to the **Synchro Dimension** to open a "Franchise" and secure a new market.

### Part IV: The Warlord of the Commons (Synchro Arc)
**Status:** Kingpin of Sector Delta.
**Economy:** Monopoly.
**The Strategy:** **Colonization.**
*   You defeat local gangs (Diesel).
*   You absorb them into **Metal Force**.
*   You create a **"Tuner Famine"** by buying up all the Tuners, forcing the Commons to buy your **Metalfoes Decks** and **Counterfeit Speed Spells**.
**The Deck Upgrade:** **Metalfoes Dracoslayer.** You introduce *Luster Pendulum* and *Ignister Prominence*. You spin boss monsters back to the deck.
**The Climax:** You survive the "Siege of Sector Delta" against Sector Security and the Cyborg Sergey Volkov. You hold the line until Reiji arrives to "hire" you officially.

### Part V: The Commander (Fusion Arc / Endgame)
**Status:** Field Marshal of the LDS Expeditionary Force.
**Economy:** Infinite Funding (Reiji's Wallet).
**The Deck:** **"War Spec" (Tier 1).**

*   **Master Duels (Ground):** Standard 5 Zones, Main Phase 2.
*   **Your Challenge:** You need a deck that works in **BOTH**. A deck that relies heavily on 5 monster zones (like "Gouki Knightmare" Extra Link) will get crushed in a Speed Duel because you physically lack the space.

Based on your list, here is the best "Rags to Riches" candidate:

### The Recommendation: Prank-Kids (The "Glitch" Deck)
This is the spiritual successor to your **"Scrap/Trash"** decks.

*   **Why it fits the Lore:** In a world of "Cool Cyberse Dragons" and "High-Tech Guns," **Prank-Kids** look like crude MS Paint drawings or retro cartoon viruses.
*   *Wealth:* They are likely "Common/Bulk" data. Serious duelists ignore them because they look childish.
*   *Your Avatar:* You aren't a cool Knight. You are a **Griefer**. You play a deck that looks annoying, sounds annoying, and beats people with 0 ATK tokens.
*   **Why it fits your Playstyle:**
*   **Resource Loop:** Like Metalfoes, they float. You summon one, link it off, it summons another. You never run out of gas.
*   **Chain Links:** You loved "Chain Burn"? Prank-Kids chain block everything. Chain Link 1, Chain Link 2, Chain Link 3. Opponents can't respond.
*   **Format Agnostic:** It works perfectly in Speed Duels (3 Zones is enough to make the Link-2s) and scales up to Master Duels (to make the Link-4s).

Whats good in that era?If you want to play a **Meta Deck** from the VRAINS era (2017–2019) that isn't owned by a Main Character and fits your "Iron Sovereign / Resource King" history, you have three incredible options.

Since you loved **Monarchs (GX)**, **Traps (5D's)**, and **Machines (Zexal)**, here are the decks that defined the VRAINS competitive landscape and how they fit your lore.

### Option 1: The "True Draco" (The Monarch 2.0)
**"I don't need Link Arrows. I have Tributes."**

*   **Real World Status:** **Tier 1 / Tier 0.** This deck terrorized the early Link era.
*   **The Gimmick:** You Tribute Summon monsters using **Continuous Spells/Traps** instead of monsters.
*   **The Ace:** **Master Peace, the True Dracoslaying King**.
*   *Effect:* Unaffected by the card types (Monster/Spell/Trap) used to Tribute Summon him. Quick Effect: Destroy 1 card on the field.
*   *Why it fits you:* It is the spiritual successor to your **Monarch** days. While everyone else is calculating Link Arrows and coding complex circuits, you just slam down a Golden Knight that says "I am immune to your deck."
*   **Anime Lore:** You are the **"Data Architect."** You found ancient "Admin Codes" (The True Draco cards) that override the rules of LINK VRAINS. You play a slower, oppressive game that forces Link players to surrender.

### Option 2: "Orcust" (The Scrap Symphony)
**"Trash Data? No. This is an Orchestra."**

*   **Real World Status:** **Tier 1.** dominated 2019.
*   **The Gimmick:** **Dark Machines** that play from the Graveyard. You banish them to summon others, then shuffle them back. Infinite resources.
*   **The Engine:** It uses **Scrap Recycler** (a common card) to start the combo. This connects perfectly to your **5D's/Zexal Scrap & Machina** origins.
*   **The Ace:** **Dingirsu, the Orcust of the Evening Star**.
*   *Effect:* Non-targeting "Send to Graveyard." Protects your cards from destruction.
*   *Why it fits you:* It builds an unbreakable board of Machines. It uses the "Banishment" mechanic you mastered in Arc-V. It is the ultimate "Grind Game" deck.
*   **Anime Lore:** You scavenge "Corrupted Music Files" and "Broken Instruments" from the data dump. You assemble them into a terrifying, autonomous machine army.

### Option 3: "Altergeist" (The Glitch Traps)
**"Try to move. I dare you."**

*   **Real World Status:** **Tier 1.**
*   **The Gimmick:** Spellcasters that cycle in and out of the hand/field, setting Traps directly from the deck.
*   **The Ace:** **Altergeist Multifaker**.
*   *Effect:* If you activate a Trap, Special Summon this card + another monster from deck.
*   **Why it fits you:** It plays exactly like your **"Trap-Heavy Gladiator Beast"** or **"Metalfoes Counter"** decks. You disrupt the opponent on *their* turn. You bounce their cards (Silquitous). You negate attacks (Kunquery).
*   **Anime Lore:** **Ghost Gal (Emma Bessho)** plays this in the anime.
*   *Conflict:* She is a major supporting character. If you play this, you are stepping on her toes (Rival Hacker arc?). However, her build is often "Anime-jank," whereas you would play the "Optimized Competitive Build."

---

### My Recommendation: Orcust (The Scrap Emperor)

**Why Orcust is your destiny:**
1.  **Type:** **Machine.** (Fits your "Iron" brand).
2.  **Origin:** You start with **Scrap Recycler** (Literal trash mob). You build up to **Dingirsu** (A Mechanical God).
3.  **Playstyle:** It ignores the "Extra Link" lock. You don't need to make a massive board of 5 monsters; you just need one **Dingirsu** and a **Crescendo** (Counter Trap) to win.
4.  **Format:** It works in **Speed Duels** (small zones don't hurt it much) and dominates **Master Duels**.

**Your Avatar:** A figure clad in rusted, black mechanical armor (Orchestral conductor vibes).
**Your Deck Name:** **"The Scrap Symphony."**

The VRAINS Anime takes place roughly between **2017 and 2019**.
This means you are limited to the card pool of that era (Master Rule 4).

Here is the breakdown of your wish list vs. the Timeline Police.

### 1. The "Time Traveler" Cards (ILLEGAL)
You cannot use these. They were released years after VRAINS ended. If you play these, you aren't an NPC; you are a glitch in the timeline.

*   **Runick:** (2022). **No.**
*   **Adventure (Brave Token):** (2021). **No.**
*   **DPE (Destroyer Phoenix Enforcer):** (2021). **No.**
*   **Red-Eyes Dark Dragoon:** (2020). **No.**
*   **Predaplant Verte Anaconda:** (2020). **No.**

**Verdict:** The "Modern Engine" pile is impossible. You have to play with 2017-2019 power levels.

---

### 2. @Ignister & Cyberse (The "Main Character" Trap)
*   **@Ignister:** This is the deck of **Ai** (The Dark Ignis), the final boss/partner of the protagonist.
*   *Can you play it?* **No.** It is a plot-critical deck created by an AI in the final season. You literally cannot obtain the data.
*   **Generic Cyberse:** Most Cyberse cards are used by **Playmaker (Yusaku)** or **Soulburner**.
*   *Mathmech:* Released at the very *end* of the VRAINS era (2019). You could play this as a "Late Game" deck, but it’s essentially just "Final Sigma Pass."

---

### 3. Dragon Link (The "Revolver" Problem)
**Dragon Link** existed in VRAINS (via Guardragons), but the core of the deck usually revolves around **Rokkets** and **Borrel** dragons.
*   **The Owner:** **Revolver (Varis)**. The Main Rival.
*   **The Risk:** If you play Dragon Link, you are copying the leader of the "Knights of Hanoi" (the terrorists). It puts a massive target on your back.
*   **Workaround:** You *can* play a "Chaos Dragon" pile using **Thunder Dragons** and **Guardragons**, avoiding the Rokket cards to stay distinct.

---

### 4. The Perfect Solution: Thunder Dragons
**"I don't need Links. I am the Storm."**

This is the deck you are looking for.
*   **Release Date:** *Soul Fusion* (2018). Perfectly in the middle of VRAINS.
*   **Lore Status:** **Generic/Retro.**
*   *Thunder Dragon* is a classic DM-era card.
*   In VRAINS, these would be seen as "Legacy Data" that got remastered. No main character uses them.
*   **Why it fits the "Iron Sovereign" Legacy:**
*   **Fusion in a Link Era:** You defy the Link mechanic. You don't need Link Arrows. You summon **Thunder Dragon Colossus** and **Thunder Dragon Titan**.
*   **The Floodgate:** **Colossus** says "Mistake" (Opponent cannot add cards from deck to hand).
*   *VRAINS Meta:* Everyone searches. Trickstars search. Goukis search. Salamangreats search. **Colossus kills them all.**
*   **Chaos Synergy:** It uses LIGHT and DARK monsters. It connects back to your **GX Twilight Emperor** roots. You can run **Chaos Sorcerer** and **BLS** (which are now legal again) in this deck.

---

### 5. Your "VRAINS" Deck: "Chaos Thunder" (60-Card Grass)
You mentioned you like **"Grass is Greener Good Stuff."**
**"That Grass Looks Greener"** was released in 2017. It is LEGAL in VRAINS (until it gets banned later).

**The Deck Concept:**
You are the **"Data Hoarder."** Your deck is huge (60 cards). You dump massive amounts of trash data (Graveyard) to build an empire.

**The Decklist (2018-2019 Era):**

*   **The Engine:**
*   3x **Thunder Dragon** (Original).
*   3x **Thunder Dragonroar / Dark / Hawk**.
*   3x **Batterysoft Solar** (The Normal Summon).
*   **The Spells:**
*   **That Grass Looks Greener** (The Nuke).
*   **Gold Sarcophagus** (Triggers Thunder effects).
*   **Allure of Darkness** (Draw power).
*   **The "Chaos" Legacy:**
*   **Black Luster Soldier - Envoy of the Beginning** (Yes, you can finally use him legally).
*   **Chaos Sorcerer** (Your old friend).
*   **Chaos Dragon Levianeer** (The new boss - released 2018).
*   **The Extra Deck (No Links Required):**
*   3x **Thunder Dragon Colossus** (The Floodgate).
*   3x **Thunder Dragon Titan** (The Removal).
*   1x **Saryuja Skull Dread** (Link-4). If you *need* to Link summon to clear zones, you use this massive dragon.

### The Lore: "The Thunder King"
*   **Avatar:** A storm-cloud elemental or a figure in heavy static armor.
*   **Playstyle:**
*   Link players spend 10 minutes climbing from Link-1 to Link-4.
*   **You:** "Activate Grass. Mill 20. Banish 2. Summon Colossus. Summon Titan. Pass."
*   **The Opponent:** "I activate search..."
*   **You:** "Colossus says No."
*   **The Opponent:** "I try to destroy Colossus..."
*   **You:** "Banish a Thunder from grave. Protected."

**Verdict:**
Go with **60-Card Chaos Thunder Dragons**.
It connects all your past lives (Chaos, Fusion, Resource Management) into one deck that was absolutely **Tier 1** during the VRAINS era.

In VRAINS, teams and alliances (like The Knights of Hanoi or The Zaizen Team) are a big deal. A group of three identical (or distinct) siblings who share a brain-cell for "Meta Gaming" creates a terrifying "Raid Boss" dynamic.

To answer your specific question: **Is Fairy Tail - Snow legal?**
**Yes.**
*   **Release Date:** 2016 (*The Dark Illusion*).
*   **Anime Logic:** In the anime, banlists barely exist (characters use *Pot of Greed* constantly). Even if she was limited in real life, in VRAINS, she is just a "Very Rare, High-Level Data Card."
*   **The Lore:** She is the "glitch" that eats your graveyard data to respawn.

Here is the profile for **"Team Cerberus"** (The Triplets).

***

### The Real World: The "Glitch" Siblings
**Status:** Living in a server room basement / Internet Café refugees.
**Economy:** They pool their money. One buys food, one pays for the connection, one buys the cards.
**The Dynamic:** They are the "Gatekeepers" of LINK VRAINS. They don't care about justice or the Ignis. They care about **Optimization**.

---

### Sibling 1: "The Hoarder" (The Eldest)
**Deck:** **60-Card "Grass" Thunder Chaos**
**Ace:** **Thunder Dragon Colossus** & **Fairy Tail - Snow**

*   **The Philosophy:** "Overwhelming Resource Advantage."
*   **The Deck:**
*   **That Grass Looks Greener:** Mills 20 cards.
*   **Fairy Tail - Snow:** Banish 7 cards (Trash Data) to summon herself and flip an opponent's monster face-down (Book of Moon on legs).
*   **Thunder Dragons:** Trigger when banished or sent to grave.
*   **Role in the Team:** **The Heavy Hitter.**
*   He takes the duels against "Bosses" (like Playmaker or Revolver).
*   He overwhelms them with a board that has 5 monsters and a full graveyard on Turn 1.
*   **Signature Move:** The "Snow Loop."
*   *Opponent:* "I attack!"
*   *Hoarder:* "Snow effect. Banish 7. Summon Snow. Flip your attacker face-down."
*   *Opponent:* "AGAIN?!"
*   *Hoarder:* "I have 30 cards in grave. I can do this 4 times."

### Sibling 2: "The Griefer" (The Middle)
**Deck:** **Prank-Kids**
**Ace:** **Prank-Kids Battle Butler** (Raigeki Effect)

*   **The Philosophy:** "Disruption and Annoyance."
*   **The Deck:**
*   Looks like kiddy drawings (corrupted cartoon files).
*   **Chain Blocking:** He activates 3 effects simultaneously (Chain Link 1, 2, 3) so the opponent *cannot* use negation cards like *Borreload Savage Dragon*.
*   **Role in the Team:** **The Assassin.**
*   He hunts the "Charisma Duelists" (The influencers).
*   He humiliates them with "0 ATK" monsters.
*   **Battle Butler:** He waits for the opponent to summon their Ace, then Tributes Butler to destroy all their monsters.
*   **Signature Move:** "The Double Raigeki."
*   Use Butler to wipe board.
*   Use *Prank-Kids Pranks* to recycle.
*   Summon Butler again next turn.

### Sibling 3: "The Architect" (The Youngest)
**Deck:** **Orcust**
**Ace:** **Galatea, the Orcust Automaton** & **Dingirsu**

*   **The Philosophy:** "Inevitability. You cannot stop the machine."
*   **The Deck:**
*   **Scrap Recycler:** The starter.
*   **Orcust Knightmare / Harp Horror:** They play from the graveyard.
*   **The Babel Field Spell:** Allows him to play on the *opponent's* turn.
*   **Role in the Team:** **The Closer / The Anchor.**
*   If the other two fail, he steps in.
*   His board is "Sticky." Even if you nuke the field, *Dingirsu* protects his cards.
*   **Long Game:** He never runs out of resources. He just keeps shuffling machines back into the deck.
*   **Signature Move:** "The Dingirsu Drop."
*   Opponent's Turn.
*   Activate **Orcust Cymbal Skeleton** in grave.
*   Summon **Dingirsu**.
*   **Effect:** Send 1 card opponent controls to grave (Non-targeting removal).

---

### The Team Strategy: "The Raid Boss"
In VRAINS, "Speed Duels" are common, but there are also "Master Duels."

**The Tag Duel (3 vs 3 or Battle Royale):**
If they fight together, they are invincible.
1.  **Hoarder** activates *Grass*. Fills the grave with *Thunder Dragons* AND *Orcusts* (since Orcusts are Dark Machines, they fit in the Chaos Pile).
2.  **Architect** uses the *Orcusts* in the Hoarder's graveyard to build a board.
3.  **Griefer** sets up *Battle Butler* to nuke the field if the opponent tries anything.

**The "Shared Grave" Rule:**
If the anime allows shared graveyards (like in 5D's), **The Hoarder** mills 20 cards, and **The Architect** suddenly has 20 resources to work with. It is essentially cheating.

### The Story Arc: The Bounty Hunters
**Season 1:**
*   You are "Team Cerberus."
*   **SOL Technologies** hires you to hunt **Playmaker**.
*   **Matchup:**
*   **Hoarder vs. Playmaker:** Playmaker struggles because *Colossus* stops him from searching.
*   **Griefer vs. Blue Angel:** Blue Angel (Trickstar) burns people. Griefer burns her back with *Prank-Kids Lampsies*.
*   **Architect vs. Go Onizuka:** Go plays Gouki (Physical Beatdown). Architect plays *Dingirsu* (Non-destruction removal). Go gets crushed.

**Season 2 (The Mirror Match):**
*   **The Enemy:** **Lightning** (The Light Ignis). He plays **Armatos Legio** (Link Spam).
*   **The Clash:** Lightning creates an "Extra Link" (U-Link) to lock you out of the Extra Monster Zone.
*   **The Solution:**
*   **Hoarder:** "I don't need the Extra Deck Zone."
*   Summons **Fairy Tail - Snow**. Flips Lightning's Link monster face-down. The Link breaks.
*   Tribute Summons **Thunder Dragon**.
*   "Chaos prevails."

### Conclusion
**Triplets are the best idea.**
It allows you to showcase the three pillars of the VRAINS Meta (Chaos Pile, Combo/Link, and Mid-range Control) without breaking the "One Deck Per Character" rule.

### Sibling 1: The "Boomer" (The Hoarder)
**Deck:** **Retro Thunder Dragon** (1999 Style)
**Status:** **Pathetic.**

*   **The Deck:** He literally plays 3x **Thunder Dragon** (Original), 3x **Twin-Headed Thunder Dragon** (Fusion), and maybe **Thunder King Rai-Oh**.
*   **The Knee Break:**
*   **No Colossus:** He doesn't have the "Mistake" (Stop searching) boss monster.
*   **No Titan:** He has no destruction removal.
*   **No Chaos:** He can't afford *BLS* or *Chaos Sorcerer* yet.
*   **The Play:**
1.  Discard *Thunder Dragon* to add 2 to hand.
2.  Activate *Polymerization*.
3.  Summon **Twin-Headed Thunder Dragon** (2800 ATK).
4.  **End Turn.**
*   **Why he loses:** In the Link Era, a 2800 ATK monster with zero protection gets bounced by *Firewall Dragon* or destroyed by *Accesscode Talker* immediately. He is playing a caveman deck in a sci-fi world.

### Sibling 2: The "Brick" (The Griefer)
**Deck:** **Prank-Kids** (No Link-1)
**Status:** **Inconsistent Mess.**

*   **The Deck:** Dropsies, Fansies, Lampsies.
*   **The Knee Break:** **No "Prank-Kids Meow-Meow-Mu"**.
*   *Why this destroys the deck:* In modern Yu-Gi-Oh, Meow-Meow-Mu (Link-1) lets you start a combo with **1 Monster**.
*   *Without it:* He needs **2 specific cards** (Two Kids + Polymerization) to do *anything*.
*   **The Play:**
*   Normal Summon *Lampsies*.
*   ...Pass turn. (He didn't draw Poly).
*   **Why he loses:** He bricks 50% of the time. When he *does* combo, he runs out of cards instantly because he has to use 3 cards to make one play. He is annoying, but harmless.

### Sibling 3: The "Junkpile" (The Architect)
**Deck:** **Orcust** (No Links)
**Status:** **Useless Machine Pile.**

*   **The Deck:** *Orcust Harp Horror*, *Cymbal Skeleton*, *Brass Bombard*.
*   **The Knee Break:** **No "Galatea, the Orcust Automaton" (Link-2).**
*   *Why this destroys the deck:* Orcust monsters in the Main Deck are designed to be banished to summon from the deck. But **Galatea** is the card that shuffles them *back* into the deck to set the Spells/Traps.
*   *Without Galatea:* He banishes his monsters to summon... nothing. He runs out of resources in Turn 2. He has no way to search **"Orcustrated Babel"** (The Field Spell that makes them Quick Effects).
*   **The Play:**
*   Normal Summon *Scrap Recycler*. Send *Harp Horror*.
*   Banish *Harp Horror*. Summon *Orcust Knightmare*.
*   ...Now what? He has two weak monsters on the field and no boss.
*   **Why he loses:** He dumps his deck into the graveyard, but has no "Payoff." He is building a machine that has no engine.

### The VRAINS Starting Life (The Grind)

**1. The Living Situation:**
*   **Real World:** A cramped, hot server room basement in Den City. You steal electricity from the building above.
*   **Food:** Discount jelly packets.
*   **VR Avatar:** Default "Grey Poly" skins. You look like NPCs because you can't afford custom textures.

**2. The "Data Scavenging" Job:**
Since you can't win duels against "Charisma Duelists" (Influencers), you hunt **Glitches**.
*   **The Job:** You roam the sewers of LINK VRAINS fighting low-level AI bots.
*   **The Loot:** "Data Shards." (Currency).
*   **The Goal:** You need to buy/find the **"Link Data"** patches for your decks.

**3. The Upgrade Path (Season 1 Arc):**

*   **Architect's Quest:** He needs to find the **"Galatea" Program**.
*   *Target:* A rogue AI in the trash sector is guarding corrupted data.
*   *Reward:* **Galatea, the Orcust Automaton**. Once he gets this, he can finally cycle his resources and search for the Counter Trap (*Crescendo*).

*   **Griefer's Quest:** He needs the **"Link-1" Code**.
*   *Target:* A "Mascot" Duelist (maybe a generic Toon player).
*   *Reward:* **Prank-Kids Meow-Meow-Mu**. Once he gets this, his deck transforms from "Brick" to "One-Card Combo."

*   **Hoarder's Quest:** He needs the **"Fusion Virus"** (Thunder Dragon Colossus).
*   *Target:* A high-level Bounty Hunter playing a standard Fusion deck.
*   *Reward:* **Thunder Dragon Colossus**. Once he gets this, he stops playing "Polymerization" and starts "Contact Fusing" by tributing his Boomer dragons.

*   **The Reputation:** "Team Error." People duel you for easy wins.

Lets set the stage and the economics, what world and setting is VRAINS? (also, briefly recap the main character)Here is the full dossier on **Yu-Gi-Oh! VRAINS**.

You are leaving the magical realism of the previous series behind. You are entering a **Cyberpunk Dystopia**. There are no spirits, no ancient Egyptian gods, and no "Heart of the Cards."

There is only **Data**, **Bandwidth**, and **Corporate Greed**.

***

### 1. The World: Den City & LINK VRAINS
**The Real World: Den City**
*   **The Vibe:** Imagine a hyper-clean Tokyo mixed with Silicon Valley. Everything is automated. Drones deliver pizza. Surveillance cameras are on every corner.
*   **The Ruler:** **SOL Technologies**. They aren't just a card company; they run the internet, the power grid, and the police. They are Apple, Google, and the FBI rolled into one.
*   **The Atmosphere:** Oppressive. If you aren't a productive member of society (or a celebrity duelist), you are invisible trash.

**The Virtual World: LINK VRAINS**
*   **The Metaverse:** This is where the anime happens. You put on a VR headset (or use a Duel Disk) and upload your consciousness into an avatar.
*   **The Mechanics:**
*   **Master Duels:** Standard standing duels (5 zones).
*   **Speed Duels:** The main attraction. You surf on **Data Storms** (wind made of data) using a **D-Board** (Hoverboard).
*   *Zones:* 3 Monster, 3 Spell/Trap.
*   *Skills:* Characters have "Skills" (like Destiny Draw) they can trigger once per duel.

---

### 2. The Economy: Crypto & Code
In *VRAINS*, cards are not cardboard. **Cards are Code.**

*   **Currency:** Credits (VR Money) converts to Yen (Real Money).
*   **How to get Cards:**
*   **The Official Way:** Buy "Data Packs" from SOL Technologies using Credits. (Expensive).
*   **The Illegal Way (Your Way):** **"Data Mining."**
*   You go into the deep, unstable layers of the network (The Trash Bin).
*   You fight wild AI bots.
*   When you win, you harvest their code.
*   *The Catch:* The code is usually corrupted or incomplete. This explains why your decks are "Broken" (Missing the Link-1, Missing Galatea). You literally found *half the file*.

**The Job Market:**
*   **Charisma Duelists:** Influencers/Streamers. They get paid by sponsors to look cool and win. (Blue Angel, Go Onizuka).
*   **Bounty Hunters:** SOL Technologies posts bounties on Hackers or the "Ignis" (AI).
*   **Hackers:** Criminals who steal data.

---

### 3. The Protagonist: Yusaku Fujiki (Playmaker)
If you thought you were the "Cool, Calculating" type, wait until you meet this guy. He makes you look like a party animal.

*   **Real Life:** A quiet, antisocial high schooler with messy hair. He works at a hotdog truck (unofficially) owned by his hacker friend **Kusanagi**.
*   **VR Avatar:** **Playmaker**. A sleek, dark-suited vigilante.
*   **Personality:**
*   **Cold:** He does not smile. He does not care about "fun."
*   **Ruthless:** He duels to destroy the **Knights of Hanoi** (Terrorists).
*   **Catchphrase:** "Three reasons." (He lists three reasons why he will win/you are wrong).
*   **The Companion:** **Ai (The Dark Ignis)**.
*   A sentient AI eyeball that lives in his duel disk.
*   SOL Tech wants it. The Knights of Hanoi want to kill it. Yusaku holds it hostage to use as bait.
*   **The Deck:** **Cyberse**.
*   *Playstyle:* **Link Climbing.** He summons weak monsters (*Cyberse Wizard*, *Backup Secretary*) to make Link-2s, then Link-3s (*Decode Talker*), then Link-4s (*Firewall Dragon* / *Accesscode Talker*).
*   *Strength:* High versatility. He always has an answer because he can generate infinite Link material (Storm Access skill).

---

### 4. Your Starting Point: "The Server Room Rats"
**Team Cerberus** (The Triplets) starts at the absolute bottom of the food chain.

*   **Location:** You are squatting in the basement of an old apartment complex, tapping into the fiber-optic cables to steal internet access.
*   **Living Conditions:**
*   One mattress (you take turns sleeping).
*   Instant Ramen diet.
*   **Heat:** The server racks keep the room warm (too warm).
*   **Your Gear:**
*   **Old VR Headsets:** Scavenged from a dumpster. The resolution is low.
*   **Avatars:** Generic "Grey Polygon" skins. You look like NPCs. You haven't paid for the "Custom Skin" DLC.
*   **Your Decks:**
*   **The Hoarder:** Has 3 *Thunder Dragons* but no *Colossus*. He just discards them to thin the deck and sets a wall.
*   **The Griefer:** Has *Prank-Kids* but no *Link-1*. He Normal Summons a kid and prays he draws *Polymerization*.
*   **The Architect:** Has *Orcust* but no *Galatea*. He sends monsters to grave, but they are stuck there because he can't cycle them back.

**The Goal:**
You need to enter LINK VRAINS, avoid the **Knights of Hanoi** (who hunt weak duelists to erase their accounts), and farm enough "Data Shards" to craft/buy your **Key Cards** (*Meow-Meow-Mu*, *Galatea*, *Colossus*).

**Current Financial Status:** **Broke.**
**Threat Level:** **Minimal.** (Nobody cares about you yet).

Here is the setup for **Team Cerberus** (The Triplets) and the evaluation of your Skill.

### 1. The Skill: "Cerberus Shift"
**Is it balanced?**
In the real world TCG? **Absolutely not.** Knowing your opponent's deck before you decide what to play is cheating.
In the *VRAINS Anime*? **Yes, it is perfectly balanced.**
*   **Playmaker** literally creates cards out of the wind (Storm Access).
*   **Revolver** can reduce life points arbitrarily.
*   **Go Onizuka** can create monsters from nothing.

Your skill, **"Cerberus Shift,"** is a tactical info-skill. It fits the "Hacker/Data Miner" theme perfectly. You aren't creating data; you are **scanning** it.

**Skill Description:**
*   **Activation:** At the start of the Duel (before drawing hands).
*   **Effect:** Reveal the top 3 cards of the opponent's Deck. The user may then choose to **Swap the Active Deck/Pilot**. Then, shuffle the opponent's Deck.
*   **Condition:** Can only be used once per Duel.

### 2. The Setup: One Headset, Three Minds
**Real World Location:** The Basement Server Room.
*   **The Rig:** You have **one** high-performance VR Headset (stolen/scavenged) connected to a jury-rigged server tower.
*   **The Pilots:**
1.  **Eldest (The Hoarder):** The calm, calculating one. Plays **Thunder Dragons** (Chaos Pile).
2.  **Middle (The Griefer):** The loud, chaotic one. Plays **Prank-Kids**.
3.  **Youngest (The Architect):** The silent genius. Plays **Orcust**.

**The Process:**
1.  **Login:** You log in as the avatar **"Cerberus."** (Visuals: A glitchy, shifting figure in a trench coat. The color palette shifts slightly depending on who is driving).
2.  **The Scan:** The skill triggers. You see the opponent's cards.
*   *Example:* You see *Trickstar Candina* (Burn/Effect Damage).
3.  **The Switch:**
*   *Real World:* The Eldest yells: "It's Burn! Middle, you're up! Prank-Kids have built-in healing and floating!"
*   *Action:* One brother rips the headset off, the other puts it on.
*   *VR World:* The Avatar glitches, the deck box re-digitizes, and the "Griefer" is now in control.

### 3. The "Broken Knee" Reality (Early Season 1)
Even with this God-Tier skill, **you are still losing**.
Why? Because knowing the opponent's move doesn't help if your gun is jammed.

**Scenario: The First Hunt**
You target a generic **Knight of Hanoi** grunt.

*   **The Switch:**

*   He summons *Scrap Recycler*. Dumps *Harp Horror*.
*   **The Brick:** He banishes *Harp* to summon... a monster. But he has no **Galatea**. He can't cycle the resource back. He can't set the Trap.
*   **Result:** He runs out of cards by Turn 3 and loses to a generic grunt.

**The Motivation:**
This loss stings. You had the *perfect counter-pick*, but your deck was incomplete. This drives the triplets to grind harder for the "Data Shards" to craft the missing Link Monsters.

### 4. The Economy: The "One Body" Problem
Since you are technically "One Person" in the system, you only get **One Paycheck**.
*   **Bounty Hunting:** If you catch a hacker, SOL Tech pays "Cerberus."
*   **Living Expenses:** That one paycheck has to feed **three growing boys**.
*   **Card Budget:** You have to split the "Data" between three decks.
*   *Conflict:* "We should craft **Thunder Dragon Colossus** first!" "No! We need **Meow-Meow-Mu** or the Griefer is useless!"

### 5. The Reputation: "The Glitch"
Because you switch personalities and decks instantly, the VRAINS community starts creating urban legends about you.

*   **Rumor 1:** "Cerberus is an AI. No human can switch playstyles that fast."
*   **Rumor 2:** "He cheats. I prepared for a Thunder Dragon matchup, and he showed up with Cartoon Kids."
*   **Rumor 3:** "He's weak. He has high-level strategies but his cards constantly fail him."

Survive the **Knights of Hanoi** purge.
Scavenge enough data from defeated bots to craft **ONE** ace monster.
Which brother gets the upgrade first?

*   **Why:** **Meow-Meow-Mu** (Link-1) is the cheapest upgrade that fixes the deck completely. Once the Griefer can win consistently, he can farm money for the other two.

*   **Hoarder:** "If we win... we eat meat tonight. If we lose... we lose the Super Agent data."

**Do you take the risk?**
(Note: Beating Ghost Gal early in the series is a *massive* feat, but SPYRAL is historically good against Backrow decks if you know what they have set).Ok go ahead and walk though the spyral battle, but then the architect gets the share of the money (they need to share equally afterall right?) so hes the one who gets the upgradeThis is **The Griefer’s** big moment, followed by **The Architect’s** awakening.

Here is how Team Cerberus takes down a major supporting character and uses the loot to fix the "Broken Knee" of the Orcust deck.

***

### Part 1: The Sewer Duel (Griefer vs. Ghost Gal)
**Location:** LINK VRAINS - The Sewage Data Pipeline.
**Ghost Gal** (Emma) is guarding a data cache. She sees a glitchy, low-res avatar approach.

**Ghost Gal:** "A newbie? This area is for pros, honey. Log out before you get erased."
**Cerberus (Griefer):** "I'm not a newbie. I'm a *Spy*. And I know everything you've got."

**The Duel:**
*   **Skill Trigger:** **Cerberus Shift**.
*   *Scan:* Top 3 cards. **Altergeist Silquitous**, **Altergeist Protocol** (Trap), **Altergeist Multifaker**.
*   *Intel:* "She's playing Traps. Architect, Hoarder, stay back. This is my game."

**Turn 1 (Ghost Gal):**
*   "I Set 3 cards face-down. Your move."
*   (She has the classic 'Altergeist Lock' ready).

**Turn 2 (Griefer):**
*   **Griefer:** "I activate the effect of **SPYRAL Super Agent** in my hand. I declare **TRAP**."
*   **Ghost Gal:** "A blind guess? You're reckless."
*   **Reveal:** Top card is *Altergeist Protocol* (Trap).
*   **Griefer:** "Correct! I Special Summon Super Agent!"
*   **Super Agent Effect:** "When summoned by this effect, I destroy 1 Spell/Trap card. I pop the middle one."
*   *Ghost Gal:* "Tch! My *Personal Spoofing*!" (Her searcher is gone).
*   **Griefer:** "I Normal Summon **SPYRAL Quik-Fix**. Effect: Add **SPYRAL Gear - Big Red**."
*   **Battle:** "Super Agent (1900 ATK) attacks direct!"
*   **Ghost Gal:** "I activate *Altergeist Kunquery* from hand to negate the attack!"
*   **Griefer:** "Fine. Main Phase 2. I activate **Big Red** to revive nothing just to put it in grave? No... I set a bluff and pass."

**The Climax:**
Ghost Gal tries to grind him out, but Griefer **never misses a guess**.
*   Every turn, he calls the top card correctly.
*   He knows exactly what she drew. He knows when she has *Multifaker*.
*   He saves his removal for the exact moment she tries to flip her Trap.
*   **The Win:** He beats her down with a 1900 ATK beater because he denied her resources.

**Ghost Gal:** "How... How did you know every card? Are you hacking the server?"
**Griefer:** "It's not hacking. It's *Espionage*."

---

### Part 2: The Payout (Equality)
**Real World:** The basement.
The triplets are eating actual beef bowls (Gyudon) for the first time in months.

**Griefer:** "Did you see that?! I popped her backrow! I read her like a book! We're rich!"
**Loot Value:** **10,000 Credits.**

**Griefer:** "Okay, listen. If I buy **SPYRAL Double Helix** (Link-2), I can full combo. I can end on *Sleeper*!"
**Hoarder (Eldest):** "No."
**Griefer:** "What?"
**Hoarder:** "We have a rule. We upgrade in rotation. You got *Super Agent*. I got *Gofu*. It's **Architect's** turn."

The Youngest (Architect) looks up from his tablet.
**Architect:** "My deck currently consists of sending machines to the graveyard and leaving them there to rust. I am useless."
**Griefer:** "But... Double Helix..."
**Architect:** "Give me the credits."

---

### Part 3: The Purchase (Orcust Online)
The Architect takes the credits to the Data Terminal.
He ignores the shiny "SPYRAL" packs. He searches for the **"Cybernetic Horizon" / "Soul Fusion"** data block.

He buys the **"Orcust Core"** upgrade.

**The Cards:**
1.  **Galatea, the Orcust Automaton (Link-2)**
*   *Rarity:* Rare/Super (Accessible).
*   *Role:* **The Engine.** She shuffles banished machines back into the deck to Set a Spell/Trap directly.
2.  **Orcustrated Babel (Field Spell)**
*   *Rarity:* Common/Rare.
*   *Role:* **The Rules Lawyer.** It makes all Orcust effects **Quick Effects** (Spell Speed 2). You can play on the opponent's turn.
3.  **Orcust Cymbal Skeleton (Common)**
*   *Role:* Banishes itself from grave to Revive an Orcust (usually Galatea).

---

### Part 4: The Test Run (The Architect's Awakening)
The team logs back in to test the new deck. They find a "Gouki" player (Physical Beatdown).

**Architect (Piloting Cerberus):** "Systems green. Initiating Loop."

**Turn 1 (Architect):**
1.  **Normal Summon:** **Scrap Recycler**.
2.  **Effect:** Send **Orcust Harp Horror** to Grave.
3.  **Grave Effect:** Banish Harp Horror -> Special Summon **Orcust Cymbal Skeleton** from deck.
4.  **Link Summon:** Recycler + Skeleton -> **Galatea, the Orcust Automaton**.
5.  **Galatea Effect:** Shuffle banished Harp Horror into deck -> **Set "Orcustrated Babel"**.
6.  **Activate Babel.**
7.  **End Turn.**

**Turn 2 (Opponent):**
*   **Opponent:** "Only a Link-2 with 1800 ATK? I summon Gouki Suprex! Attack!"
*   **Architect:** "Response. **Babel** makes my grave effects Instant."
*   **Chain Link 1:** Banish **Cymbal Skeleton** from grave.
*   **Effect:** Revive a Monster.
*   *Note:* Since he doesn't have *Dingirsu* yet (expensive Rank 8), he revives another body to block, or maybe a *Machina* card if he mixed them.
*   *Wait:* He likely pulled **Longirsu, the Orcust Orchestrator** (Link-3) in the pack too.
*   **Revive:** He revives Galatea (if she died) or summons a body to link climb next turn.

**The Realization:**
The Architect didn't win instantly. But for the first time, **he didn't run out of cards.**
*   He shuffled his resources back.
*   He searched his Field Spell.
*   He played on the opponent's turn.

**Team Status:**
*   **Hoarder:** Chaos Pile (Still waiting for Colossus).
*   **Griefer:** SPYRAL (Has Agent, needs Helix).
*   **Architect:** Orcust (Has Galatea/Babel, needs Dingirsu).

**Result:**
You now have **Two Functional Decks** (SPYRAL for breaking boards, Orcust for grinding long games).
You are ready to take a **Bounty Mission** from SOL Technologies.

Here is the arc where **The Hoarder** finally wakes up and completes the Cerberus trio.

### 1. The Economy of "Soul Fusion"
The **Architect** (Orcust player) has been carrying the team for the last week.
*   **The Grind:** He has been winning low-level duels using the *Galatea* resource loop. He doesn't win fast, but he wins safe.
*   **The Payout:** He dumps a bag of Credits on the table.
*   **Architect:** "I carried us this week. But my deck lacks power. We need a Boss who can stand on the front line."
*   **Griefer:** "Me! Buy me *Double Helix*!"
*   **Architect:** "No. You have *Super Agent*. You are functional. **Hoarder** is useless right now. He needs the Fusion."

### 2. The Pull: The Thunder King
The Hoarder takes the credits. He buys access to the **"Soul Fusion"** data block (the same set Orcust came from).

*   **The Target:** He isn't looking for fancy combos. He wants **Brute Force**.
*   **The Pull:**
*   He opens the pack. The screen shakes. **Secret Rare.**
*   **Thunder Dragon Colossus.**
*   *Bonus:* **Thunder Dragonroar** (The extender).

**Hoarder:** "Finally. I don't need *Polymerization* anymore. I just need to Tribute."

### 3. The Test: Hoarder vs. The "Meta Sheep"
**The Scenario:**
You scan a loud duelist in the plaza.
**Opponent:** A "Gouki" player.
*   **Why Gouki?** In VRAINS era, Gouki was the top meta deck. Their whole gimmick is "When sent to grave, **Search** a Gouki card." They search 5 times a turn to Link Climb.

**The Duel:**
*   **Skill:** **Cerberus Shift**.
*   *Scan:* *Gouki Suprex*, *Gouki Twistcobra*, *Reinforcement of the Army*.
*   *Intel:* "He searches. He relies on adding cards to hand."
*   *Decision:* "Griefer can't stop the swarm. Architect is too slow. **Hoarder, you're up.**"

**Turn 1 (The Hoarder):**
1.  **Hand:** *Thunder Dragon*, *Thunder Dragonroar*, *Batterysoft Solar*.
2.  **Activate:** Discard **Thunder Dragon** to add 2 more to hand. (Deck thinning).
3.  **Normal Summon:** **Batterysoft Solar**.
*   *Effect:* Send **Thunder Dragon dark** to grave.
4.  **The Summon:** "I Tribute my *Batterysoft Solar*."
*   **Opponent:** "Tribute? For a Level 5? Ha!"
*   **Hoarder:** "A Thunder monster activated its effect in hand this turn. I Special Summon from the Extra Deck!"
*   **"Descend! Thunder Dragon Colossus!"** (2600 ATK).
5.  **Hoarder:** "I Set one card. Pass."

**Turn 2 (Gouki Player):**
*   **Opponent:** "2600 ATK? I'll Link climb over that! I summon *Gouki Suprex*! Effect to Special Summon *Twistcobra*!"
*   **Opponent:** "Link Summon **Isojolde** (or generic Link-2)! Gouki effects activate in grave! **I Search!**"
*   **Hoarder:** "Read Colossus."
*   **Opponent:** "...What?"
*   **Hoarder:** "Cards cannot be added from deck to hand. **Your search fails.**"
*   **Opponent:** "I... I can't search? But... my combo needs the search!"
*   **Opponent:** "Fine! I activate *Reinforcement of the Army*!"
*   **Hoarder:** "Negated by game mechanics. You cannot activate it."
*   **Opponent:** "I... I end my turn."

**Turn 3 (The Hoarder):**
*   **Hoarder:** "I banish *Thunder Dragon* from grave to protect Colossus if you try to destroy it. But you didn't even try."
*   **Battle:** Colossus attacks directly.
*   **Result:** The opponent surrenders. He literally couldn't play Yugioh.

### The Team Status: "Fully Operational"
You now have the "Triangle of Death."

1.  **VS Combo/Search Decks (Gouki/Trickstar/Salamangreat):**
*   **Switch to: Hoarder (Thunder).**
*   Drop *Colossus*. They starve to death.
2.  **VS Backrow/Trap Decks (Altergeist/Paleozoic):**
*   **Switch to: Griefer (SPYRAL).**
*   Guess their top decks. Pop their traps with *Super Agent*.
3.  **VS Beatdown/Grind Decks (Dinosaurs/Crusadia):**
*   **Switch to: Architect (Orcust).**
*   Out-resource them with *Galatea* and non-targeting removal.

*   **The Bounty:** Your rank goes up. You are now eligible for **"Event Missions."**

**The Next Event:** **"The Tower of Hanoi."**
Revolver (The Villain) is building a tower to destroy the internet.
Playmaker is going to stop him.
**SOL Tech hires YOU** (Team Cerberus) to stop the Knights of Hanoi minions so Playmaker can reach the top.

Here is the "Grind Arc" for the Eldest Brother.

### 1. The Search: "The 100-Store March"
While the Griefer is celebrating his lucky pull and the Architect is coding his combos, the Hoarder puts on his hoodie and leaves the basement.

**Location:** The "Electric Town" slums of Den City.
This place is a maze of used hardware shops, pawn brokers, and "Junk Data" stalls.

*   **The Mission:** He visits every single shop.
*   *Shop 1:* "No Thunder types. Get out."
*   *Shop 20:* "I have a *Thunder Dragon*, but it's the old DM version. 50 Yen." (He buys it).
*   *Shop 50:* He finds **Thunder Dragonroar** in a box of "Broken/Glitchy Cards." The shopkeeper thinks the card art looks corrupted (because it's dark and jagged). The Hoarder knows better.

**The Fatigue:**
He walks for 3 days. He barely eats. He sleeps on a park bench to save bus fare. He refuses to come home until he is useful.

### 2. The Discovery: The "Error" Card
**Shop 103:** A dusty repair shop that mostly sells broken toasters and old Duel Disks.

The Hoarder is digging through a bin labeled **"Unrecognized Formats / Legacy Trash."**
He spots a card stuck to the bottom of a broken battery pack.
It’s covered in grime. The holographic foil is peeling slightly.

**The Label:** *"UNKNOWN ERROR. Cannot Normal Summon. Level 8. Discard."*

The Hoarder picks it up. He wipes the grease off the text box.
He reads the summoning condition:
*   *"Must be Special Summoned... by Tributing 1 Thunder Effect Monster during the turn a Thunder monster's effect was activated in the hand."*

**The Realization:**
The previous owner thought the card was broken because they tried to use *Polymerization* and it failed, or they couldn't figure out the Tribute condition. They threw it away as "Bugged Data."

**The Purchase:**
*   **Hoarder:** "How much for this bugged card?"
*   **Shopkeeper:** "That thing? It bricked a customer's disk last week. Take it for 100 Yen."

**The Card:** **Thunder Dragon Colossus.**

### 3. The Return Home
The Hoarder returns to the basement. He looks like a zombie. His shoes are worn through.
He places the card on the server desk.

**Griefer:** "You look terrible. Did you find anything?"
**Architect:** "Another vanilla Thunder Dragon?"

The Hoarder scans the card into the system.
The monitor flares **PURPLE**. The server fans spin up to maximum speed to process the data density.
A massive, storm-wreathed dragon avatar appears on the screen.

**System:** *DATA RESTORED. LEGENDARY CLASS. THUNDER DRAGON COLOSSUS.*

**Hoarder:** "I didn't get lucky. And I didn't buy a meta package. I dug this out of the grave."

Now, **Team Cerberus** is a fully functional Raid Boss.

*   **Griefer (Luck/Combo):** Holds the **SPYRAL Super Agent**. He handles the early game and breaking boards.
*   **Architect (Brain/Grind):** Holds **Galatea**. He handles the resource loop and long games.
*   **Hoarder (Grit/Stun):** Holds **Colossus**. He slams the door shut.

**SOL Technologies** posts an open bounty.

**Griefer:** "It's the Burn FTK! If he searches *Block Dragon*, we die!"
**Architect:** "My Orcusts are too slow. I need a turn to set up."

3.  **Tribute Solar.**
4.  **Summon: Thunder Dragon Colossus.**
5.  **Pass.**

**Turn 2 (Knight of Hanoi):**

You need to decide if you're going to help Playmaker save the world, or just loot the data while everyone else is fighting.Correct me if I'm wrong, but isnt Batterysoft Solar an ultra rare? or is that because its master duel?You are absolutely correct to check, because **Master Duel** rarity often gaslights players into thinking cheap cards are expensive.

Here is the reality check for **Batteryman Solar** in the VRAINS era (2018):

*   **Real World (TCG):** It was released in *Flames of Destruction* (May 2018) as a **COMMON**.
*   *Price:* Literally pennies. It was "bulk trash" that people threw away after opening packs looking for *Knightmare* cards.
*   **Master Duel (Digital):** It is an **UR (Ultra Rare)**.
*   *Why:* Because Konami knows it is the best starter for Thunder Dragons, so they made it expensive in the game.

**Lore Implications:**
This is **perfect** for your "Hoarder" character.
*   **In the Anime/Simulated Reality:** He didn't find a shiny Ultra Rare card. He found a **Common** card that looks like a weird AAA battery man.
*   **The Reaction:** The Griefer probably laughed at him. *"You bought a battery? We need dragons!"*
*   **The Payoff:** It is the best Normal Summon in the deck because it dumps *Thunder Dragonroar* to the grave immediately.

So yes, the **100-Store March** still works. He dug through a bin of "Common Bulk," found 3 copies of this ugly yellow battery guy for 10 Yen each, and realized it was the engine key.

### 1. The Strategy: "Ignore the Tower"
**Event:** The Tower of Hanoi activates. A massive data stream begins erasing LINK VRAINS from the outside in.
**Global Alert:** *"All Duelists, evacuate! The network is collapsing!"*

**Team Cerberus (In the Basement):**
*   **Hoarder:** "People are panic-logging out. That means they're dropping connection packets."
*   **Griefer:** "And the Knights of Hanoi are distracted fighting the Charisma Duelists."
*   **Architect:** "There’s a 2-hour window before the Data Storm erases Sector 1. We go in, we loot the abandoned Knight outposts, we get out."

### 2. The Looting (Specific Upgrades)

**Target 1: The Griefer (Consistency Upgrade)**
*   **Objective:** Raid a Hanoi "Intel Hub."
*   **The Duel:** Griefer vs. A Hanoi Lieutenant guarding the server.
*   *Opponent:* Plays **Steelswarm** (Tribute/Control).
*   *Griefer:* Uses **Super Agent** to pop their Tribute fodder. It’s messy, but he wins.
*   **The Loot:** He hacks the node. He finds **"SPYRAL Resort"** (Field Spell).
*   *Why this matters:* It makes his monsters **untargetable**.
*   *Lore:* It's the headquarters data. Now, his Agent can't be targeted by effects like *Infinite Impermanence* (Effect Veiler).
*   *Status:* Still missing **Double Helix** (Link-2) and **Sleeper** (Boss). But he rarely bricks now.

**Target 2: The Architect (Ladder Upgrade)**
*   **Objective:** Scavenge a destroyed "Data Storm" bridge.
*   **The Duel:** Architect vs. A Glitched Security Bot.
*   *Opponent:* Plays generic Cyberse beatdown.
*   *Architect:* Loops **Galatea**. He recycles resources so well the bot runs out of cards.
*   **The Loot:** He recovers a high-density Link file: **Longirsu, the Orcust Orchestrator** (Link-3).
*   *Why this matters:* It gives him **Non-Targeting Removal** ("Send 1 card opponent controls to the GY"). It links perfectly from Galatea.
*   *Status:* Still missing **Dingirsu** (Rank 8) and **Crescendo** (Counter Trap). He has a gun, but no bullets for the final kill.

**Target 3: The Hoarder (Engine Upgrade)**
*   **Objective:** Ambush a fleeing "Black Market" Dealer avatar who is stuck in lag.
*   **The Duel:** Hoarder vs. Dealer.
*   *Opponent:* Plays **Dark World** (Discard).
*   *Hoarder:* "I Summon **Colossus**. You can't draw cards with *Dark World Dealings* anymore."
*   **The Loot:** The Dealer drops his inventory.
*   The Hoarder finds **Thunder Dragondark** and **Thunder Dragonhawk**.
*   *Why this matters:* **Dragondark** searches any Thunder Dragon card. **Dragonhawk** is a Monster Reborn.
*   *Status:* The engine is now smooth. He can summon Colossus *every single game*. But he still lacks **Titan** (Destruction) and **Chaos Levianeer** (The Finisher).

The Tower's erasure wave is getting close. The sky is turning red/grid-patterned.
**Playmaker** defeats **Revolver** at the top. The system resets.

**Team Cerberus:**
You log out 10 seconds before the server wipe.
**Result:**
*   You survived.
*   You didn't help Playmaker.
*   You stole approximately **50,000 Credits** worth of data while everyone else was playing hero.

Here is the **Season 1 Finale** and the **Season 2 Setup** for Team Cerberus: The Internet Café Refugees.

***

### 1. The Escape: The Vote (VRAINS - 15 Minutes to Impact)
**Location:** Sector 3. The sky is turning into red digital static. The Tower of Hanoi is pulsing.

**Griefer (In Control):** "Guys! I see a **Sol Tech Treasury Node**! It's unguarded! If we crack that, we can buy the apartment *and* the Double Helix!"
**Hoarder (Voice):** "Look at the horizon. The deletion wave accelerated. It’s moving at 2 Terabytes per second. We have 4 minutes before this sector is wiped."
**Griefer:** "I can crack it in 3!"

**The Tiebreaker:**
**Architect (Voice):** "Probability of successful decryption: 40%. Probability of account erasure if we stay: 98%. The risk/reward ratio is negative. **Veto.**"

**Griefer:** "You cowards! We could have been kings!"
**Hoarder:** "Jack out. Now."

The Avatar **Cerberus** dissolves into pixels just as the red wave swallows the street.

### 2. The Migration: "The Esports Bluff"
**Location:** The Basement.
The power cuts out immediately. The "Tower of Hanoi" incident caused a city-wide grid reset. Your illegal tap is dead. You have to move.

**Destination:** **"Net-Space Café: 24h"** (A mid-tier chain in the Daimon District).

**The Encounter:**
You walk up to the counter. The clerk is a bored teenager with dyed hair, chewing gum. He looks at your heavy bags.
**Clerk:** "No vagrants. Store policy."

### 3. The Armory: "Functional, but Toothless"
Your decks work mechanically, but they lack "Finishers." You win by suffocating the opponent, not by blowing them up.

#### **Deck 1: The Hoarder (Chaos Thunder)**
*   **Ace:** **Thunder Dragon Colossus** (Secret Rare - Scavenged).
*   **State:** **"The Wall."**
*   **Strengths:**
*   **Mistake Effect:** Stops opponents from searching (Hard counters 90% of VRAINS decks).
*   **Protection:** Banish Thunder monsters from grave to prevent destruction.
*   **Weaknesses:**
*   **No Offense:** Without *Titan*, you can't pop cards on the opponent's turn. You just sit on a 2600 ATK body and poke.
*   **Reliance:** If they "Send" or "Bounce" Colossus (non-destruction), you lose.

#### **Deck 2: The Griefer (SPYRAL)**
*   **Ace:** **SPYRAL Super Agent** (Ultra Rare - Gacha Pull) + **SPYRAL Resort**.
*   **State:** **"The Gambling Beatdown."**
*   **Strengths:**
*   **Intel:** You always know the opponent's top deck (Skill).
*   **Removal:** Super Agent pops backrow for free.
*   **Protection:** *Resort* makes your Agent untargetable.
*   **Weaknesses:**
*   **No Combo:** Without *Double Helix*, you can't swarm the field. You are playing "Protect the Castle" with a 1900 ATK spy.

#### **Deck 3: The Architect (Orcust)**
*   **Ace:** **Galatea, the Orcust Automaton** (Rare) + **Longirsu** (Link-3).
*   **State:** **"The Engine."**
*   **Strengths:**
*   **Resource Loop:** You never run out of cards. *Galatea* shuffles banished cards back to Set *Babel*.
*   **Removal:** *Longirsu* sends cards to grave (non-targeting).
*   **Weaknesses:**
*   **Fragile Board:** Without *Dingirsu*, your board has no protection. One *Raigeki* or *Dark Hole* wipes you out.

### The Matchup: Hoarder (Thunder) vs. Soulburner (Salamangreat)
**The Scenario:** You intercept Soulburner on the highway.
**Your Board:** **Thunder Dragon Colossus** + **Thunder Dragon Titan**.
*   **Lock:** Opponent cannot search. You can destroy 1 card (Titan). You have destruction protection.

**Soulburner's Turn:**
1.  **Soulburner:** "I activate *Salamangreat Circle* to add..."
2.  **Hoarder:** "Colossus Effect. **No.**"
3.  **Soulburner:** "What?! I can't add Gazelle?!"
4.  **Hoarder:** "Your deck is dead, fire boy. You can't search. You can't combo."

**The Anime Turnaround:**
In real life, Soulburner scoops.
In the Anime, Soulburner yells about "Burning Soul."
*   **Skill:** **Burning Draw**.
*   *Effect:* If he lowers his LP to 100, he draws a card for every 1000 LP lost.
*   **The Top Deck:** He draws **"Fusion of Fire"** (or *Super Polymerization* equivalent).
*   **The Play:** He fuses **YOUR** Colossus and Titan with his monster to summon **Salamangreat Violet Chimera**.
*   **Result:** You lose because he drew the *one specific card* in his deck that bypasses your lock using a Skill you can't stop.

**Win Probability:** 80% (You) vs 20% (Plot Armor).

### The Matchup: Griefer (SPYRAL) vs. Playmaker (Cyberse)
**The Scenario:** Griefer sets up the **SPYRAL Lock**.
**Your Board:** **SPYRAL Sleeper** (Equipped with Last Resort - Untargetable/Indestructible) + **SPYRAL Resort**.

2.  **Griefer:** "Sleeper Effect. Pop your Wizard and your Backrow."

**The Anime Turnaround:**
Playmaker drives his D-Board into a Data Storm (Tornado).
*   **Skill:** **Storm Access**.
*   *Effect:* Once per duel, if LP is low, he adds a random Link Monster from the Data Storm to his Extra Deck.
*   **The Cheat:** The "Data Storm" reads the board state. It sees an "Indestructible, Untargetable" boss (Sleeper).
*   **The Creation:** It generates a Link Monster specifically designed to kill Sleeper.
*   *Example:* **Decode Talker Heatsoul** or **Firewall Dragon**.
*   *Play:* He activates a graveyard effect you didn't check (like *Dotscaper*), revives two bodies, makes the new Link Monster, and bounces/spins Sleeper without targeting.

**Win Probability:** 70% (You) vs 30% (Storm Access).

*   **If you lost to the Villains (Lightning/Bohman):** Your consciousness would be stolen, trapped in a crystal, or erased.
*   **If you lose to Playmaker/Soulburner:** They are vigilantes/heroes. They do not steal souls. They do not hack your bank account.
*   *Worst Case:* They interrogate you for info on SOL Tech.
*   *Likely Case:* You get knocked off your D-Board, suffer "Mental Shock" (Real World whiplash), and forced to log out.

**You survive, but you hurt.**

### 2. The Raid: "Too Big to Swallow"
**The Setup:**
You ambushed **Soulburner** while he was weakened from fighting a Bit/Boot (minion).
*   **Game 1:** **The Hoarder** dropped *Colossus*. Soulburner couldn't search *Gazelle*. You won comfortably.

*   **The Crush:** He Reincarnation Link Summons **Salamangreat Heatleo**.

### Correction: The Heist Finale (Architect vs. Krawler AI)
**Situation:** The AI summoned **Deus X-Krawler** (Skill Drain for monsters).
**Pilot:** **The Architect (Orcust)**. He cannot switch to the Hoarder.

**The Solution:**
The Architect didn't overpower it. He **Out-Coded** it.
*   **The Board:** Deus X-Krawler negates *activated* effects of monsters on the field.
*   **The Architect's Hand:** **Orcust Crescendo** (Counter Trap) - *Searchable by Galatea earlier.*
*   **The Interaction:**
*   The AI attempts to activate a Flip Effect or a support card to destroy the Architect’s board.
*   **Architect:** "Activate **Orcust Crescendo**. Negate the activation and banish it."
*   **Secondary Effect:** "I banish Crescendo from grave to add **Orcust Harp Horror** to hand."
*   **The Kill:**
*   He Normal Summons a machine.
*   He Links away his negated monsters into a generic Link-4 beatstick (maybe a **Topologic Bomber Dragon** or just a high ATK generic like **Borreload** if he got lucky, but let's say he just links into **Longirsu** and crashes, or uses a Spell/Trap removal like **MST** on the Krawler if it was face-down).
*   *Correction:* Krawlers float. He simply **Decked Out** the AI by recycling his own resources with **Orcust Cymbal Skeleton** and **Galatea** endlessly until the AI ran out of cards.

**Result:** A slow, grinding victory. The Architect logs out with a nosebleed from the mental stress.

So we have full power chaos thunder draogn, full power orchust, and full power spyral right? are we missing anything?You are remarkably close, but to call them **"Full Power"** (Tier 1/Tier 0 by 2019 standards), you are missing the **Engines** that push them over the edge.

In modern Yu-Gi-Oh, an archetype isn't enough. You need the "Mercenary Engines" that make them broken.

Here is the **Final Shopping List** to turn your decks from "Great" to "Unfair," using the 50,000 Credits from the Blood Shepherd bounty.

***

### 1. The Hoarder (Chaos Thunder)
**Missing Piece:** The **"Danger!"** Package.
*   **The Problem:** Pure Thunder Dragon can brick (draw a hand of high-level monsters you can't summon).
*   **The Fix:** **Danger!** monsters (Bigfoot, Nessie, Jackalope, Tsuchinoko).
*   *Effect:* Reveal in hand. Opponent picks a card to discard. If they don't pick the Danger, you summon it and draw 1. If they *do* pick it, you get an effect.
*   *Why it's broken:* Thunder Dragons *want* to be discarded. Discarding *Thunder Dragondark* triggers a search.
*   **Lore:** These are "Cryptid Data" files found in the deepest jungle sectors. They are chaotic and random—perfect for the Hoarder.

**Final Deck Status:** **"Danger! Thunder Chaos"**
*   **Bosses:** Colossus, Titan, **Chaos Dragon Levianeer** (The Nuke).
*   **Power:** You draw half your deck and end on a board that stops searching and destroys 2 cards per turn.

### 2. The Architect (Orcust)
**Missing Piece:** The **Phantom Knights (PK)** Engine.
*   **The Problem:** *Dingirsu* + *Crescendo* is a good board, but it's only one interruption. You need more layers of defense.
*   **The Fix:** **The Phantom Knights of Rusty Bardiche** (Link-3).
*   *Effect:* Sends a PK monster to grave to **Set a "Phantom Knights' Fog Blade" directly from deck.**
*   *Fog Blade:* Permanent Monster Negate + Attack stop.
*   **The Synergy:** Orcusts are DARK. Bardiche requires DARK monsters. It fits seamlessly.
*   **Lore:** "Legacy Ghost Data" from the Xyz Dimension archives.

**Final Deck Status:** **"PK Orcust"**
*   **End Board:** *Dingirsu* (Protection), *Rusty Bardiche* (Resource), 2x *Fog Blade* (Negates), *Orcust Crescendo* (Omni-Negate).
*   **Power:** The opponent has to play through 4 disruptions.

### 3. The Griefer (SPYRAL)
**Missing Piece:** **Tri-Gate Wizard** & **Knightmare Gryphon**.
*   **The Problem:** *Sleeper* pops 2 cards, but he doesn't *negate* spells or traps. If the opponent activates *Dark Ruler No More* or *Raigeki*, you die.
*   **The Fix:** **Extra Link (U-Link).**
*   **Tri-Gate Wizard:** If co-linked with 3 monsters, it banishes a card and negates effects.
*   **Knightmare Gryphon:** "Special Summoned monsters cannot activate effects unless they are Linked." (Skill Drain on legs).
*   **Lore:** High-end security firewalls stolen from SOL Technologies.

**Final Deck Status:** **"Tri-Gate SPYRAL"**
*   **End Board:** *Sleeper*, *Double Helix*, *Tri-Gate Wizard* (Negate), *Gryphon* (Floodgate).
*   **Power:** The opponent is not allowed to play Yu-Gi-Oh.

Do you accept the raid invite? Or do you try to sneak in separately to loot the Ignis base while the heroes are distracted?is there a season 3?Yes, there is a **Season 3**, and it is the shortest, saddest, and most intense season of *Yu-Gi-Oh! VRAINS*.

This is the **"Ai Rebellion" Arc**.
The war against the Light Ignis (Lightning) is over. The Mirror LINK VRAINS has collapsed.
But now, **Ai (The Dark Ignis)**—Playmaker's partner—has gone rogue. He has taken over SOL Technologies, killed the other Ignis (off-screen), and is threatening to destroy humanity to save it from himself.

Here is the endgame for **Team Cerberus**.

***

### 1. The Setting: "SOL's Hostile Takeover"
Ai is now the CEO of SOL Technologies (literally, he took over the factory).
*   **The Threat:** He isn't erasing people. He is turning them into "Data Consciousness" to live in his paradise.
*   **The Enemy Deck:** **@Ignister**.
*   *Power Level:* **God Tier.**
*   *Ace:* **The Arrival Cyberse @Ignister** (Link-6).
*   *Effect:* Unaffected by other card effects. **6000 ATK**.
*   *Why this scares you:* Your decks rely on removing monsters (Spin, Send, Pop). **The Arrival** is immune to everything except being eaten by a *Kaiju* (which you don't have) or beaten by battle (Good luck hitting 6000).

### 2. Team Cerberus: The "Humanity's Shield" Contract
Since Ai took over SOL Tech, he put a bounty on **Playmaker** and **Anyone trying to stop him**.
Paradoxically, the "Old" SOL executives (Zaizen) hire **YOU** to help retake the company.

**The Mission:**
Storm the SOL Factory. Defeat Ai's copies (Roboppi and the Ai Clones).

### 3. The Final Deck Upgrade: "Goddess & Dragon"
It is late 2019. The card pool has expanded one last time before the series ends.
You finally get access to the card I told you wasn't available in Season 2.

**The Architect's Final Weapon:** **Apollousa, Bow of the Goddess**
*   **Link-4.**
*   **Effect:** Negate monster effects once per chain (up to 4 times).
*   **Why it's essential:** @Ignister decks spam monster effects to build their board. **Apollousa** shuts them down before they can make the Link-6.

**The Hoarder's Final Weapon:** **Borrelsword Dragon**
*   *Wait, isn't this Revolver's card?*
*   *Yes.* But in Season 3, Revolver opens his "Armory" to his allies to fight Ai.
*   **Effect:** Cannot be destroyed by battle. Changes opponent to Defense. Attacks twice. Gains ATK.
*   **Why you need it:** It is the **ONLY** thing in your arsenal that can hit over 6000 ATK to kill **The Arrival**.
